# sbox-ui Agent Learnings

A growing file of things the agent has learned while working on Beastborne UI. Every invocation reads this; every invocation may append to it.

## Rules for writing to this file

1. **Append at the end**, under the correct section (project conventions / user preferences / s&box quirks / style observations / feedback responses). Don't reorganize existing entries.
2. **Every entry MUST be dated** in `YYYY-MM-DD` format.
3. **Every entry MUST cite the source** — file path + line number if from code, task description if from user feedback.
4. **Include the why** — a bare rule is useless later. Future-you needs to know *why* it was learned so you can judge edge cases.
5. **Check for duplicates before writing.** If a similar entry exists, update it instead of adding a new one.
6. **Flag promote-worthy entries** with `[PROMOTE]` tag. These are items important enough that the user should consider moving them to `CLAUDE.md` (agent does NOT write to CLAUDE.md directly).
7. **Mark entries as outdated** with `[OUTDATED]` tag rather than deleting, so there's a record of what changed. Prune `[OUTDATED]` entries older than 3 months on occasion.
8. **Hard cap: ~100 active entries.** If the file is bloating, compress or remove low-value entries.

## Rules for reading this file

- Treat entries as observations with context, not laws.
- An entry dated 6 months ago about a file that no longer exists is worth less than an entry from last week.
- When acting on an entry, verify it's still current (file still exists, rule still applies).
- If an entry contradicts current code reality, flag it and propose updating or removing the entry.

---

## Project conventions

_(Patterns specific to how Beastborne is structured — naming, file locations, state management conventions)_

- **2026-04-09:** Daily popup panel exists and is production-polished at `Code/UI/Panels/DailyPanel.razor` (542 lines) with `DailyPanel.razor.scss` (927 lines). Project memory `project_daily_missions.md` previously said "ready for implementation" but that's stale — the system is live. Source: Explore agent task, 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** UI particles are implemented via CSS `@keyframes`, NOT a runtime particle system. Reference pattern: `MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss:1811` `.helix-particle` class. Follow this convention for any new UI burst effects. Why: user confirmed the project has a particle system, but for UI work the established pattern is CSS-based. Source: user confirmation + code grep 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** No global SCSS variables file (`_variables.scss` or `theme.scss`) exists. Colors are hardcoded per-component. When adding new UI, either reuse values from `style-guide.md` or flag the introduction of a new token with justification. Why: consistency depends on designer discipline, not tooling. Source: Explore agent task.
- **2026-04-09:** Hover sounds use `onmouseover` (NOT `onmouseenter` — unsupported in s&box) with a state guard to prevent repeat-firing. See `feedback_sbox_hover_sound.md` in user memory for the full pattern. Source: user auto-memory.
- **2026-04-09:** `DailyPanel.razor` uses `popVersion` counter on `.pop-@popVersion` CSS class to retrigger the modal open animation each time Show() is called. Source: `DailyPanel.razor:12, 338-339`.
- **2026-04-24:** Battle UI has TWO turn events with different timing — `BattleManager.OnTurnExecuted` fires at SIMULATION time (can be 1-2 turns ahead of visuals since BattleManager pre-simulates rounds), and `BattleSceneController.OnTurnVisualsStarted` fires the moment a queued turn's on-screen animation actually begins. For any UI readout that should land with the swing/impact (battle log text, ticker entries, floating labels), subscribe to `OnTurnVisualsStarted`. `OnTurnExecuted` is fine for bookkeeping that doesn't need animation sync (e.g. caching `lastTurn`). Source: `BattleSceneController.cs:47, 242-243`; `BattleView.razor` log rewire 2026-04-24 (ticker redesign).
- **2026-04-24:** For a single-element "ticker" that needs to fade in/out and whose element stays DOM-mounted across content swaps, CSS `@keyframes` won't replay (they only play on first mount in s&box). Pattern that works: render a host `<div class="foo-host @(visible ? "visible" : "")">` with default `opacity: 0` + hidden transform and `.visible` overriding both, plus a `transition` — transitions re-fire on every class toggle, unlike keyframes. Use two helper methods: `GetTickerEntry()` returns the entry during `visible + fade` window so the DOM stays mounted; `IsTickerEntryVisible()` returns true only during the `visible` window so the `.visible` class flips off and the transition animates the fade-out. Source: `BattleView.razor` battle-ticker-host / .visible pattern 2026-04-24.

- **2026-05-17:** **Keyboard navigation — input routing is centralised through `Code/UI/UIModalState.cs`.** `Input.Pressed` is GLOBAL and edge-triggered, so without routing guards every keyboard consumer (GameHUD tab bar, the active panel's `TickInput()`, every open popup's `Tick()`) sees the same press in the same frame. The fix: `UIModalState.AnyModalOpen` / `TopModal` reads each blocking popup's visibility (patterns vary — static `IsVisible`, `Instance.IsVisible`, etc.), and `GameHUD.HandleKeyboardInput()` early-returns when `Is3DBattleActive()` OR `UIModalState.AnyModalOpen` is true, while `ExpeditionPanel.HandleKeyboardInput()` early-returns when `BattleManager.Instance?.IsInBattle`. **When adding a new blocking popup, register it in `UIModalState.TopModal`** in priority order (ConfirmDialog is always highest — it can stack on anything). A popup's own `Tick()` should gate keyboard input behind `UIModalState.IsTopModal("PopupName")` so only the topmost modal in a stack consumes presses. Non-blocking HUD widgets (chat, radio, notifications, effects tray) are deliberately NOT registered. **`OptionsPanel` is mounted under BOTH MainMenu and GameHUD** — a single `Instance.IsVisible` read is unreliable, so it exposes a static `AnyVisible` that scans all instances; do the same for any other dual-mounted panel. Source: Phases 0/3/4 keyboard-nav work, `UIModalState.cs` + `GameHUD.razor` + `ExpeditionPanel.razor` 2026-05-17.

- **2026-05-17:** **Popup keyboard pattern — `kbIndex` cursor + `.kb-active` class, both in `BuildHash()`.** s&box panels are NOT `:focus`-able, so a keyboard-highlighted control needs an explicit `.kb-active` CSS class (mirror the panel's `:hover` treatment but make it visually distinct — usually a bright purple/element-color ring so it reads with the mouse parked elsewhere). Every popup that tracks a `kbIndex` MUST add that field to `BuildHash()` or the highlight never repaints. Canonical key map applied everywhere: arrows = navigate, `attack1`/`jump` = confirm/activate, `Menu`/`run` = back/cancel. **Do NOT bind new `Escape` handlers** — s&box reserves Escape; leave existing additive Escape checks alone and add `Menu`/`run` alongside them. Single-action popups (BeastShowcasePopup, TamerCardShowcasePopup, CreditsPanel) just treat confirm AND cancel as "dismiss" — no index needed. For panels where selection state already drives a highlight (ShopPanelContent's `selectedItem`, InventoryPanel's `selectedItem`), reuse that field as the keyboard cursor instead of inventing a parallel one. **Two-stage destructive confirms (OptionsPanel reset) stay mouse-only** — gate them out of keyboard handling so a stray keypress can't wipe a save. Source: Phase 4 popup pass — ConfirmDialog, OptionsPanel, MenuPopup, ExpeditionResultPopup, InventoryPanel + others 2026-05-17.

- **2026-05-17:** **`RenderFragment` helper + per-render counter pattern for indexed keyboard highlight.** OptionsPanel renders its toggle rows via a `RenderToggleRow(...)` `RenderFragment` helper called 9× in document order. To give each row a stable index for the `.kb-active` highlight without rewriting the markup: keep a `private int _toggleRowCounter`, reset it to 0 in a `@{ _toggleRowCounter = 0; }` block at the very top of `<root>` (runs first during `BuildRenderTree`), and `int rowIndex = _toggleRowCounter++;` inside the fragment body (executes in document order as each `@RenderToggleRow(...)` is rendered). Compare `rowIndex` against the keyboard cursor field for the class. Works because Razor renders top-to-bottom and fragment bodies execute inline at their call site. Source: `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor` keyboard pass 2026-05-17.

## User preferences

_(Taste calls the user has made — what they like, what they don't, what they've explicitly said)_

- **2026-04-09:** User wants UI to feel *exciting to open*, not just functional. Specifically flagged the Expedition panel as "doesn't feel satisfying or exciting to open" despite being visually consistent. The bar is emotional feel, not style consistency. Source: user message 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** User is aware of Persona 5's UI and specifically noted that "sometimes it doesn't have the impact" — tells us user distinguishes style from impact and prioritizes impact for Beastborne. Prefer Persona 5 Strikers and P3 Reload over base P5 as references. Source: user message 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** **Anti-gacha guardrail**: user explicitly stated "we also dont want the game to feel too gatcha at the same time" when approving gacha-adjacent references (HSR). HSR and Genshin are references for aesthetic moments ONLY, never structure. See `ui-references/hsr.md` for full rules. Source: user message 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** User explicitly wants the agent to keep learning over time and to be able to handle all Beastborne UI, not just specific panels. This file is the learning record. Source: user message 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** User has a human designer making monsters/beasts — do NOT propose monster designs, descriptions, or mythology picks. The agent's scope is UI only. Source: user message 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-28:** **"There is so so much right now" = license to cut hard.** When the user invokes that phrasing on a panel a previous agent stamped or revised, default to ruthless reduction, not micro-tightening. Profile card example: Resources sidebar (already in HUD), Career Stats grid (zeroes for new players, noise for veterans), Skills meter (dedicated screen), Expeditions/Trades footer (low-signal counters) — all cut. Surviving zones got bigger paddings + slightly larger headline typography to use the freed space as whitespace, not denser content. Test the agent should run before adding ANY field to an identity card: "if this number is 0 for a new player, does the card still look intentional?" If no, the field is noise. Identity cards are identity + flex, not stat dumps; if a number isn't earning its real estate it should leave. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor` reduction pass 2026-04-28 — went from 7+ zones to 5 (Hero · Arena · Favorite+Discovery · Achievements · Footer).

## s&box CSS quirks

_(Things learned about s&box's CSS engine that aren't in CLAUDE.md's quirks table yet)_

- **2026-04-09:** `DailyPanel.razor.scss:770` has `line-height: 40px` on a `font-size: 40px` element — this is flagged as a potential clipping risk by the morning agent dry-run. CLAUDE.md rule says line-height should be numerically ≥ font-size for 30px+. Need to verify empirically if 40px/40px actually clips or if it's fine at this exact ratio. If it clips, this is a real bug to fix during the juice pass. Source: morning agent dry-run 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-09:** `flex-wrap: wrap` is present in multiple modified razor.scss files (Beastiary 3, Expedition 6, MonsterRoster 8 instances flagged). CLAUDE.md says wrap miscalculates height — but it's widely used in working code. Possibly the quirk only triggers under certain conditions (specific child types? fixed-vs-auto parent height?). Needs investigation before declaring all uses broken. Source: morning agent dry-run 2026-04-09.
- **2026-04-15: [PROMOTE]** `object-position` with percentage pairs (e.g. `object-position: 50% 25%`) is REJECTED by s&box — console logs `Generic 50% 25% is not valid with object-position`. Only keyword values work (`center top`, `center center`, `left bottom`, etc). Workaround: use keyword values, OR drop `object-position` entirely and rely on `object-fit: contain` default centering. Source: `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:1547` during evolution chain headshot-crop attempt, runtime error reported by user.
- **2026-04-15: [PROMOTE]** `scrollbar-color` with two rgba values (e.g. `scrollbar-color: rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.35) rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.25)`) is REJECTED — console logs "is not valid with scrollbar-color". `scrollbar-width` with pixel values (e.g. `scrollbar-width: 6px`) also REJECTED — keyword-only (`auto`, `thin`, `none`). BOTH properties are effectively non-functional for styling. Remove entirely; scrollbars render using s&box defaults. Source: `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:512/513/555/556` runtime errors during chat/beastbook polish, 2026-04-15.
- **2026-04-15: [PROMOTE]** `filter` accepts only a SINGLE function — chaining two (e.g. `filter: brightness(0) opacity(0.55)`) causes the whole declaration to silently drop. The `opacity()` filter function in particular is unsupported. Workaround: split into a single `filter: brightness(0);` and a separate `opacity: 0.55;` property. Same applies to any multi-function combo like `grayscale(1) brightness(0.8)`. Source: repeated runtime errors across `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss` silhouette code during 2026-04-14/15 polish passes.
- **2026-04-15:** `border-left: 3px solid rgba(...)` DOES work in s&box — roster has been using it throughout for section accent bars. CLAUDE.md's quirks table previously claimed this breaks; the claim is wrong. Only certain border-side shorthand + solid + specific color combos fail — uniform `border: 1px solid rgba(...)` and per-side with color all render. Discovered while porting roster's `.section-title` accent into Beastbook. Source: grep confirmation 2026-04-15 in `MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss:7229` + successful port into `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss`.
- **2026-04-15: [PROMOTE]** Absolutely-positioned background-layer children render BEHIND the parent's `background-color` in s&box, not above. An `<img>` with `opacity: 0.025` inside `.bg-scroll { position: absolute; top/left/right/bottom: 0 }` on a panel with `background-color: #0c0a18` is invisible because the absolute child paints UNDER the parent fill. Fix: make the bg layer a plain in-flow flex child (first child, no `position: absolute`, no `.bg-scroll` CSS rules). Matches the working `RadioWidget` pattern. Source: Chat/Effects/Notifications popup background debug, 2026-04-14.
- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** `position: absolute` elements that declare ONLY `width: 100%; height: 100%;` (no `top: 0; left: 0;`) can collapse to zero size in s&box — the positioning context doesn't get set and the box is laid out at the default inset, often rendering invisibly. Standard CSS would still fill, but s&box requires explicit anchoring. **Rule:** every `position: absolute` full-fill background/overlay layer MUST declare at minimum `top: 0; left: 0;` in addition to `width: 100%; height: 100%;`. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss:35-43` `.menu-background` — image was configured correctly (path valid, size cover) but rendered pitch-black because `top/left` were omitted. Adding them restored the pattern immediately.
- **2026-04-17:** Main menu bg video trial — attempted `<video class="menu-bg-video" src="ui/main menu/loopbackground_purple.mp4" autoplay loop muted />` as the first-pass syntax for an ambient loopback background. s&box API for video in Razor UI is NOT documented in the LLM docs index (`sbox.game/llms.txt`) — zero prior `<video>` / `<Video>` / `VideoPlayer` references anywhere in `Code/`. If this first-pass plain-HTML syntax doesn't render, the fallback is likely a `Panel` subclass driving an `ImageMovieSet` or `Video` class via code-behind. Flag this entry if the user reports the video doesn't play — update with the working pattern. Source: `MainMenu.razor:12` task 2026-04-17.
- **2026-04-17:** Bare `background-color: transparent` (as a CSS color property, NOT inside gradient stops) IS accepted by s&box. The quirk only applies to the `transparent` keyword INSIDE `linear-gradient(...)` / `radial-gradient(...)` stop lists. Confirmed by inventory across `Code/UI/**/*.scss` — dozens of panels use bare `background-color: transparent` without runtime errors. The one-off `transparent` error the user saw on the main menu was likely from a different panel loading; MainMenu itself had zero gradient-stop `transparent` tokens when audited. Source: task 2026-04-17 `transparent` audit on `MainMenu.razor.scss`.
- **2026-06-03:** **Gate `.visible`-class state reveals on the CLASS the `<root>` actually carries, NOT the element-name form — `ElementName.visible` is unverified and risks never matching, which silently hides the gated content.** AchievementPanel's `<root class="ach-container @(IsVisible ? "visible" : "")">` means the panel element carries `ach-container` + `visible`. A juice pass added `AchievementPanel.visible .modal-scroll .ach-row { opacity: 1 }` as the resting (revealed) state for a staggered list entrance, while the base rule set every row to `opacity: 0`. The element-name selector form (`AchievementPanel.visible`) is used nowhere else in the codebase and is unverified in s&box's selector engine — if it doesn't resolve, EVERY row stays at `opacity: 0` and the entire list renders invisible (a silent, total content blackout, not an obvious error). Fix: gate on `.ach-container.visible` — the exact selector the modal's own working entrance already uses (`.scss:60`). Generalizes: when writing a `.visible`-gated reveal, copy the SAME selector the panel's existing proven `.visible` rules use; never introduce a novel element-name selector for a load-bearing reveal. Source: adversarial review of AchievementPanel T3 juice pass, `AchievementPanel.razor.scss:873` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **One-shot ENTRANCE `transform: translateY` on scroll-grid rows is acceptable when it's small + downward + resolves to 0 + the header above is opaque — distinct from the banned persistent hover/selected transform.** The scroll-clip quirk bans `transform` on cards inside `overflow-y: scroll` because it leaks past the viewport. But a brief entrance translateY (10px, downward, animating to 0 over ~0.34s) doesn't leak UPWARD into the header zone (downward shift pushes top rows further into the viewport, not out the top), the header is opaque, and there's no steady-state overhang. Judged ship-safe in adversarial review. The dangerous cases the quirk targets are PERSISTENT transforms (hover lift held while hovering), UPWARD/large transforms, and colored box-shadow halos — those leak visibly. Keep entrance transforms small + downward + transient. Source: AchievementPanel `.modal-scroll .ach-row` entrance, `.scss:851` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** Binding a live s&box `Texture` (e.g. from `VideoPlayer.Texture`) as a Panel's `background-image` CANNOT be done via inline-style string interpolation (`style="background-image: url({texture})"`). `Texture.ToString()` does NOT produce a CSS-parseable URL — the parser either fails silently or logs "Unknown Image Type". **Canonical pattern:** capture the Panel via `@ref="foo"` in Razor and call `foo.Style.SetBackgroundImage(texture)` each Tick once `texture != null`. Cheap to call repeatedly with the same Texture handle. Source: `MainMenu.razor` ambient video background debug, 2026-04-17 — `VideoBackground.cs` exposes `VideoPlayer.Texture`; initial inline-url approach produced no video, switching to `@ref + Style.SetBackgroundImage(Texture)` is the fix.

- **2026-06-03: [PROMOTE]** **CSS custom properties (`--foo` / `var(--foo)`) are NOT supported in s&box — do not parameterize @keyframes with them.** A natural radial-burst pattern is one shared `@keyframes mote { to { transform: translate(var(--mx), var(--my)) } }` plus per-mote `--mx/--my` overrides. s&box has zero `var(--...)` usage anywhere in `Code/UI` (grep confirmed) and almost certainly won't resolve it — the burst would collapse to all motes landing at the origin. **Use the proven baked pattern instead:** one numbered `@keyframes` per mote with the end-translate hardcoded (cf. DailyPanel/AchievementPanel `cbP0..cbP11`). Caught pre-ship during the TradingPanel T3 pass before it could fail at runtime. Source: TradingPanel trade-complete burst, `TradingPanel.razor.scss` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **Mount-time @keyframes that animate `transform` will CLOBBER an element's static base `transform` (e.g. `skewX(-8deg)` or a per-element `rotate()`) for the duration + (with `forwards`) permanently.** Two safe options: (a) if the element has a base transform you must preserve, animate OPACITY ONLY for its reveal (used on `.request-title` skew and the `.cray` god-ray beams — the parent owns the spin, each ray keeps its static rotate while only fading); (b) if you must animate transform, bake the base transform INTO every keyframe step (used on `.complete-title`: `transform: skewX(-8deg) translateY(...)` at both 0% and 100%). The @keyframes analog of the class-toggle-transform clobber; applies even to reliable mount-time keyframes. Source: TradingPanel T3 + request-popup polish, `TradingPanel.razor.scss` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **TradingPanel raised to T3 on trade-complete + T2 polish on the request popup.** The trade-complete overlay was already conditionally-rendered (`@if showTradeComplete`), so mount-time @keyframes fire reliably every completion — built the T3 celebration entirely on @keyframes (scrim fade, green screen flash, twin shockwave rings, 12-mote baked-direction burst, opacity-fading god-rays under a slow-spinning parent, slab slam with icon pop + staggered title/sub settle). Layered `PlaySuccess()` + `PlayGoldReward()` as T3 audio (no dedicated trade chime exists — flag for product). Auto-close 2.5s→3.2s to let the reveal breathe. Request popup (preserved): gold icon disc gets a swap-in spin + halo loop, content staggers in (title/body/buttons at 0.12/0.20/0.28s), accept/decline gained `:active` press states + accept `PlayForward()`. Anti-gacha clean. Source: TradingPanel juice pass, `TradingPanel.razor` + `.scss` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03: [PROMOTE]** **A `transition` SHORTHAND in any matching rule RESETS `transition-delay` to 0 — so a per-item inline `transition-delay` stagger gets silently clobbered by a `.entered ... { transition: opacity 0.3s ease-out }` rule. Use transition LONGHANDS when the delay comes from elsewhere.** The reliable 26.06.03 stagger pattern is per-card inline `style="transition-delay: Ns"` + a class-gated reveal rule that sets the resting `opacity: 1` and the transition. If that reveal rule uses the `transition` shorthand (which omits delay → defaults delay to 0), the cascade can drop the inline delay and every card fades in simultaneously. Inline specificity *should* win per spec, but s&box's longhand-vs-shorthand cascade resolution is unverified — don't bet the stagger on it. **Fix:** in the reveal rule use `transition-property/-duration/-timing-function` longhands (all confirmed working in 26.06.03) and NEVER the shorthand, leaving `transition-delay` to come purely from the inline style. Caught pre-ship in CardCollectionPanel adversarial review. Source: `CardCollectionPanel.razor.scss:388` (`.entered .card-grid .collected-card-wrapper`) 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-05:** **An infinite `@keyframes` that animates `box-shadow` on an element CLOBBERS that element's `:hover`/`.selected` box-shadow (animations beat transitions/static declarations on the shared property).** On MainMenu's `.lh-play` a breathing `box-shadow` loop hid both the hover glow and the keyboard-`.selected` gold ring. Two fixes used: (1) for `.selected`, set `animation: none` so the static ring shows — but note `animation: none` kills ALL animations on that element, so keep any idle effect you want to survive (e.g. a sheen) on a CHILD element, not the animated parent; (2) accept that hover-glow is masked and let the hover `transform` lift carry the interaction signal. Generalizes: if an element needs both an ambient box-shadow loop AND state-driven box-shadow, split them onto parent/child or animate different properties. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-play` color+animation pass 2026-06-05.

- **2026-06-05:** **Idle "sheen sweep" pattern that works in s&box:** a child `<span>` overlay (`position:absolute; width:50%; left:-60%; height:100%`) with a 105deg white linear-gradient (use `rgba(255,255,255,0)` not `transparent` for the end stops) + `@keyframes` translating it `translateX(700%)` with a long dwell off-screen (`55%→100%` held at end) reads as an occasional gleam, not a strobe. Parent needs `overflow:hidden` to clip to the rounded rect — AND `flex:0 0 auto` if the parent is itself an auto-sized flex child, because `overflow:hidden` on an auto-sized flex child can collapse it to zero (s&box quirk). Text siblings need `position:relative; z-index:1` to stay above the sheen. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-play` / `.lh-play-sheen` 2026-06-05.

- **2026-06-05:** **Card elevation in s&box without `box-shadow: inset`:** to make a dark card read as RAISED off a near-black page bg, (a) bake a top-edge highlight into the bg gradient (brighter band at 0–30%, deepening to near-black at 100%) using a ~160deg angle so "lit from above" reads, (b) add a thin absolute top-edge specular strip (`height:2px; left/right:0`) with a white-centered horizontal gradient, (c) layer the box-shadow: deep ambient drop + a faint colored rim (`0 0 0 1px rgba(255,255,255,0.04)`), (d) optional faint breathing rim-glow @keyframes gated on the `.entered` class so it doesn't fight the entrance fade. Avoids the banned CSS radial disc behind the hero. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-featured` + `.lh-feat-topedge` 2026-06-05.

- **2026-06-05:** **Screenshot placeholder pattern (on-brand, reads intentional not broken):** s&box has no `border-style:dashed`, so fake a placeholder frame with a faint solid `1px` violet-tint border + a slightly-darker inset gradient fill, a centered `lucide:image` iconify glyph, an uppercase tracked "SCREENSHOT" label, and a small dim hint line. Best placement on MainMenu was REUSING the sidebar's `.lh-spacer` slot (flex:1 between nav and the Discord card) — gives a genuine empty art slot without compressing the already-full main column (event pill + flex:1 featured + 250px card row leaves no vertical room there). Swap the frame's inner content for a real `<img>` later. Source: `MainMenu.razor` `.lh-shot-sidebar` 2026-06-05.

- **2026-06-03:** **Multi-phase setpieces: a per-phase CLASS SWAP on a still-mounted element does NOT replay its @keyframe — the loop + exit phases die silently.** Even after the 26.06.03 parser rewrite, the "@keyframes only fire on first DOM mount" rule holds. DailyPanel's Day-7 setpiece drove sub-phases via `<div class="day7-scrim phase-@day7Stage">` with `.phase-2 { animation: in }`, `.phase-3 { animation: loop }`, `.phase-4 { animation: out }` on ONE persistent element. Only the FIRST class the element mounted with played; every later phase animation was dead (spotlight froze, nothing eased out on settle). The comment in the code even claimed "s&box replays @keyframes on element-own class change" — FALSE. **Two-part fix pattern for setpiece elements:** (1) PERSISTENT elements (live across stages) — drive enter/hold/exit via CSS TRANSITIONS keyed on a parent `.stage-N` class (transitions DO re-fire on class toggle); keep only genuine infinite ambient loops as @keyframes (start at mount, run continuously) and make those loops animate a DIFFERENT property than the transition owns (loop=transform, transition=opacity — animations beat transitions on a shared property, so the exit fade would be blocked otherwise). (2) MOUNT-AND-UNMOUNT elements (iris @stage 1, rays/banner/confetti @stage 3) — gate with `@if (stage == N)` so they truly mount/unmount; @keyframes fire fresh on mount. **Critical companion:** transition-driven ENTER only fires on a class change AFTER mount — an element that mounts already matching `.stage-2 .foo` pops into the target with no animation. Fix: mount persistent layers at a stage-0 PRIMING FRAME (hidden base state) one frame before the first real stage, so every later stage flip is a real class change. DailyPanel adds `day7Stage=0; await Delay(0.05f);` before `day7Stage=1`. Source: adversarial review of DailyPanel Day-7 setpiece, `DailyPanel.razor.scss:2178+` / `DailyPanel.razor:905` 2026-06-03.

## Input / keyboard

_(How s&box input actions resolve, and the project's keyboard-nav conventions)_

- **2026-05-17:** `Input.Pressed(string)` action-name lookup is **case-INSENSITIVE** in s&box. Evidence: `MainMenu.razor:1490` ships a player-facing Ctrl+Shift+R reset shortcut using lowercase action names (`"duck"`, `"run"`, `"reload"`) since v1.0.1 Open Beta; the reset-confirm overlay (`MainMenu.razor:549`) only appears when that check fires AND renders the hint text "(Triggered by Ctrl+Shift+R on the title screen)" — a no-op lookup would mean a documented shortcut had been broken across multiple releases. So lowercase action names are NOT silent no-ops; no casing churn is needed. Source: case-sensitivity investigation task 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-17:** Canonical keyboard-nav map across all Beastborne panels/popups: arrows or WASD = navigate, **Space + Enter** = confirm, **Q** (`Menu` action) = back/close (Shift / `run` is an accepted back-alias, pre-existing convention), number keys = numbered slots/tabs. Confirm must be `Input.Pressed("Jump") || Input.Pressed("Enter")` — NOT `attack1`, which is bound to mouse1 (not a keyboard key) and could double-fire with a mouse click. Normalized ~20 confirm sites this session. Source: keyboard-nav consistency sweep 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-17:** Input actions `PrevWeapon` / `NextWeapon` are referenced in `BeastiaryPanel.razor`, `MonsterRosterPanel.razor`, and `ShopPanelContent.razor` but **do not exist in `ProjectSettings/Input.config`** — they are genuine no-ops (the s&box defaults are `SlotPrev`/`SlotNext`, bound to mouse4/mouse5, no keyboard codes). Element-cycle / store-swap / fusion-mode-toggle keyboard shortcuts in those panels currently do nothing. Fixing requires editing `Input.config` (out of UI-agent scope) or rebinding to existing actions — flagged for the user. Source: keyboard-nav audit 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-17: [RESOLVED]** The three dead `PrevWeapon`/`NextWeapon` shortcuts above were rebound to real `Input.config` actions: Beastiary element-cycle and Shop store-swap → `RadioPrev`/`RadioNext` (`[`/`]` — a natural prev/next pair, no collision with grid arrows / Space-Enter confirm / Q back). Roster fusion mode → `Reload` (R) exits fusion, `Use` (E) toggles parent slot / enters fusion; `Menu` (Q) was already taken by "breed" so exit could not reuse it. Rule for future rebinds: `[`/`]` (RadioPrev/RadioNext) are the cleanest free pair for prev/next cycles since no panel handler binds them; `Reload`/`Use` are good single-action keys. Source: Task A, `BeastiaryPanel.razor:1290`, `ShopPanelContent.razor:1070`, `MonsterRosterPanel.razor:3944/4027` 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-17:** **Grid ↔ detail-panel keyboard focus-switch pattern** (added to `MonsterRosterPanel` + `BeastiaryPanel`, both of which use the shared `collection-layout with-detail` → `collection-main` (grid) + `detail-sidebar` layout). A `string kbFocusZone` field (`"grid"` default | `"detail"`) gates which zone the keyboard drives; `Tab` (`Score` action) hands focus grid→detail, `Tab` or `Q` (`Menu`) returns. In the detail zone the handler intercepts BEFORE grid nav and `return`s, so the grid cursor freezes while detail has focus. Visible affordance: a clickable `.kb-focus-switch` pill at the top of the detail scroll area (mouse parity via `ToggleKbFocusZone()`), label flips `TAB · Control details` ⇄ `Q · Back to grid`. Source: Task B, `MonsterRosterPanel.razor` + `BeastiaryPanel.razor` 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-17: [OUTDATED — superseded below]** _(original focus-switch entry's container-zone-border + action-buttons-only detail nav)._
- **2026-05-17:** **Container-level zone outline LEAKS on `.detail-sidebar` — never put a focus border on it.** Roster + Beastbook `.detail-sidebar` has `margin-left: -20px` (it tucks UNDER the grid panel by design, with `padding-left` compensating) and a `0 16px 16px 0` radius. Any `border-color` change on that element paints the left edge BEHIND the grid panel / outside the visible bounds — a visible purple sliver. Rule: communicate keyboard-zone focus ONLY via (a) the per-control `.kb-focused` highlight and (b) the affordance pill state — NOT a container outline. Removed `.kb-zone-active`/`.kb-zone-dim` from both panels. Source: user bug report, roster detail focus border leak 2026-05-17.
- **2026-05-18:** **Multi-view panel keyboard nav — single (`_kbZone`, `_kbIndex`) cursor, not one cursor per region.** GuildPanel has 4 views (landing/browse/myguild/create) plus ~8 blocking sub-states. Rather than a field per region, ONE `string _kbZone` + `int _kbIndex` + `bool _kbActive` covers everything: `_kbZone` names the focusable region (`"landing"`, `"browse-grid"`, `"tiles"`, `"roster"`, `"perks"`, `"danger"`, `"create"`, `"raid-select"`, …) and `_kbIndex` walks items inside it. `TickInput()` dispatches blocking sub-states FIRST (each `return`s — they own the keyboard), then `switch (guildView)`. `ResetKb()` clears all three on every view/sub-state transition so a stale index can't carry a ghost cursor. Per-region `IsXxxKb(idx)` predicates gate the `guild-kb-selected` class. **Conditionally-rendered button lists are the trap:** the plaza danger bar and member context menu render buttons via `@if` (claim/transfer/leave/disband; promote/demote/kick/close). The `TickInput` handler MUST build the SAME live list with the SAME `@if` conditions and in the SAME order as the markup, and the markup must assign each button a running `dangerKbIdx++` / `menuKbIdx++` index inside an `@{ }` block — a fixed 0-3 mapping breaks the moment one `@if` is false. Source: GuildPanel keyboard-nav task, `Code/UI/Panels/GuildPanel.razor` 2026-05-18.

- **2026-05-18:** **Prefer continuous W/S vertical flow over Tab-to-switch-zones for a multi-zone panel — Tab is undiscoverable.** GuildPanel's plaza first used Tab to cycle zones `{tiles, info, roster, perks, danger}`; a playtest found players never discovered Tab and got stuck in the first zone. Reworked so W/S flow vertically through ALL zones as ONE continuous cursor (crossing zone boundaries automatically in visual top-to-bottom order) and A/D move horizontally WITHIN the current zone. Implementation keeps the internal `_kbZone`/`_kbIndex` pair: a `PlazaVertical(dir)` helper checks if the current zone is multi-row (the roster grid) and steps a grid row, otherwise calls `PlazaCrossZone(dir)` which walks `_plazaZones` to the next zone with a non-zero live item count (skipping runtime-empty zones like the officer-only `info` zone), landing on its FIRST item when going down / LAST item when going up. A multi-row zone only crosses out at its true top/bottom grid edge (`next = idx ± cols` out of `[0,count)`). Per-zone `PlazaZoneCount(zone)` + `GetPlaza<Zone>...()` live-list helpers keep counts in sync with the markup. Tab is reserved ONLY for genuine "escape a text input" cases (the browse view's search-field⇄grid toggle) where W/S would type into the field. Source: GuildPanel plaza nav rework — playtest feedback, `GuildPanel.razor` 2026-05-18.

- **2026-05-18:** **Reset a sub-panel's keyboard cursor on its OPEN EDGE, not on the keystroke that opens it — a modal can be opened by mouse too.** GuildPanel's settings drawer is opened either by the keyboard (the Settings plaza tile) or a mouse click on that same tile. Resetting `_kbIndex` only in the keyboard-open path leaves a stale index when the drawer is mouse-opened. Robust fix: track `bool _settingsWasOpen`, and in `Tick()` do `if (showSettingsModal && !_settingsWasOpen) _kbIndex = 0; _settingsWasOpen = showSettingsModal;` — fires once on the open transition regardless of input source. The tracking bool does NOT go in `BuildHash()` (it doesn't affect rendering). Source: GuildPanel settings-drawer keyboard nav 2026-05-18.

- **2026-05-18:** **`guild-kb-selected` focus ring must recolor an EXISTING base border, never add one.** s&box is content-box (`box-sizing: border-box` rejected) — a focus rule that introduces a fresh `border: 2px solid` on an element whose base had no border shifts that element's content by 2px the instant it's focused. Fix: if the base control has no border, add a transparent reserved one to the BASE rule (`border: 2px solid rgba(0,0,0,0)`) so the focus rule only changes `border-color`. Did this for `.create-stamp-pick-row` (base was borderless). Controls that already have a base border (`.browse-card-v2`, `.plaza-action-tile`, `.perk-row`, `.member-portrait`, `.guild-btn`, …) just recolor. Exception accepted: `.raid-select-option` (base borderless, inside a scroll grid whose `:hover` ALREADY shifts via `translateY` — layout shift on interaction is established there, so a fresh 2px focus border is consistent). Source: GuildPanel keyboard-nav task 2026-05-18.

- **2026-05-17:** **Full detail-panel keyboard nav — 2D (row,col) cursor over a dynamically-built focusable grid.** Roster detail panel made fully keyboard-operable: `GetDetailFocusRows()` returns `List<List<string>>` where each inner list is one navigable ROW of control-ids, built fresh each call so conditional controls (Journal diamond, Evolve button) only appear when rendered. Cursor is `(detailRowIndex, detailColIndex)`: Up/Down step rows (col clamped to new row width), Left/Right move within a row. `IsDetailControlFocused(id)` drives the markup `.kb-focused` class; `ActivateFocusedDetailControl()` dispatches to the SAME methods each control's `onclick` calls. Reaches: nickname pencil, 3 portrait diamonds, action buttons, all 4 move slots. STATS/INFO/TRAITS are display-only (`.trait-card` has no `onclick`) — skipped. **Scroll-into-view:** s&box has no native scroll-into-view; put `@ref` on the scroll container, find the focused element by recursing `Panel.Children` for `HasClass("kb-focused")` (pattern from `TutorialPanel.FindByClass`), then compute `ScrollOffset.y` from `Box.Rect` (control top/bottom in content space vs viewport, ±margin). Must DEFER the scroll by one tick after the cursor moves (a `bool ...FollowPending` flag consumed in `Tick()`) so layout has positioned the new `.kb-focused` element before `Box.Rect` is read. **Highlight quirk-safety:** every `.kb-focused` rule is BORDER + BACKGROUND only — no `transform`, no `box-shadow` — because the controls live in an `overflow-y: scroll` container that does NOT clip descendant shadow/transform (CLAUDE.md). Inline pickers (item/move) that open inside the scroll: make Q/Tab close the picker rather than leave to the grid so the keyboard player isn't stranded. All cursor fields go in `BuildHash()`. Source: Task B follow-up, `MonsterRosterPanel.razor` 2026-05-17.

- **2026-05-18:** **A child rule that sets `background-color` cannot recolor a parent/base rule that set its color via `background: linear-gradient(...)`.** A gradient resolves to the `background-IMAGE` property, which paints ON TOP of `background-color` — so the child's color is hidden underneath the inherited gradient. To recolor such an element you must ALSO clear the gradient: `background-image: none;` (a proven s&box-safe value — used 9+ places across the UI incl. `GameHUD.razor.scss`). Compounding trap: `background: #hex` shorthand-with-a-color is itself REJECTED by s&box (parsed as a `background-image`, "Unknown Image Type") and silently no-ops — so an override written as `background: #7c3aed` does nothing at all, masking the real problem. **Rule: for any solid-color override use `background-color:` for the color AND `background-image: none;` to kill any inherited gradient — never `background:` shorthand with a color.** Symptom that points here: "my color override isn't taking effect and the element keeps its old gradient." Source: Quests `!` badge purple recolor — `.cb-action-badge` base set green via `background: linear-gradient(...)`, `&.quests-badge` override failed twice until split into `background-color` + `background-image: none`, `GameHUD.razor.scss` 2026-05-18.

## Style observations

_(Observations about Beastborne's visual identity, recurring patterns, signature elements)_

- **2026-04-09:** Beastborne's core color identity: purple `#8b5cf6` (interaction), gold `#fbbf24` (reward), green `#34d399` (success). Backgrounds are dark gray-purple (`#0c0a18` to `#12101e`). This is the palette — new UI should respect it unless there's a specific reason. Source: style-guide.md, extracted from `DailyPanel.razor.scss`, `BattleView.razor.scss`, `MonsterCard.razor.scss`.
- **2026-04-09:** Signature move: escalating heights on day nodes (90→165px across days 1-7). Good pattern for "building toward something" visuals. Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss:323-348`.
- **2026-04-09:** Signature move: rotating silhouette on Day 7 cycles through 4 legendary species every 3 seconds as a teaser. Could be generalized to "mystery prize" teasers elsewhere. Source: `DailyPanel.razor:75, 362-372`.
- **2026-04-17:** Painted-bevel technique for buttons — instead of using `border-top-color` with a solid hotter tone for the "highlight" edge (which reads as a bolt-on rim that runs edge-to-edge and hard-stops at the corners), use a `::after` pseudo-element absolutely positioned `top: 0; left: 0; right: 0; height: 2-3px;` with a horizontal `linear-gradient(to right, rgba(r,g,b,0) 0%, rgba(r,g,b,0.85) 50%, rgba(r,g,b,0) 100%)` background. The parent needs `position: relative; overflow: hidden;` (inherits border-radius clip). The solid `border-top-color` should be DEMOTED to a low-alpha base (~0.28-0.32) so it's a subtle backstop, not a hard duplicate line. Hover state can brighten the gradient's mid-stop while preserving the end-fades. Remember: `transparent` keyword inside gradient stops is REJECTED by s&box — must use `rgba(r,g,b,0)` explicitly. Result: the top bevel reads as PAINTED (fades into the corners) rather than bolt-on. Used on `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.menu-btn` / `.btn-play` / `.menu-chip` 2026-04-17 per user feedback that edge-to-edge highlights looked like hard stripes. Pattern is reusable on any chunky-border "Beastborne-tier" button across the project. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss:497-510, 597-602, 697-710` painted-highlight pass 2026-04-17.
- **2026-04-28: [PROMOTE]** **Persona-stamp skew is a SINGULAR accent, never a global treatment.** When applying P5-style chrome (sharp corners, 2px black borders, hard offset shadows, halftone, diamond bullets), it's tempting to skew every stamp pill / corner tag / button — every prior agent pass on the profile card did. Result: 6+ skewed elements all rotating at slightly different angles read as a tic, not a flourish. Persona itself uses ONE iconic angled stamp per surface; everything else is upright. **Rule for any P5-stamp panel:** pick a single hero stamp (usually the top corner-tag identifier) and keep `transform: rotate(...)` on it; every other stamp / pill / corner tag / button stays upright. Italic-stamp typography on numerics (italic + hard text-shadow) follows the same rule — too much italicization on every value reads as a font tic; bold + hard text-shadow + uppercase letterspacing are the always-on Persona vocabulary, italic is reserved for one or two big setpiece numbers (and even there, keep the giant "identity number" upright — it's identity, not flair). User direction: "we don't always need that angled box look. it's for one times on a page." Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor.scss` skew-restraint pass 2026-04-28 — kept skew on `.profile-corner-tag`; removed from `.avatar-rank-stamp`, `.title-edit-btn`, `.rank-pill`, `.guild-pill`, `.level-stamp-pill`, `.arena-stamp-tag`, `.rank-icon-square`, `.fav-change-hint`. Removed `font-style: italic` from `.level-number`, `.rank-name`, `.arena-winrate-value`, `.arena-stat-value`, `.discovery-count`, `.discovery-cap`, `.prog-meter-value`, `.prog-meter-cap`.
- **2026-04-28:** **`margin-left: auto` on a flex child can absorb the parent's `gap`.** A flex row with `gap: 6px` containing `[count][cap][label]` where `.label { margin-left: auto }` will push the label to the far right but ALSO collapse the gap between cap and label, so they render flush ("146DISCOVERED"). Standard CSS would still apply the gap, but in s&box's flex impl the `auto` margin trumps it. **Fix:** add explicit `padding-left` on the auto-margined child (or swap to a non-auto margin model). Discovered fixing the discovery slab on the profile card. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor.scss` `.discovery-label` 2026-04-28 — added `padding-left: 12px` alongside the existing `margin-left: auto`.
- **2026-05-01:** **Stacked offset-zero `box-shadow` colors are the right way to fake "two accent strips" on opposite edges of an element when per-side `border-X: ... solid color` shorthand is fragile in s&box.** The 3D battle action cluster backplate originally had a single `-3px 0 0 rgba(255, 206, 58, 0.85)` (left-edge yellow strip) — adding a balancing right-edge yellow accent via stacking `3px 0 0 rgba(255, 206, 58, 0.35)` on the same `box-shadow` works perfectly and renders both strips simultaneously alongside the main drop shadow. **Pattern:** `box-shadow: 4px 4px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.5), -3px 0 0 rgba(yellow, 0.85), 3px 0 0 rgba(yellow, 0.35);` — first stop is the drop shadow, second/third are the edge accents (zero blur, zero spread, only X-offset to one side). Asymmetric alpha makes the leading edge the hero and the trailing edge a quieter flourish — matches the Persona dialect of "stamped, but with one obvious anchor." Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss` `.action3d-cluster-bg` polish pass 2026-05-01.
- **2026-05-01:** **Cluster-wide hover via `:hover` on the parent flex container resolves a common P5-stamp need: "treat 4 buttons + a backplate + a halo as ONE coherent surface that lights up when the mouse enters its zone."** Pattern: `.action-buttons:hover .cluster-bg { background-color: brighter; }` and `.action-buttons:hover .fight-halo.awaiting { transform: scale(1.05); }`. Sibling-combinator scoping to a SINGLE child's hover (e.g. only when FIGHT is hovered, not BAG) requires the affected sibling to come AFTER the trigger in DOM — for elements that precede the buttons (cluster-bg z-index 0, halo z-index 1), this isn't possible without DOM reorder. **Cluster-wide trigger is honest to the "one surface" framing**; reserve the per-child sibling combinator for cases where the trigger genuinely IS one of the visually-later siblings. Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss` `.action3d-buttons:hover .action3d-cluster-bg` and `.action3d-buttons:hover .action3d-fight-halo.awaiting` polish pass 2026-05-01.

- **2026-05-01:** **Tab-content @switch in QuestPanel mounts a fresh `.tab-content` per tab change — single-instance stamp targets like `.tab-title`, `.timer-pill`, `.multiplier-badge`, `.claim-streak-btn`, `.bonus-claim-btn` are SAFE to apply the full Persona-stamp dialect to even though the renderers are reused across 6 tabs.** Each `case`/`RenderFragment` swap mounts new DOM, so per-tab "single-instance" elements behave like genuinely single-instance for stamp/compounding purposes. The repeating elements that DO compound (and must NOT be stamped) are `.mission-card` (3-15 per tab), `.cal-day` (always 7 in a row), `.sidebar-btn` (6 stacked vertically), `.reward-pip` (multiple per row × multiple rows), and the per-row `.claim-btn` inside mission cards. **Override the per-row `.claim-btn` selectively for `.bonus-claim-btn`** — bonus-card claim is single-instance per tab and is the appropriate "complete-everything payoff" target for the stamped recipe. Keep the inherited `questClaimBreath` animation since the stamp surface (border + shadow + italic label) reads through the breathing scale just fine. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/QuestPanel.razor.scss` Persona-stamp dialect pass 2026-05-01.

- **2026-05-01:** **`animation: none` is rejected the same way `filter: none` is — use `animation-play-state: paused` to neutralize an inherited animation.** When a `.bonus-claim-btn` inherits `.claim-btn`'s `animation: questClaimBreath 1.5s ease-in-out infinite` but you want the variant to stay perfectly still, the keyword `none` likely fails parser-side (mirroring the documented `filter: none` failure). Safer override: `animation-play-state: paused` — keeps the animation declaration valid but freezes it at the start frame. (In this pass we ended up KEEPING the breath animation on the bonus-claim variant because the stamp visual reads through it cleanly, but the override knowledge stays useful for cases where you genuinely want the stamp dead-still.) Source: `Code/UI/Panels/QuestPanel.razor.scss` `.bonus-claim-btn` Persona-stamp pass 2026-05-01.

- **2026-05-01:** **ArenaPanel + QuestPanel single-instance stamp inventory** — confirmed safe targets across both panels: panel/header titles (`.mode-title`, `.team-select-title`, `.history-title`, `.rules-title`, `.quest-title`, `.tab-title`, `.team-label`); single-instance counter/timer pills (`.team-counter`, `.timer-pill`, `.multiplier-badge`, `.season-badge`); hero CTAs (`.battle-btn.online`, `.battle-btn.ai`, `.action-btn` family, `.between-ready-btn`, `.result-continue-btn`, `.mm-cancel-btn`, `.claim-streak-btn`); side-action toolbars limited to ≤3 instances per row (`.side-btn` for LEADERBOARD/HISTORY/RULES); single-instance scoreboard headlines (`.bo3-label-text`); single-instance bonus CTAs (`.bonus-claim-btn`). **Skipped for compounding:** `.mode-card` (only 2 but they ARE the hero of mode-select; stamping would compete with their card identity), `.history-entry` rows, `.ban-entry` rows, `.cal-day` (7 in row), `.game-pip`/`.result-game-pip`/`.bonus-dot` (repeating pips), `.mission-card` rows, per-row `.claim-btn`, `.sidebar-btn` (6 stacked = vertical rail), `.reward-pip` (many per row). Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ArenaPanel.razor.scss` + `Code/UI/Panels/QuestPanel.razor.scss` Persona-stamp pass 2026-05-01.
- **2026-05-01:** **Persona-stamp dialect punch list for any panel:** when bringing a panel up to dialect, the safe "single-instance" stamp targets are (a) the panel/header title, (b) any single-instance counter or completion pill in the header, (c) `.section-title` rules (only one swappable view is open at a time, so they don't compound), (d) the BIG hero label in the detail sidebar (e.g. `.bb-name`), (e) detail-sidebar primary action buttons, (f) any single-instance modal title/close/control row, (g) single-instance summary labels like TOTAL / TRAITS at the bottom of a stats grid. **NEVER stamp:** repeating grid cards (50+ instances), pill families used >5 times in identity rows (rarity / element / personality / trait / mastery-tier), per-row diamonds in evolution chains or move lists, list rows (lore / mastery tiers / stat rows). Per-entity accents COMPOUND in scrollable grids — they create a continuous visual rail and break the "grid stays calm so detail pops" hierarchy. The italic-display recipe is consistent: `font-weight: 900; letter-spacing: 2; text-transform: uppercase; font-style: italic; text-shadow: 1px 1px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.85);` paired with `line-height >= font-size + 2` to give the slant headroom. Slab pill recipe: `background-color: rgba(20, 14, 32, 0.92); border: 2px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55); border-radius: 4px; box-shadow: 3px 3px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4);` Source: BeastiaryPanel stamp pass 2026-05-01 + ExpeditionPanel pass 2026-04-30.

- **2026-05-18: [PROMOTE]** **When reusing the shared `.cb-key` gold hotkey cap (`GameHUD.razor.scss:860`) outside the command bar, DO NOT enlarge it or change its `line-height` — keep its native 16×14 size, 9px font, and unitless `line-height: 11` completely untouched. That exact metric recipe is what makes the glyph sit dead-centre; any deviation floats the glyph high in the cap.** This cost THREE rounds on the TeamPicker before the right answer landed. What went wrong: enlarging the cap (16×14→18→20px), bumping the font (9→12px), and swapping the unitless `line-height` for a `px` value all broke vertical centring — the glyph drifted up with more empty gold below it than above. Two further rounds of `px` line-height tweaking did not fix it. **The fix was to STOP overriding geometry and let the caps render at native `.cb-key` size.** The reason a panel-scoped companion class is still needed at all is THREE non-geometry deviations only: (1) `opacity: 1` — `.cb-key` rests at 0.8 and is lifted to full opacity by host selectors (`.cb-tab:hover .cb-key` …) that exist ONLY in the command bar, so a plain `.cb-key` elsewhere is stuck dim; (2) `letter-spacing: 0` on the span — `.cb-key span` sets `0.5px`, which adds trailing space after a lone glyph and nudges it left-of-centre; (3) upright glyph — `.cb-key span` is italic, and an italic lone char shifts horizontally + reads worse, so `font-style: normal` + `transform: skewX(0deg)` (both halves, since the project italic is `font-style` + `skewX`). Everything else — size, font-size, line-height, color (`#fbbf24` canonical game gold; a paler `#fcd34d` was rejected as washed-out), weight, border, shadow — INHERITS unchanged. **The "native size is too small to read" worry was a false alarm** — it was really the dull test colour; at the correct `#fbbf24` the native 16×14 cap is legible even on a 60px hero CTA (the command bar proves native size daily). Rule: native-and-centered beats bigger-and-misaligned every time. Never restyle `.cb-key` globally. A cap is an ANNOTATION not a peer of the icon — tuck it tight (negative `margin-right` vs the flex `gap`, or ~6-7px). Source: `Code/UI/Panels/TeamPickerPopup.razor.scss` `.picker-cap` / `.picker-cap-embark` — final native-passthrough resolution 2026-05-18 after 3 failed enlargement attempts.
- **2026-05-18:** **When a control row MUST stay one line, the fix is `flex-wrap: nowrap` + compacting the children to fit the column width — never let it wrap.** TeamPicker's PRESETS row (label + 3 preset slots) broke two ways: first the widened caps squeezed the "PRESETS" label until it wrapped mid-word ("PRESET"/"S"); then a `flex-wrap: wrap` "safety net" was wrong too — it dropped the 3rd preset slot to a second line, which the user explicitly rejected ("the label + all three slots must fit on ONE row"). Correct recipe: (1) `white-space: nowrap` + `flex-shrink: 0` on the label so it's a fixed atom; (2) `flex-shrink: 0` on every button/slot so they keep content width; (3) `flex-wrap: nowrap` on the row; (4) BUDGET the cluster against the column width and trim children (button padding, cap→icon gaps, label tracking + margin, inter-slot margins, drop the last slot's trailing margin) until label + N slots clear the column with headroom. For the 380px left column the trio + label landed at ~363px. Lesson: `flex-wrap: wrap` is only a safety net when wrapping is acceptable — when one row is a hard requirement, compact instead. Also pin control rows to `height: auto` not a fixed px height — a fixed row height clips content overflow. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/TeamPickerPopup.razor.scss` `.ps-presets-row` / `.ps-preset-label` / `.preset-btn` / `.preset-slot` 2026-05-18.
- **2026-05-18:** **For a seamed button PAIR (two buttons sharing a border, e.g. preset-load + preset-save), the press-shift transform must live on the PARENT wrapper, not the individual button.** A `transform: translate(1px,1px)` on one half shifts only that half and tears the shared seam open for the duration of the press. Put the `:active` transform on the wrapping `.preset-slot` (a flex row of both buttons) so the pair moves as one unit; give each individual button a non-motion press cue (background darken) so the player can still see which half they hit. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/TeamPickerPopup.razor.scss` `.preset-slot:active` 2026-05-18.
- **2026-05-18:** **Keyboard-hint annotations should be visually attached to the control they drive, not floated near unrelated chrome.** The TeamPicker `[ ] cycle element` hint originally rendered as a sibling after `<FilterBar>` and, because FilterBar is `flex-wrap: wrap`, landed in the top-right next to the SORT controls — yet it controls the far-LEFT element dropdown. Fix: own row directly under the FilterBar, `align-self: flex-start` (and make the wrapper an explicit `flex-direction: column; align-items: flex-start` so s&box stacks it deterministically), small enclosing chip with a faint purple border, plus a `lucide:arrow-up-left` glyph pointing back at the dropdown. The arrow makes the "this controls the thing above-and-left of me" relationship explicit. General rule: a hotkey hint with no spatial relationship to its target is just noise — anchor it. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/TeamPickerPopup.razor` `.picker-cycle-hint` reposition 2026-05-18.

- **2026-06-03: [PROMOTE]** **26.06.03 `transition-delay` finally enables a real "panel-open stagger that REPLAYS on every re-open" — but the staggered elements must carry position-aware transforms, and the delay must live ONLY on the `.visible` (open-direction) rules.** ContractNegotiationPanel's four approach diamonds used first-mount `@keyframes diamondFanIn ... both` which never replayed on the 2nd+ contract (panel root stays mounted across opens; only `IsVisible` toggles). Converted to: rest state on `.radial-approach` (`opacity: 0; transform: <slot-offset> scale(0.4)`), active state on `.contract-radial.visible .radial-approach.<slot>` (`opacity: 1; transform: <slot-offset> scale(1); transition-delay: 0.02s..0.23s`). Two gotchas: (1) The two vertically-centered slots use `transform: translateY(-50%)`, so EVERY interactive state (`:hover`/`.selected`/`.dimmed`/`.cant-afford`) must RE-COMPOSE that offset (`translateY(-50%) scale(1.1)`, not bare `scale(1.1)`) or they snap out of vertical center — author the transform states per-slot under `.visible`, keep only position-INDEPENDENT paint (border/glow/color) on the base class. (2) Put `transition-delay` only on the visible-state rules; rest-state rules carry no delay so re-closing snaps back instantly without a reverse stagger fighting the next open. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ContractNegotiationPanel.razor.scss` T3 capture-celebration pass 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **A radial spark/particle burst where each mote travels a DIFFERENT direction needs one `@keyframes` PER mote — you cannot share one keyframe and vary direction via a static per-element `transform`.** An animating `transform` fully overrides any static `transform` on the same element for the animation's duration, so a shared `sparkPop` keyframe (scale+opacity only) plus per-spark `transform: translate(Xpx,Ypx)` makes all sparks scale in place at center with no travel. Fix: bake the travel vector into each spark's own keyframe (`@keyframes sparkPop0 { 0% { transform: translate(0,0) scale(0.3) } 100% { transform: translate(0,-92px) scale(1) } }`, etc.). 8 sparks = 8 tiny keyframes; verbose but correct. Stagger via per-element `animation-delay` for a breathing-outward ring. This is a fresh-mount context (the success result subtree mounts when `currentState` flips) so `@keyframes` ARE valid here — the "keyframes only fire on first mount" constraint is about RE-OPENED panels reusing the SAME element, not state-driven subtree swaps. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ContractNegotiationPanel.razor.scss` `.success-spark` / `sparkPop0-7` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **ExpeditionPanel "FLAT ON OPEN" is mostly an architecture mismatch — the live expedition entry is `WorldMapPanel` + `TeamPickerPopup`, NOT ExpeditionPanel.** `GameHUD.razor:69-81` mounts `WorldMapPanel` for the expedition tab whenever no run is active; `ExpeditionPanel` only mounts once `CurrentExpedition != null` (i.e. mid-run), and `SyncWithBackgroundExpedition()` then flips it straight to the `battle` view. Inside ExpeditionPanel: the `list`/`result` views are DEAD (`@if (false && ...)`), team-picking moved to the standalone `TeamPickerPopup`, and the post-run result is the global `ExpeditionResultPopup` (GameHUD child, separate file). So the only ExpeditionPanel-owned surfaces left are (a) the legacy `team` view (still styled/reachable via the dead list's GoToTeam + keyboard) and (b) the `battle` host. When a task says "juice ExpeditionPanel's open + clear" but you may only touch ExpeditionPanel's two files, the in-scope wins are: the `team`-view entrance cascade and a clear-celebration overlay the panel renders itself in `OnBattleComplete`'s full-clear branch (behind the out-of-scope result popup). Source: ExpeditionPanel juice pass 2026-06-03, traced via GameHUD + TeamPickerPopup + ExpeditionResultPopup.

- **2026-06-03:** **Render-keyed fresh-mount is the clean way to fire a one-shot celebration `@keyframes` that must REPLAY on every trigger.** ExpeditionPanel's "ZONE CLEARED" burst renders `@if (showClearCelebrate) { <div class="clear-celebrate" @key="clearCelebrateVersion"> ... }`. Bumping `clearCelebrateVersion++` before setting the flag forces a NEW DOM node each clear, and `@keyframes` fire on first mount of that fresh node — so the slam/ray/ring/banner keyframes replay every clear with zero transition two-step. Correct when the effect is a state-driven SUBTREE SWAP (mount→hold→unmount), vs a persistent re-openable PANEL reusing the same element (which needs transitions + a `.visible` class). Both patterns coexist in this one panel: team-view ENTRANCE = transitions+`.entered` (element persists across re-opens); clear BURST = keyframes+`@key` (element freshly minted each time). Source: `ExpeditionPanel.razor` `FireClearCelebrate()` + `.clear-celebrate` SCSS 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **A full-screen overlay added as a direct child of `.expedition-panel` is forced in-flow by the panel's own `> *:not(...)` rule — scope your override or it won't overlay.** ExpeditionPanel has `.expedition-panel > *:not(BattleView):not(.monsters-popup-overlay):not(.negotiation-overlay):not(.panel-header) { position: relative; z-index: 1; }`. A bare `.clear-celebrate { position: absolute }` (specificity 0,1,0) LOSES to that child-combinator rule and renders in-flow at z-index 1. Fix: scope as `.expedition-panel .clear-celebrate { position: absolute; ... }` to out-specify it. General rule: before adding an absolutely-positioned child to any panel, grep that panel's SCSS for a `> *` blanket position/z-index rule and beat its specificity. Source: ExpeditionPanel clear-celebration overlay 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **[WATCH] Replayable `.entered` transition staggers may still flicker on panels whose `BuildHash()` includes a per-frame value (`SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame`).** Per the 2026-04-10 flicker learning, a per-frame re-render can make s&box re-diff a conditional class (`@(teamViewEntered ? "entered" : "")`) and restart the CSS transition mid-reveal. ExpeditionPanel's hash includes `SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame`, so its team-view entrance cascade is theoretically exposed. Mitigations used: (1) `.entered` is a SINGLE bool flipped once 30ms after mount (not a per-stage C# counter), so the class string is stable once set; (2) the stagger is pure CSS `transition-delay`, not C# stages; (3) on a fresh team-view open nothing is hovered so GlobalFrame doesn't change the rendered subtree meaningfully during the ~0.5s reveal. 26.06.03's parser/diff rewrite + confirmed transition-replay success suggest this is fine now, but it's UNVERIFIED on this specific panel — if the entrance flickers in-editor, fall back to the proven C#-stage `revealStage >= N` recipe (2026-04-09 entry). Source: ExpeditionPanel team-view entrance pass 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **BeastiaryPanel (Beastbook) is a panel that REMOUNTS on every open — so mount-time `@keyframes` and `OnAfterTreeRender(firstTime)` fire each time the tab is activated.** `GameHUD.razor:87-88` renders `<BeastiaryPanel>` inside a `@switch (currentTab)` `case`, so switching away DELETES the panel and switching back MOUNTS A FRESH ONE (unlike ContractNegotiation which toggles `IsVisible` on a persistent element, or ExpeditionPanel whose root persists). Practical consequences: (1) grid-card entrance `@keyframes` (`bbCardFadeIn`) already replay on every open for free — no `.entered`/transition plumbing needed; (2) you can subscribe to manager events in `OnAfterTreeRender(firstTime)` and tear down in `OnDeleted()` — both fire reliably per open/close; (3) per-species detail entrances are best done with a render-key class (`detail-rev-{N}` bumped on species change) so the `.bb-detail-fixed` subtree remounts and its mount-time keyframes replay — no transition two-step. Don't assume "panel = persistent element" — check whether GameHUD mounts it conditionally vs toggles visibility. Source: BeastiaryPanel T3 discovery + entrance pass 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-08:** **For "angled UI" (3D recede) elements that stack with different WIDTHS but should read as the SAME plane and align their right edges, the wider element needs a SMALLER rotateY — roughly inversely proportional to width.** A `rotateY(θ)` around `transform-origin: left center` swings the right edge left + foreshortens it by an amount that scales with the element's width. So a full-width hero card at the SAME angle as a half-width card recedes its right edge ~twice as far → it sits further left (more receded) and reads as a steeper plane. The main-menu featured card (full width) was at 7deg matching the cards (half width); halving it to ~4deg lands the right edges flush and the slant consistent. Rule of thumb: `θ_wide ≈ θ_narrow × (width_narrow / width_wide)` — for a 2× wider element, halve the angle. Keep `perspective` and `transform-origin: left center` identical across both. CRITICAL: also update any cursor/ring that pivots to match the element's plane (here `CursorRot(7/8)` 7f→4f for the featured CTAs; card pills 9/10 stayed 7f). Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-featured` 7deg→4deg + `MainMenu.razor` `CursorRot` 2026-06-08.
- **2026-06-08:** **A `margin-left: auto` flex child (a right-pinned pill) gets PUSHED PAST an `overflow:hidden` parent's right edge when a sibling in the same row is non-shrinkable and the row's content-min-width exceeds the content box.** Main-menu Community card's row was `[icon][htxt][Join pill margin-left:auto]`; `.lh-card-htxt` had no `flex-shrink`/`min-width:0`, so its long "Join the Community" title forced the row wider than the card's content box and shoved the Join pill (and visually the divider below) past the right edge where `.lh-card { overflow:hidden }` clipped them. The short-title sibling card (Roadmap, "Roadmap") was fine. Fix: `.lh-card-htxt { flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0; }` so the title column absorbs the slack / shrinks instead of overflowing — pill stays inside the content box. Shared rule, zero harm to the short-title card (nothing to shrink). General rule: any flex row inside an `overflow:hidden` box that ends in a `margin-left:auto` element needs a `flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0` filler/text child so the auto-margin element can't be pushed out the clipped edge. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-card-htxt` 2026-06-08.
- **2026-06-03:** **A fullscreen celebration overlay added as a direct child of `<root class="beastbook-panel">` needs the `.beastbook-panel > .overlay-class` SPECIFICITY BOOST or the panel's `> * { position: relative; z-index: 1 }` blanket rule (BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:42) stomps `position: absolute` + the high z-index and renders it in-flow at the bottom.** Same failure mode + fix as ExpeditionPanel's `> *` rule (2026-06-03 entry) and the panel's own `.bb-art-editor-toolbar` / `.bb-art-drag-shield` (which already use `.beastbook-panel > ...`). General rule restated: before adding any absolutely-positioned child to a panel, grep that panel's SCSS for a `> *` blanket position/z-index rule and out-specify it. The discovery overlay (`.bb-discovery-overlay`, z-index 4000) is a SIBLING of `.view-container` and the beast-popup — correctly OUTSIDE the scroll grid, so its colored rings/rays/sparks don't leak past the scroll viewport. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss` `.beastbook-panel > .bb-discovery-overlay` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-09:** **Persona-pop on a cursor-tracked item: fold the CSS `.selected` transform displacement into the gliding cursor's TARGET RECT, not into its per-frame transform.** Main-menu sidebar items now lunge on selection (`.lh-item.selected { transform: ... translateX(9px) ... scale(1.04) }`), but CSS transforms don't move `Box.Rect`, so the imperatively-positioned `.lh-cursor` would wrap the un-popped layout rect and break the ring+gap concentricity. Fix: a single source-of-truth helper (`SelectedPop(i)` in `MainMenu.razor`) returns the (x, scale) the CSS applies per index; `UpdateCursor` derives the item's VISUAL rect from it (`visL = t.Left + popX; visW = t.Width * popS; visT` re-centers Y because `transform-origin: left center` pins the left edge) and computes left/top/w/h from that. Because the cursor glides via CSS transitions on left/top/width/height, the pop offset INHERITS the 0.27s glide for free — concentric mid-travel, no extra animation plumbing. Also remember the rotateY pivot: `pivotX` must reference the popped visual left edge (`pivotEl.Box.Rect.Left + popX`) or the ring tilts around a point 9px short. Since the cursor's target is by definition the selected item, the pop applies unconditionally per index — no "is selected" check needed. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` `SelectedPop`/`UpdateCursor` + `MainMenu.razor.scss` sidebar pop pass 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **When a transform must TRANSITION between base and a state class in s&box, author BOTH states with the same-SHAPED function list (identity values in the base).** Sidebar base went from `perspective(1300px) rotateY(13deg)` to `perspective(1300px) translateX(0px) rotateY(13deg) scale(1)` purely so the `.selected` pop (`translateX(9px) ... scale(1.04)`) interpolates per-function instead of risking a structure-mismatch snap between a 2-function and a 4-function list. Same trick as the ContractNegotiation "re-compose the positioning offset in every state" rule (2026-06-03), generalized: identity functions are free, structure mismatches are not. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-play`/`.lh-item` 2026-06-09.

## Feedback responses

_(What the user approved, rejected, or refined when reviewing agent proposals)_

- **2026-04-09:** **The proven s&box staircase reveal recipe (after 4 failed attempts):**
  ```csharp
  // C# side
  private int revealStage = 0;
  private async Task PlayRevealSequence() {
      revealStage = 0;
      await GameTask.DelaySeconds(0.12f);
      revealStage = 1;
      await GameTask.DelaySeconds(0.10f);
      revealStage = 2;
      // ... 100ms minimum between stages
  }
  // BuildHash must include revealStage
  ```
  ```razor
  <!-- Razor side -->
  <div class="my-element @(revealStage >= 1 ? "entered" : "")">
  ```
  ```scss
  /* SCSS side */
  .my-element {
      transition: opacity 0.25s ease-out;  /* PLAIN transition, no delay */
  }
  .my-element:not(.entered) {
      opacity: 0;
  }
  ```
  This recipe has been validated against s&box's CSS engine and works. Avoid: `transition-delay`, `:nth-child`/`:nth-of-type`, `animation forwards` for visibility, intervals < 100ms. Source: `DailyPanel.razor` `PlayStreakRevealSequence()` 2026-04-09 (final working version after 3 broken attempts).

- **2026-04-10:** **CRITICAL: `:not(.entered) → .entered` reveal pattern flickers when the panel's BuildHash re-hashes every frame.** Cause: when `BuildHash()` includes a value that increments per frame (like `SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame` for sprite animations), the panel re-renders every frame. Each re-render causes Razor to re-evaluate conditional class expressions like `@(stage >= N ? "entered" : "")`. Even if the class string is identical, s&box's diff appears to remove-and-re-add the class, restarting the CSS transition. Result: the section flickers on/off rapidly during what should be a smooth fade-in. **Symptoms:** "entered" sub-sections flicker visibly during the reveal; only ONE section reliably animates (the one whose stage flips during the right render-frame window).

  **Where this matters:** any panel that needs sprite-animated child elements (monster cards, idle frames, etc.) and ALSO wants a staged reveal of its UI sections. The two are incompatible with the standard `:not(.entered)` pattern because the per-frame re-render fights the staged class flip.

  **Workarounds:**
  1. **Use a single-stage reveal only** (parent slides in, children are always visible). This is what works in MonsterRosterPanel.
  2. **Remove the per-frame BuildHash addition** and re-render only when state actually changes. Risky — breaks sprite animations.
  3. **Drive the reveal from `Style.SetClass()` in C# code-behind** instead of Razor conditionals — once-only application means no per-frame re-evaluation. Untested but theoretically immune to the issue.

  Source: tested in MonsterRosterPanel 2026-04-10, sub-section staircase reveal failed twice with flicker. Reverted to single-stage. The Daily panel's reveal works because its BuildHash doesn't include a per-frame value. Reference: `MonsterRosterPanel.razor:3140` adds `SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame` to BuildHash for monster card sprite animation; same file's earlier `BuildHash` was where the conflict originated.

- **2026-04-10:** **CRITICAL TASTE / IDENTITY: There is a recognizable "Claude UI signature" that real players can spot.** Player feedback verbatim: *"Look at the borders. It looks so claude generated. Claude likes those types of borders and those colors."* The signature is composed of:
  - **2px semi-transparent borders** (`border: 2px solid rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.1)` or similar)
  - **Rounded corners on everything** (`border-radius: 12-16px`)
  - **Layered drop shadows** (`box-shadow: 0 4px 16px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5)`)
  - **Purple accents everywhere** (`#8b5cf6` / `#a78bfa` / `#c4b5fd`)
  - **Dark cards with low-alpha overlays** (`rgba(20, 20, 35, 0.95)`)
  - **Lucide vector icons** sprinkled as decoration

  This composition is the AI-default "polished modern web" look, NOT a pixel-art monster collector look. Used systematically across an entire UI, it becomes a visual fingerprint that says "AI generated this."

  **The fix is NOT to ban these properties** — they're fine in moderation and on a few elements. **The fix is to STOP applying them uniformly to every container as a reflex.** Real designers vary their visual register: some elements get borders, some don't. Some have shadows, some are flat. Some are purple, most aren't. The variance is what makes UI feel hand-built.

  **For Beastborne specifically (pixel-art monster collector), consider these alternatives:**
  - Hard 1-2px solid color borders (not rgba) using palette colors from the actual game art
  - Pixel-bevel effect (`border-top: 2px solid #lighter; border-bottom: 2px solid #darker`)
  - Sharp corners (radius 0-4px) on game-content elements
  - Solid color blocks instead of semi-transparent overlays
  - Reserve rounded corners + shadows + glow for moments that EARN them
  - Use colors from the existing game palette (element colors, monster sprite colors) not generic UI purples

  **Before adding ANY border/shadow/glow/radius to a new element, ask:** "Am I doing this because the element needs it, or because every other element has one?" If the answer is the latter, leave it off. Heterogeneity is more valuable than consistency for avoiding the AI tell.

  **Source:** real player feedback 2026-04-10 from "matt944" and "kellie" on Beastborne UI work done in this session. Multiple panels (DailyPanel, MonsterRosterPanel, multi-release confirmation) had been built with the signature pattern. The player feedback specifically called out borders + colors as the tell.

- **2026-04-13:** **Hover debounce for item-grid cards: use a `lastHoveredItemId` string guard.** Call `OnCardHover(itemId)` from `onmouseover`, check if `itemId == lastHoveredItemId`, early-return if so, else update and play sound. This prevents hover sound spam as the mouse traverses the item grid. The DailyPanel's `.mission-card` child `pointer-events: none` pattern is the complementary approach for mission cards (fewer items) — for large grids the ID guard is more appropriate. Also: add `> * { pointer-events: none; }` on the card root so child elements (icon-wrap, nameplate, stamps) don't re-fire `onmouseover` as the mouse crosses their boundaries. Source: InventoryPanel aliveness pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **s&box `::after`/`::before` pseudo-elements with `position: absolute` + `content: ""` DO work** — confirmed in BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss (scrolling pattern overlay) and ExpeditionPanel.razor.scss (toggle dot indicator). Safe to use for shimmer overlays, diagonal disabled patterns, and button sheen animations. NOT confirmed: `transform: scaleX()` on a pseudo-element — avoid this for critical visible features, use fallback border-bottom transitions instead. Source: InventoryPanel aliveness pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Razor file tab indentation causes Edit tool string matching to fail.** Files with deep Razor nesting (11-13 tabs + an extra space before `onclick=`) cause the Edit tool's old_string to not match even with correct tab counts. Use Python subprocess with exact byte string construction to do targeted replacements in deeply-nested Razor blocks. Source: InventoryPanel aliveness pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Full palette rollback from themed reskin: when a panel has drifted to a non-standard theme (e.g. leather/brass), the cleanest approach is a full SCSS rewrite, not surgical edits.** The InventoryPanel leather theme touched every component down to the buckle, stitching, tag, sidebar, and item cards. Trying to edit in-place meant finding ~40 individual color occurrences. Full rewrite is faster and produces cleaner output with no residue. Also: `#1a0c05` (near-black dark brown) left over in a "dark text on gold button" pattern looks like a leather artifact even when used for readable contrast — swap to `#120800` or true black to avoid the association. Source: Reliquary rebuild task 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Themed panel reskins use deliberately different palette from the game's purple/arcane baseline to give each tab navigational identity.** The InventoryPanel leather-satchel reskin uses a warm brown family (`#2a1810` / `#35200f` / `#1f120a`) to contrast against the purple panels (BeastiaryPanel, MonsterRosterPanel). The dithering approach: modal chrome is leather, item card interiors are only gently warmed (`#1a1208` instead of `#151522`), rarity colors remain exactly unchanged — they are gameplay vocabulary that must read consistently across all panels. Source: InventoryPanel leather reskin task 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Stitching effect on s&box: use an absolutely-positioned inset div, NOT border-style.** s&box doesn't support `border-style: dashed` or `dotted`. The double-border stitching trick: outer panel gets `border: 2px solid #darkcolor;` and an inner child div with `position: absolute; inset: 5px; border: 1px solid rgba(cream, 0.2); border-radius: slightlySmaller; pointer-events: none;` The inner div must be a sibling (inside the panel), not a child of a flex container, or it may collapse.
