Set `z-index: 10` on it to ensure it sits above background layers. Source: InventoryPanel leather reskin task 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **`::before` / `::after` pseudo-elements are unreliable in s&box.** When the weight strap rivet needed a purely decorative dot, it had to be implemented as a real `<div class="strap-rivet">` in Razor markup rather than `::before { content: ''; }`. Empty divs that have explicit `width`, `height`, `background`, and `position: absolute` DO render correctly — they're not empty and won't trigger the gray-artifact bug. Only truly contentless empty divs (no sizing, no background) are problematic. Source: InventoryPanel polish pass 2026-04-13. [PROMOTE]

- **2026-04-13:** **`box-shadow: X Y BLUR SPREAD COLOR inset` syntax is broken in s&box.** The `inset` keyword must NOT appear after the color — s&box doesn't parse this correctly. The pre-existing monster-picker-panel had `box-shadow: 0 2px 0 rgba(74, 47, 29, 0.3) inset;` which would cause a CSS parse error. Replace with a standard outer shadow. Source: InventoryPanel polish scan 2026-04-13. [PROMOTE]

- **2026-04-10:** **TASTE: `glow` (drop-shadow / box-shadow with colored alpha) is over-used as a "make it look polished" reflex. The user explicitly called this out as a tell that UI was AI-coded.** Glow should be a SIGNAL (this thing is active, this thing is the hero of the screen, this thing demands attention), not decoration. Sprinkling glow on every element flattens its meaning — when everything glows, nothing glows. **Rules of thumb:**
  1. **Hero/active elements only** — the selected card, the primary action button, the breathing portrait. Not every icon, button, or container.
  2. **Hover states are fine** — glow as an interaction signal ("you can click this") is meaningful. Idle glow is decoration.
  3. **Element identification icons, currency badges, level pills, small UI chrome** — should look clean, not glowing. Size + opacity + color does the job.
  4. **If you find yourself adding glow to "make it pop more"** — first try increasing size, contrast, or weight. Those are the legible alternatives.
  5. **The exception:** signature game-feel moments (the day-7 legendary reveal, a critical hit, a level-up) are appropriate places for big glow. Routine UI is not.

  Source: user feedback 2026-04-10 on the bulk release element icon glow halo (`filter: drop-shadow` with white alpha) — looked over-decorated and "AI-based." Removed it; the icons read fine at full opacity + 18px size alone.

- **2026-04-10:** **`calc()` for vertical positioning behaves unexpectedly in s&box.** Specifically `bottom: calc(100% + 8px)` on an absolutely-positioned tooltip placed the tooltip MUCH higher than the expected 8px above the parent — it appeared at the very top of a distant ancestor container instead. `calc(100% + Npx)` works for `width:` (e.g. ArenaPanel.razor.scss:1267 uses `width: calc(100% + 40px)` successfully) but not reliably for `bottom:`. **Use hardcoded pixel values for tooltip vertical positioning** instead of percentage-based calc. Source: tooltip placement experiment 2026-04-10 in MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss `.pill-tooltip`.

- **2026-04-10:** **CRITICAL: do NOT use `transform: scale(...)` on hover for cards in a dense grid.** Causes a hover-flicker loop: hovered card scales up → overlaps neighboring card → cursor is now technically over both cards → browser alternates which one is hovered → both cards flicker between hover and rest states forever. **Safe alternative:** `translateY(-Npx)` only + `box-shadow` + `border-color` for the lift effect. Vertical motion can't push the card sideways into its neighbor so no overlap can occur. Daily panel day-cards get away with `scale(1.18)` because they live in a horizontal `flex: 1` strip with explicit gap, but a dense vertical grid like the monster roster has cards too close together for any scale-up to be safe. Source: bug found in MonsterCard.razor.scss `&:hover` 2026-04-10, user reported flickering when hovering grid cards. Fix: remove the `scale(1.06)` from the hover transform, keep `translateY(-6px)` only.

- **2026-04-10:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **CRITICAL: hover sound spam from `onmouseover` bubbling — fix with `pointer-events: none` on children, NOT a state guard.** When a card has nested children (icons, text spans, progress bars, reward pips, etc.) and you attach `onmouseover=@(() => SoundManager.PlayHover())` to the parent card, the sound fires constantly as the mouse moves across child element boundaries because each child fires its own mouseover and they all bubble up. The user has reported this issue multiple times across different panels. **The fix is structural, not state-based:**
  ```scss
  .my-card {
      pointer-events: all;
      > * {
          pointer-events: none;
      }
      // Re-enable on any interactive child (claim buttons, etc):
      .claim-btn { pointer-events: all; }
  }
  ```
  This makes the parent card the only event target — child mouseovers don't fire at all, so the sound only plays once when the mouse crosses the card's outer boundary. **Always prefer this over a JavaScript-side state guard** (`if hoveredX != current { play; set; }`) because the structural fix has zero per-frame cost and doesn't require BuildHash plumbing.

  **Reference working pattern:** `.day-node` in DailyPanel.razor.scss has used this since the original implementation — that's why day-node hover never had the spam issue while mission cards did. **Apply this rule preemptively to ANY card-style UI element that plays a hover sound and has nested children.** Source: bug found and fixed 2026-04-10 on mission cards in DailyPanel; same root cause as previous similar reports across other panels.

- **2026-04-09:** `[OUTDATED — superseded by 2026-06-03 entry; transition-delay WORKS as of s&box 26.06.03]` **(historical) s&box did NOT support `transition-delay`.** Confirmed via runtime log: `[6] Didn't handle transition style: transition-delay`. This means staggered reveals via `transition-delay` (the standard CSS technique used in every browser) are impossible in s&box. Workarounds:
  1. **C# state increments** — the MainMenu `entranceStage` pattern, `await Task.Delay(...)` between flag flips, each element keys off `>= N`. **WARNING:** very short delays (<100ms) may be missed by s&box's render/Tick cadence — observed in DailyPanel where 40ms intervals between days resulted in only the final stage being visible. Use ≥120ms intervals to be safe.
  2. **`animation-delay` on keyframe animations** — s&box appears to support `animation-delay` since several files use it. BUT see the separate `animation forwards` learning — combining `opacity:0 + animation-delay + forwards` is unreliable. Test empirically before relying on this approach.
  3. **Multiple staggered class adds via separate state fields** — set `streakRevealStage1`, `streakRevealStage2`, etc. as separate booleans flipped at known-good intervals. More verbose but more predictable.
  Source: 2026-04-09 DailyPanel reveal attempt, runtime log.

- **2026-04-09:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **CRITICAL: s&box does NOT support `:nth-of-type()` (and likely `:nth-child()` as well).** Confirmed via runtime log: `Error parsing stylesheet: Unsupported Pseudo Class "nth-of-type(1)"`. **This parse error is catastrophic** — when s&box hits an unsupported selector, it appears to stop parsing the rest of the stylesheet, which is why DailyPanel stopped opening entirely after my staircase block introduced `:nth-of-type` selectors. **Symptom:** entire panel goes blank or stops rendering, not just the affected rule. **Workaround:** add explicit per-element classes via Razor (`<div class="my-section section-1">`, `section-2`, etc.) and target `.section-1`, `.section-2` in SCSS instead of `:nth-of-type`. This is more verbose but it's the proven project pattern (e.g. `.day-1`, `.day-2`, ..., `.day-7` in DailyPanel.razor.scss). **Verification needed:** test `:nth-child()` empirically — the previous agent claimed it was "verified used in GuildPanel" but that claim was hallucinated (zero matches in actual grep). Source: 2026-04-09 DailyPanel reveal attempt.

- **2026-04-09:** `[OUTDATED — see 2026-04-17 correction below]` s&box does NOT parse `radial-gradient` AT ALL. Verified by runtime logs across multiple forms:
  - `radial-gradient(circle at 40% 35%, ...)` → `Cannot read a color from 'circle at 40% 35%'`
  - `radial-gradient(ellipse at center, ...)` → `Cannot read a color from 'ellipse at center'`
  - `radial-gradient(ellipse, ...)` → `Cannot read a color from 'ellipse'`

  The "every form fails" conclusion was an overgeneralization from the failing forms actually tested. The default-centered `radial-gradient(circle, stop, stop, ...)` form DOES parse and render correctly — see production usage in `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.btn-swirl`, `.ring-glow` (all three elemental variants), confirmed running in-game. Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss` flyer styling + spotlight 2026-04-09, multiple runtime parse failures documented — but narrower than first stated.

- **2026-04-17:** `[OUTDATED — superseded by 2026-04-17 PM entry below]` Originally claimed bare `radial-gradient(circle, stop1, stop2, ...)` parses. Runtime retest that same day contradicts this — see next entry.

- **2026-04-17 PM:** `[PROMOTE]` **The `circle` shape keyword in `radial-gradient()` fails in s&box — drop the shape word entirely.** Runtime console on live build logs `[8] Cannot read a color from 'circle'` whenever the stylesheet declares `radial-gradient(circle, ...)`. The parser treats the bare `circle` keyword as a color token in that slot and rejects the whole gradient. This contradicts the earlier same-day learning that claimed the bare-circle form worked. Same failure mode as `ellipse`.

  **What WORKS:** omit the shape keyword entirely — `radial-gradient(stop1, stop2, ...)`. Default shape is ellipse; on a square-ish container (or when you size the element yourself) the visual result is indistinguishable from a circle. Confirmed working pattern in `Code/UI/Panels/SkillTreePanel.razor.scss:550-557` (the SkillTreePanel comment explicitly documents this failure mode and was an authoritative in-repo source the earlier entry should have caught).

  **What FAILS:**
  - `radial-gradient(circle, ...)` — parser rejects `circle`, logs "Cannot read a color from 'circle'".
  - `radial-gradient(circle at X% Y%, ...)` — still fails as before (`at X% Y%` qualifier).
  - `radial-gradient(ellipse, ...)` or `radial-gradient(ellipse at ..., ...)` — still fails as before.

  **Workaround for off-center blooms:** same element-sizing trick — position the element so its geometric center sits where the bloom should be, then paint a default `radial-gradient(...)` inside. Source: MainMenu bg wash fix 2026-04-17 PM, after runtime console showed 8x repeated `[8] Cannot read a color from 'circle'` errors. Nine `radial-gradient(circle, ...)` rules in `MainMenu.razor.scss` were converted to shape-less form. Also see existing working usage in `SkillTreePanel.razor.scss:553-557`.

- **2026-04-09:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **For staggered reveals: drive from C# state, NOT from CSS animation delays.** The proven working pattern is MainMenu's `entranceStage` recipe (`MainMenu.razor:370, 493-525`):
  1. C# field `private int revealStage = 0`
  2. `async void PlayRevealSequence()` that ticks `revealStage` up via `await Task.Delay(...)` between each step, calling `StateHasChanged()` after each increment
  3. Razor adds class via conditional: `@(revealStage >= N ? "entered" : "")`
  4. SCSS uses `transition` (NEVER `animation forwards`) on the property + `&.entered` flips to the visible state
  5. Hash-add `revealStage` to `BuildHash()` so the panel re-renders when stage changes

  The key win over `animation: ... forwards` is that **`transition` interpolates from the current computed state to the new state**, which s&box implements correctly. `animation` keyframes appear to have unreliable replay/completion behavior.

  Pattern is now used in `DailyPanel.razor` `PlayStreakRevealSequence()` / `PlayMissionsRevealSequence()` after the animation-based attempt failed twice. When you need to gate elements with existing transitions (like `.day-node` which has `transition: all 0.15s ease`), use `&:not(.entered)` for the hidden state instead of declaring a competing transition. Source: this conversation.

- **2026-04-09:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **CRITICAL: Never make UI visibility depend on a CSS animation completing in s&box.** Pattern that DOES NOT WORK:
  ```scss
  .element {
    opacity: 0;
    animation: fadeIn 0.3s ease-out 0.2s forwards;
  }
  ```
  In standard browsers this works because `animation-fill-mode: forwards` keeps the element at the final keyframe state. In s&box, *something* in this chain is unreliable — possibly `forwards` not sticking, possibly the animation never firing because the parent class-toggle doesn't replay, possibly delay-based animations starting wrong. The result: elements stay invisibly stuck at `opacity: 0` forever, and the panel renders with nothing in it. **Symptom:** panel background visible (or partially rendering), but text/widgets/cards completely missing. User reports "doesn't pop up properly" or "background showing but no content."

  **The rule:** **Base state of every element must be the visible state.** Animations are additive — they enhance an already-visible element, never reveal an invisible one. If you want a fade-in, do it via the parent's `transition: opacity 0.2s ease` on a class-toggle (the existing `DailyPanel { opacity: 0; &.visible { opacity: 1; } }` pattern at line 1-19 works perfectly because the `transition` interpolates from current state, not a fixed keyframe).

  **For staggered staircase reveals specifically:** there is currently NO known-working pattern in this project. The MainMenu uses class-toggle staging (`@(entranceStage >= 1 ? "entered" : "")`) — that's the closest working analogue. To do a staggered reveal in s&box, drive it from C# state changes that toggle classes, NOT from CSS animation delays on `opacity: 0` base elements. ProfilePanel uses the broken pattern too (`opacity:0 + animation forwards`) at lines 64-93 — it's worth verifying empirically whether ProfilePanel's staircase actually works in-game or whether it has the same silent failure.

  Source: 2026-04-09 DailyPanel Phase 1 reveal staircase. Two attempts to fix it (different selectors) both failed. Removing the `opacity: 0` base rules entirely was what fixed the panel — at the cost of the staircase animation not playing. Trade-off was correct: working panel without animation > broken panel with animation.

- **2026-04-13:** **HUD popup iconify audit pattern: keep `<img>` for three categories, swap everything else.** Category 1 — custom art: monster sprites, item sprites, pixel art icons from the DB, Steam avatars, tamer character art. These have no lucide equivalent and must stay as `<img>`. Category 2 — close button animated hover swap: `close.svg` + `close.webp` paired inside `.close-btn` is a deliberate CSS-only animated hover pattern (opacity 0/1 swap). Do NOT touch it. Category 3 — all other icons that map to lucide: bell, message-square, radio, sparkles, send, trophy, zap, target, rocket, trending-up, swords, map, info, triangle-alert, check-circle, volume-x, volume-1, volume-2. Use `<iconify icon="lucide:X" Size=@(N) Color="colorString" />`. For notification type-to-icon mapping, a `GetNotificationIconName(type)` + `GetNotificationIconColor(type)` pair of switch helpers in code-behind is the right pattern — keeps the razor template clean. Source: HUD popup audit 2026-04-13, NotificationPanel.razor + RadioWidget.razor.

- **2026-04-13:** **Rarity left-accent pattern for cards: absolutely-positioned div, NOT `border-left` shorthand.** s&box rejects `border-left: 3px solid rgba(...)` with "solid is not valid with border-style". The working pattern: add `position: relative` to the card, add `<div class="relic-accent @rarityClass"></div>` as first child in the card HTML, then style: `position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; bottom: 0; width: 3px; border-radius: 10px 0 0 10px;` with `background-color: <rarity-color>`. This gives crisp asymmetric left accents without any border-side quirks. Accent div must be `position: absolute` on a `position: relative` parent — no `overflow: hidden` on the parent (that collapses flex children). Source: ActiveEffectsPanel relic/boost card polish 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **CSS `@keyframes` entrance animations DO replay for `@foreach`-rendered lists.** When new items are added to a C# list and `StateHasChanged()` causes a Razor re-render, newly added `<div>` elements are truly mounted fresh — meaning `animation: myAnim 0.25s ease-out` on them will fire exactly once on mount, as expected. This is safe for chat messages, notification rows, and boost/relic cards. The s&box restriction is about class-toggle animations replaying (they don't — transitions are required for that). Keyframe-on-mount for fresh list items works correctly. Source: HUD popup entrance animation pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-09:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **s&box modal entrance pattern: use the element-name selector + `&.visible`, NOT a class-name selector on `<root>`.** s&box panels expose their root via the element name (the C# class — e.g. `DailyPanel`, `ProfilePanel`, `AchievementPanel`). The `.visible` class is added to that element when `IsVisible = true` and the SCSS pattern that works is:
  ```scss
  DailyPanel {
    opacity: 0;
    &.visible {
      opacity: 1;
      .child-element { opacity: 0; animation: fadeIn 0.3s ease-out 0.1s forwards; }
    }
  }
  ```
  Adding a class like `class="daily-panel-root visible"` to a child `<root>` div and then writing `.daily-panel-root.visible .descendant { ... }` does NOT work reliably for staggered children — the descendants get stuck at their base `opacity: 0` because the animation either fails to apply or `forwards` doesn't stick. Reference working pattern: `ProfilePanel.razor.scss:31-93`. Symptom of getting it wrong: panel renders (background visible) but all child content stays invisible. Source: bug introduced 2026-04-09 in DailyPanel staircase reveal block, fixed by switching to the element-name selector pattern. **When a panel uses both an element-name root selector AND a `<root class="...">` class on the inner div, ALWAYS use the element-name selector for state-driven animations — that's where `.visible` gets toggled by s&box's panel system.**
- **2026-04-09:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **s&box Razor scoping rule — `@{...}` block-scope is NOT shared with sibling `@foreach`/`@for` blocks.** Declaring a counter in `@{ int _idx = 0; }` and trying to mutate it from inside a sibling `@foreach (var x in list) { var i = _idx++; }` does NOT compile in s&box Razor. Each `@{...}` block compiles to its own scope; the counter is invisible to the next `@`-block. Symptom: the .razor file silently fails to compile, the panel object never instantiates, `Show()`/`Toggle()` flip `IsVisible` but nothing renders, and the user reports "click does nothing." Verified by: DailyPanel was the only file in the entire `Code/UI/` tree (1 of 25 using `@{...}`) attempting this counter pattern, and the panel didn't render until the pattern was replaced. **Correct pattern:** cache the list inside `@{...}` and use `@for (int i = 0; i < cached.Count; i++) { var item = cached[i]; ... }`. Both the cache and `@for` index are correctly scoped this way. The file's existing day-track loop at `DailyPanel.razor:63` already uses this pattern — when needing an index in a foreach, ALWAYS prefer `for` with a cached list. Source: bug at `DailyPanel.razor:207, 254` introduced 2026-04-09, fixed same day after user reported clicking the MISSIONS button did nothing.

## GameHUD currency roll-up — 2026-04-08

- **2026-04-08:** GameHUD `OnUpdate` already calls `SpriteAnimator.Update()` + `StateHasChanged()` every frame that sprites are animating, AND `BuildHash()` includes `SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame`. This means the HUD is effectively re-rendering per frame whenever anything in the scene is animating — any displayed value mutated during `OnUpdate` will flush to the template naturally. For the currency roll-up lerp this was free; no extra `StateHasChanged()` call needed. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor:431-434, 1333`.
- **2026-04-08:** Pattern: **lerp-toward-real-value via OnUpdate + float display state** works well in s&box Razor. Use `k = 7.49 / duration` (i.e. `-ln(0.05)/duration`) and `alpha = 1 - exp(-k * dt)` for an ease-out exponential lerp that closes ~95% of the gap over the target duration and is frame-rate independent. Snap when the remaining gap is < 0.5 to prevent fractional tails. This is the reusable pattern for any future "animate a discrete value" work (XP bars, stat gains, health, etc). Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor` `TickCurrencyAnimation()`.
- **2026-04-08:** Icon pulse via CSS class toggle (`.pulse-up` / `.pulse-down`) works — the C# side holds a timer, sets `_pulseDir != 0` while active, hash picks it up, Razor applies the class, the CSS `@keyframes animation: ...` fires once. When the timer expires, direction resets to 0, class is removed. Rapid repeat triggers within the animation duration will NOT retrigger (class is already applied) — acceptable for ambient feedback but note it for any future "streak counter" rapid-fire pulses where you'd need a version counter in the class name like `pulse-up-@pulseVersion`. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss` `.resource.pulse-up/down`.
- **2026-04-08:** `filter: drop-shadow(...)` with RGBA colors is widely used in the project (57 occurrences across 13 files) and works in s&box. Safe for icon glow effects. Source: grep across `Code/UI/`.
- **2026-04-08:** Always **initialize lerp state lazily to the real value on first frame**, not to zero. The GameHUD roll-up uses `_displayedGold = -1f` as a sentinel meaning "uninitialized, snap on first read/tick" — this prevents the awful "roll from 0 to 50000 gold every time you load a save" bug. Any future lerp state should follow this pattern. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor` `GetDisplayedGold()` / `TickCurrencyAnimation()`.
- **2026-04-08:** Currency counters in `GameHUD.razor` are three: Gold (`money.svg`), Contract Ink (`ink.svg`), Boss Tokens (`token.svg`). NO Gems display in the HUD top bar even though `TamerManager.CurrentTamer.Gems` exists and has a `GetGems()` helper. If Gems ever become a HUD currency, they'd need to be added to the same roll-up path. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor:99-131, 893-896`.
- **2026-04-08:** GameHUD `.currency-pill` lives in the top-right of the `.unified-top-bar`, padded `4px 12px`, dark background `rgba(15,12,28,0.85)`, thin purple border. It's a clean anchor for the upcoming DailyPanel particle fly-to-HUD animation — no `overflow: hidden` on the pill, so particles landing on the icons can escape the pill bounds cleanly.

- **2026-04-13:** **ChatPanel had `animation: panelPopIn 0.4s ease-out` on `.open` class + staggered `panelContentFadeIn` animations on children.** These don't replay on re-open because `@keyframes` only fires on first mount in s&box. Converted to the proven transition pattern: `opacity: 0; transform: scale(0.88); transition: opacity 0.22s ease-out, transform 0.28s cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)` on the popup, then `.open { opacity: 1; transform: scale(1); }`. Children are always visible — no per-child stagger, just the container pop-in. This ensures the open animation fires every time the chat is toggled. Source: chat/effects unification pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **ActiveEffectsPanel had no separate `.razor.scss` file** — the SCSS was presumably embedded or missing entirely. Created `ActiveEffectsPanel.razor.scss` as a new companion file. s&box picks up `.razor.scss` files automatically when they share the filename prefix with the `.razor` file. Source: chat/effects unification pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Tab switch handlers should early-return if the same tab is already selected, before playing sound.** Both `SetChannel()` in ChatPanel and `SetFilter()` in NotificationPanel now guard `if (currentChannel == channel) return;` before the `PlayTabSwitch()` call. Without this guard, clicking the already-active tab fires the sound and marks a state change on every click even though nothing actually changed. Apply this guard to any future tab/filter handler. Source: sound audit pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Incoming message ping in ChatPanel: use `RealTimeSince` for the debounce, NOT `DateTime.UtcNow` comparison.** `RealTimeSince` is the s&box idiomatic elapsed-time type — it auto-advances via the engine tick, requires no subtraction math, and can be compared directly to a float threshold (`if (_lastIncomingPing > 1.5f)`). Resetting is `_lastIncomingPing = 0`. Don't use `Task.Delay` for debounce in event handlers — it runs async and creates subtle ordering issues with `StateHasChanged()`. Source: ChatPanel incoming message ping, sound audit pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Toggle sounds (shuffle, repeat): read the RESULTING state to pick the right sound.** The pattern is: call the toggle method first, THEN read the state to decide `PlayToggleOn` vs `PlayToggleOff`. RadioWidget's `OnShuffleClick` now does `RadioManager.ToggleShuffle(); if (RadioManager.IsShuffle) PlayToggleOn(); else PlayToggleOff();`. This is safe because `RadioManager` is synchronous — the state is the new state by the time the next line executes. Source: RadioWidget sound audit pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Toast dismiss animation: add a `.dismissing` class for 220ms before actual removal.** Pattern: `_dismissingIds: HashSet<Guid>` in code-behind. `DismissNotification()` adds the id to the set, calls `StateHasChanged()` to apply the CSS class, then `await Task.Delay(220)` before calling `NotificationManager.Instance.RemoveNotification()`. The 220ms matches the `notifDismiss` keyframe duration. BuildHash must include `_dismissingIds.Count` and per-notification `_dismissingIds.Contains(id)` so the dismissing class actually renders. CSS keyframe: `notifDismiss` goes `opacity 1→0, translateX 0→20px`. Source: NotificationPanel animation pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Radio track-change text fade: use `_trackChanged: bool` + `Tick()` to detect the change, not a timer or BuildHash comparison.** Track `_lastKnownTrack` string in Tick, compare to `GetTrackName()` each frame, set `_trackChanged = true` when they differ, kick off a `ClearTrackChangedFlag()` async method to reset after 350ms. Wire `track-changed` class to the `.rp-np-info` container. CSS uses `@keyframes trackFadeIn { from { opacity: 0.2; } to { opacity: 1; } }` — a fade from 0.2 (not 0) so the text doesn't fully disappear on slow renders. Because `_trackChanged` is in BuildHash, the class flips correctly even though Tick runs outside the normal render cycle. Source: RadioWidget animation pass 2026-04-13.

## Scan learnings — 2026-04-09 (initial panel inventory)

- **2026-04-09:** Show/Close/Toggle pattern is nearly universal across modal panels — `public static bool IsVisible` + `SoundManager.PlayPopup/PlayPopdown`. Only `DailyPanel` uses the `popVersion++` counter to retrigger entrance animations on repeat-opens. This is the cleanest pattern for "play the open animation every time" and should be promoted to any panel where we want the modal to feel fresh each open (Profile, Shop, Achievement etc). Source: `DailyPanel.razor:338-346` vs `InventoryPanel.razor:385-388`. **[PROMOTE candidate]** — if the user wants a convention, `popVersion++` is the one.
- **2026-04-09:** Currency counters in `GameHUD.razor` top-bar are a static value-snap — no roll-up or pulse when amounts change. This is a project-wide flat moment that affects every reward claim. A Balatro-style roll-up on currency change would upgrade DailyPanel, ShopPanel, mission claims, and expedition rewards in one shared place. Source: `GameHUD.razor:1-40` skim. Implication: propose GameHUD-level counter animation as a shared dependency before per-panel claim juice.
- **2026-04-09:** `MenuPopup.razor:14-15` and `ShopPanel.razor:15` use bare emoji icons (⚔️, 🛒) in headers while the rest of the project uses `ui/icons/*.svg`. Minor consistency drift — worth cleanup when touching those panels.
- **2026-04-09:** `DailyPanel.razor.scss:671` — `.missions-content` has `padding-bottom: 200px`. This is almost certainly a workaround for the s&box scroll-height quirk (scroll containers miscalculating content height with nested flex children). Don't remove it without verifying scroll behavior. Source: direct file read. Related to CLAUDE.md "scroll containers need flat children" quirk.
- **2026-04-09:** `DailyPanel.razor.scss` has TWO `overflow: hidden` declarations on `.daily-modal` (line 39 AND 48). Redundant but harmless — noting in case the second one is a leftover from a stale edit. Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss:31-49`.

## DailyPanel Phase 1 — 2026-04-08 (coordinated claim + stagger reveal + mission card hierarchy)

- **2026-04-08:** **Deferred-manager-call pattern for coordinated claim sequences.** When you want a reward particle to visually "arrive" at a GameHUD counter and trigger its roll-up + pulse, defer the actual `Manager.Instance?.Claim()` call by the flyer's flight duration using `await GameTask.DelaySeconds( 0.45f )` inside an `async void` click handler. The GameHUD currency roll-up (lerps over ~400ms on real-value change) will then start exactly when the flyer crosses the top-right viewport corner, creating a visible cause-effect link. Source: `DailyPanel.razor:455-487` `ClaimDailyReward()`. Why it matters: the previous agent flagged this tradeoff — the deferred-call approach was cleaner than overlapping the roll-up with mid-flight, and `GameTask.DelaySeconds` is already a project idiom (see `MonsterRosterPanel.razor:2807`).

- **2026-04-08:** **Particle layer must be a SIBLING of `.daily-modal`, not a child.** `.daily-modal` has `overflow: hidden` which would clip any fly-to-HUD particles. The fix is to spawn `<div class="claim-particle-layer">` INSIDE `<root>` but OUTSIDE `.daily-modal`, with `position: absolute; top/left/width/height 100%; z-index: 1000; pointer-events: none`. This pattern generalizes: any panel that has `overflow: hidden` on its modal box but needs particles to escape (for fly-to-HUD, for celebration confetti) should use this sibling-layer pattern. Source: `DailyPanel.razor:340-357`, `DailyPanel.razor.scss` `.claim-particle-layer`.

- **2026-04-08:** **Fly-to-HUD via fixed translate works "well enough" without pixel-perfect coordinate measurement.** The DailyPanel reward flyers use `@keyframes flyToHudGold` with a hardcoded `translate(480px, -420px)` at 100% — aiming roughly at the top-right corner from the reward box center. This is sloppy in the sense that on wide ultra-wide monitors the flyer will under-shoot the HUD currency pill, but the player reads it as "it went that way" and the moment GameHUD's own `.pulse-up` fires on the value change, the brain stitches the two events into "the particle landed and the counter pulsed." Not worth measuring pixel-perfect positions with Razor C# layout queries (fragile in s&box). Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss` `@keyframes flyToHud*`.

## Chat / Notifications / ActiveEffects triple pass — 2026-04-13

- **2026-04-13:** **NotificationManager event subscription to AchievementManager must be done in `OnStart`, not `OnAwake`.** AchievementManager's `Instance` is set in its own `OnAwake` (same frame). Using `OnStart` (after all `OnAwake` calls complete) guarantees the instance is non-null when subscribing. Failure mode: subscribing in OnAwake silently no-ops if AchievementManager hasn't initialized yet. Source: NotificationManager.cs, triple-pass task 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Don't double-fire notifications from both manager call-site AND event subscription.** When AchievementManager called `NotificationManager.AddNotification(...)` directly AND fired `OnAchievementUnlocked`, and NotificationManager subscribed to that event and called `AddNotification` again, the result was two toast notifications for one achievement. The correct split: manager fires the event, notification system owns the toast entirely. Call-site in AchievementManager should have NO direct notification call. Source: AchievementManager.cs:670-675, triple-pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **`@keyframes expiring-border-pulse` on `.boost-card.expiring-soon` is correct** — the class is applied when `<60s` and stays applied, so the animation runs continuously. This is different from visibility-toggle `@keyframes` (which only play on first mount). The key rule: `@keyframes` is fine for always-on loop animations on a persistent element (breathing, warning pulse), but NOT for one-shot state reveals on re-shown elements (should be transitions). Source: ActiveEffectsPanel polish, 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **`GuildInvite` and `GuildMemberJoined` in `NotificationType` enum were dead values** — nothing in the codebase called `AddNotification` with these types. GuildManager has its own invite handling via `OnInviteReceived` event and GuildPanel handles them directly. Safe to remove without breakage (verified by grep). Future guild notification work should route through GuildManager's existing event, not a dead NotificationType. Source: NotificationManager.cs + GuildManager.cs grep, 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Trade channel pattern: global-visible `[Rpc.Broadcast]`, filtered in UI by `ChatMessageType.Trade`.** Guild chat is scoped server-side (only guild members get it, enforced in BroadcastGuildChat by checking `guild.Guild.Id`). Trade chat is different — everyone can see it (commodity market vibe), so the broadcast needs no scoping filter. The UI filter (`GetMessages()` returning only `.Trade` when on trade channel) keeps the log clean. Non-trade messages are still excluded from the trade channel view. Source: ChatManager.cs trade wiring, 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-08:** **Stagger reveal retrigger via `.daily-panel-root.visible` class gate.** To replay a staircase entrance animation every time a panel opens, gate the animation selectors under `.daily-panel-root.visible .daily-modal <child>`. When `Close()` removes `.visible` from the root and `Show()` re-adds it, s&box re-evaluates the child selectors and replays the CSS animations. This is cleaner than the `popVersion++` class-cycle pattern for child animations — the pop counter is still useful as a force-redraw trick on the modal itself, but children just need the `.visible` gate. The missions tab staircase similarly retriggers every time the user swaps into the tab because the `@if(activeTab=="missions")` block creates a fresh DOM subtree. Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss` "PHASE 1 - STAGGERED CONTENT REVEAL" block.

- **2026-04-08:** **`:nth-of-type` and `:nth-child` both work in s&box** and are used extensively (GuildPanel has 29 `animation-delay` instances, most keyed by nth-child). Safe to use for mission card section cascades. When sections can conditionally disappear (DailyPanel's monthly challenge is null-guarded), `:nth-of-type(N)` correctly skips absent siblings — verified by reading GuildPanel's pattern. Source: `GuildPanel.razor.scss:675-684`, `DailyPanel.razor.scss` missions reveal block.

- **2026-04-08:** **Sound layering works — no fancy mixer needed.** Stacking three SoundManager calls in sequence (`PlayClick()` + `PlaySuccess()` + `PlayGoldReward()`) inside the claim handler produces a satisfying layered cue without any special scheduling. The sounds fire on the same frame and the audio system mixes them. Pattern generalizes: for any Tier 2+ claim, layer click (initial tap) + success (finalize) + a reward-specific cue (coin/gem/xp). Source: `DailyPanel.razor:462-464`.

- **2026-04-08:** **Mission card state hierarchy = 3 new state classes** applied via Razor conditionals on `GetMissionPercent() >= 75f`. `.near-complete` gets amber tint border + subtle purple progress-fill pulse. `.completed` gets a green glow box-shadow + the inner claim button pulses 1.0→1.04 every 1.8s. `.claimed` (existing class, extended) now collapses to `max-height: 64px`, `scale(0.96)`, `opacity: 0.35`. Reads at a glance exactly like Marvel Snap daily missions. Source: `DailyPanel.razor.scss` "MISSION CARD STATE HIERARCHY" block.

- **2026-04-08:** **`async void` click handlers work fine in s&box Razor** for coordinated sequences — no special dispatch needed. Use `_ = SomeAsyncTask();` for fire-and-forget inside a non-async context. Source: `DailyPanel.razor:455`.

- **2026-04-10:** **`!important` is not valid on `opacity` in s&box.** Compiler logs `Generic 1 !important is not valid with opacity`. To override an opacity rule, use a more specific selector or `:not()` exclusion instead of `!important`. Likely affects other properties too — prefer specificity over `!important` everywhere in s&box CSS.

- **2026-04-11:** **`overflow: hidden` on a flex CHILD that is ALSO a flex container will silently collapse the entire panel** to zero width. CLAUDE.md mentions this rule but the symptom is brutal: the panel renders invisibly, no error, no warning. Hit it on `.result-panel-v2` which had `display: flex`, `width: 480px`, `flex-shrink: 0`, AND `overflow: hidden` to clip the rounded corners. The width and flex-shrink were ignored — the panel collapsed. Fix: drop `overflow: hidden`, accept that the children may slightly overlap the border-radius (they almost never do in practice). Compare against the working `.fusion-genes-card` pattern which has all the same properties except no overflow:hidden. **If a panel mysteriously vanishes, check overflow:hidden on flex children FIRST.**

---

## InventoryPanel polish pass — 2026-04-13

- **2026-04-13:** **`flex-wrap: wrap` inside a scroll container is the root cause of scroll height miscalculation in s&box.** InventoryPanel's `.items-scroll-area` used `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap` to arrange item cards in a grid. The scroll container couldn't calculate its content height correctly — items near the bottom would be clipped or the scroll thumb would be wrong. Fix: replace with explicit `.item-row` divs (one per N items, generated via `@for (rowStart = 0; ...)` in Razor), each row being `display: flex; flex-direction: row; gap: Npx`. The scroll container becomes `display: flex; flex-direction: column` with rows as direct children. This is the roster-grid pattern from CLAUDE.md and it resolves height calculation correctly. Applied the same fix to `.picker-grid` → `.picker-scroll` + `.picker-row`. Source: InventoryPanel.razor.scss, CLAUDE.md quirk.

- **2026-04-13:** **Relic slot "Empty" text alongside "+" is redundant.** The "+" alone is a sufficient empty-slot signifier — adding the word "Empty" makes the slot read as two labels competing. Dropped the `<span class="relic-slot-empty">` element; the "+" remains. Easier to scan at a glance. Source: InventoryPanel.razor:97-98.

- **2026-04-13:** **Equipped item cards need card-level visual treatment, not just a corner badge.** Original equipped state was `background-color: rgba(74, 222, 128, 0.06)` — a 6% green tint that is invisible in a dark panel. Fix: override the `.item-rarity-bar` to green + opacity 1 on equipped cards AND set a visible `border-color: rgba(34, 197, 94, 0.25)` + background `rgba(34, 197, 94, 0.08)`. Result: at a glance the left-edge bar reads green = equipped, no need to scan for the corner badge. Pattern: use the rarity bar as a semantic indicator, not just a rarity signal. Source: InventoryPanel.razor.scss `.item-card.equipped`.

- **2026-04-13:** **Sort buttons belong in the topbar, not the sidebar.** Sort affects the main item grid, so placing them next to the rarity pills is spatially logical. Moving them to the topbar frees the sidebar for categories + relic slots only, which improves the IA. The sidebar's sort bar had its own `border-top` separator, SCSS class `.sort-bar`, and localized sort-label that were all repurposed. New pattern: `.sort-bar-inline` sits inline in `.main-topbar` separated by a `border-left` divider from the rarity pills. Source: InventoryPanel.razor.scss.

- **2026-04-13:** **Unified toast pattern is cleaner than separate boostMessage + consumableMessage render paths.** Original code had inline `.boost-message` inside `.detail-actions` (shifts layout on appear) and a floating `.consumable-feedback` at position:absolute. Both now route through a single `toastMessage`/`toastSuccess`/`toastTimer` triple, rendered as `.inventory-toast` at `position: absolute; bottom: 120px`. Source: InventoryPanel.razor code section. Fewer fields in BuildHash, simpler Razor template, consistent visual for all item-use feedback.

### Phase 2 prep notes (Day 7 setpiece)

- **Day 7 detection in current code:** `GetTodayReward().IsDay7 == (GetStreakDayInCycle() == 7)` at `DailyPanel.razor:447`. Phase 1 sets `claimBurstIsMilestone = todayReward.IsDay7` in the claim sequence — Phase 2 should detect this BEFORE calling the routine claim path and instead launch a fullscreen setpiece that bypasses the routine particle/flyer burst.
- **Rotating silhouette DOM location:** `.legendary-sil` at `DailyPanel.razor:75` — rendered INSIDE the `.day-7` node, positioned absolutely at `top: 25px`, opacity 0.12, z-index 0. It rotates through 4 species via `RealTime.Now / 3f % 4` in `GetLegendarySilhouetteIndex()` (`DailyPanel.razor:412`). For Phase 2, the silhouette should be pulled out of the day node into a fullscreen setpiece layer when Day 7 claim fires — OR keep the day-node silhouette and have the setpiece spawn a NEW `.legendary-reveal` element at a higher z-index that resolves from silhouette → lit portrait via the HSR light-pillar + name-slam pattern (see ui-references/hsr.md and hades.md).
- **Headroom for escalation:** routine claim is now 12 gold particles + 1-3 flyers + 450ms flight + ~500ms linger. Day 7 setpiece should clearly dwarf this — target 3-6 second fullscreen sequence with a ramped anticipation phase, resolved reveal, and celebration phase. The current Tier 2 particle count is deliberately held to 12 (not 30-50) to leave room for Phase 2 to go bigger without feeling redundant.
- **Milestone purple particles:** `.claim-particle-layer.milestone` is already wired to tint p2/p5/p8/p11 purple for Day 7 / milestone claims — can be reused or overridden in Phase 2.
- **Reward flyer colors:** the `.fly-gold` / `.fly-ink` / `.fly-token` flyers each use a radial-gradient sphere with a matching box-shadow glow. If Phase 2 wants a new "legendary monster egg" flyer for Day 7, extend with `.fly-legendary` using the gold palette + larger size.
- **GameHUD pulse-up class timing:** fires once when the currency value changes, classes auto-clear. Does NOT retrigger if the same currency changes again within the pulse window. The Phase 1 claim stagger is safe because gold/ink/token are three different classes that won't collide. Phase 2's bigger payoff can count on pulse-up firing predictably.
- **`GetTotalUnclaimedCount()` static helper** at `DailyPanel.razor:511` already exposes the total unclaimed count for badge display — the GameHUD daily button badge pattern (Marvel Snap reference) can hook into this.

## Agent self-check before writing a new entry

Before adding to this file, ask:
1. Is this observation already captured (in this file, style-guide, css-quirks, feel-principles, or CLAUDE.md)?
2. Is the source concrete (file:line or specific user quote)?
3. Will this be useful in a future task, or is it ephemeral to this one?
4. If someone else read this in 3 months with no context, would they understand?

If any answer is "no", don't write the entry.

- **2026-04-13:** **Compartment-plus-nameplate card pattern for item grids.** Removing the outer card container and splitting cards into two distinct visual pieces (compartment = the icon pocket, nameplate = the colored label below) gives a dramatically more tactile, readable grid. Key structural rules: (1) `.item-card` is a transparent container — no background, no border; (2) `.item-compartment` is the strong square with rarity-tinted interior and leather border; (3) `.item-nameplate` below it uses rarity color as background, like a fabric label sewn on. This pattern is borrowed from Diablo's loot vocabulary. The rarity color on the nameplate telegraphs item tier at a glance across the whole grid. Source: InventoryPanel deep pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **`overflow-x: hidden` is safe on the scroll container.** The CLAUDE.md rule says `overflow: hidden` collapses flex children, but using `overflow-y: scroll; overflow-x: hidden` separately on a container that already has `flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0` does NOT collapse it. The collapse risk is `overflow: hidden` as a shorthand applied to a flex child that doesn't have an explicit size. Setting only overflow-x prevents the horizontal scrollbar without any collapse risk. Source: InventoryPanel scroll area fix 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **Hover scale on action buttons is safe (they're not in a dense grid).** The learnings file warns against `transform: scale(...)` on grid cards because of hover-flicker loops. Action buttons in the detail bar are isolated elements with no grid neighbors, so `transform: translateY(-2px)` lift on hover is safe there. The grid-card rule only applies to `.item-card` and similar elements that share a row with peer cards at low gap. Source: InventoryPanel button audit 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **HUD popup header spec (Chat / Radio / Notifications / ActiveEffects) — all four must be pixel-identical.** Locked values: `height: 60px`, `padding: 0 18px`, `gap: 12px` on both left/right groupings, title `18px/800/2.5px/uppercase`, icon `Size=@(22)`, close-btn `background-color: rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` + `border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` + `:active scale(0.93)` + icon 14px. Radio `.rp-hdr` needed a new `.rp-hdr-left` wrapper in both razor and scss because the header was flat-siblings (icon + title + close) rather than grouped (left-group + close). All four tab bars locked to `12px/700/1px letter-spacing/uppercase/0.55` inactive color. Source: precision alignment pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **HUD popup body vocabulary — locked across Chat / Radio / Notifications / ActiveEffects.** Scroll container: `padding: 14px 14px 18px; gap: 8px`. Card base: `bg rgba(20,20,35,0.85); border rgba(255,255,255,0.08) 1px; radius 10px; padding 12px 14px; gap 12px; hover bg rgba(30,28,50,0.9) border rgba(139,92,246,0.3)`. Text: primary `13px/700/#fff`, secondary `12px/500/0.70alpha`, meta `11px/600/0.45alpha`. Icon containers: `40×40px; radius 8px; bg rgba(255,255,255,0.04); border rgba(255,255,255,0.08)`. Count pills: `padding 3px 9px; radius 10px; bg rgba(139,92,246,0.18); border rgba(139,92,246,0.35); 10px/800/0.5px/#c4b5fd`. Action buttons: `padding 6px 14px; 11px/700/1px/uppercase; bg rgba(139,92,246,0.12); border rgba(139,92,246,0.3); radius 8px; #c4b5fd`. Dividers: `1px; bg rgba(139,92,246,0.18); margin 4px 0`. Section labels: `11px/800/2px/uppercase/#c4b5fd; padding 4px 2px 8px`. System/achievement rows in chat get `bg rgba(139,92,246,0.08); border rgba(139,92,246,0.22)` as the purple-tint exception. Effects cards switched expiring-soon from red (`ef4444`) to amber (`fbbf24/0.5`) to keep the palette arcane. Source: full body unification pass 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-13:** **HUD popup height lock and final residual diffs (pass 4).** After three prior passes the panels still looked different due to: (1) NotificationPanel `height: 500px` vs the 560px target — bumped to 560px; (2) `.nf-pill` tab padding was `10px 14px` vs chat's `10px 16px` — corrected; (3) `.ns` (notification subtitle) `line-height: 15` vs chat's `line-height: 18` — corrected; (4) `.notif-list` missing `position: relative` that chat-messages has; (5) empty states had no `animation: emptyFloat` and used non-standard child class names — rebuilt to unified vocab with `.empty-headline` / `.empty-hint` / `iconify` targeting. Effects panel `.ep-empty-full` used `flex: 1` instead of `flex: 1 1 0`. Lesson: residual diffs hide in secondary text properties (line-height), secondary padding (tabs), and structural shorthands (flex). Source: template clone pass 4, 2026-04-13.

- **2026-04-14:** **Stat-bar transitions — delete the Task.Delay `statsResetting` hack.** Previous BeastiaryPanel code reset stat-fills to 0%, StateHasChanged, awaited 50ms, then restored to animate width on species change. This hack is unnecessary: s&box CSS handles `transition: width 0.3s ease-out` fine on `style="width: Xpx"`. Computing the fill width as an int in code (`(pct * trackPx) / 100`) and letting CSS transition re-fire on render gives smooth bar animation on selection change without any `async`/`Delay`/extra `StateHasChanged`. Source: BeastiaryPanel → Beastbook rewrite, 2026-04-14. Pattern: see `GetStatFillPx()` + `.bb-stat-fill { transition: width 0.3s ease-out; }`.

- **2026-04-14:** **Rarity accent strip pattern for cards.** Per-side border shorthand with style keyword (`border-left: 3px solid rgba(...)`) is unsupported in s&box per CLAUDE.md. The clean pattern for a colored left-edge strip on a grid card is a nested absolutely-positioned div: `.card-rarity-strip { position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; bottom: 0; width: 3px; background-color: ...; }` with the card `position: relative; overflow: hidden`. The card's own `border: 1px solid ...` handles the rest of the frame. Use this for any card that needs asymmetric colored accents — inventory items, beast cards, mission cards. Source: BeastiaryPanel/Beastbook rewrite, 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Hover-sound guard pattern for grid cards (s&box `onmouseover` fires repeatedly).** `onmouseenter` is unsupported — `onmouseover` fires every frame while the cursor stays on an element. For a grid of clickable cards, store `private string lastHoveredCardId` and gate the sound: `if (lastHoveredCardId != id) { lastHoveredCardId = id; SoundManager.PlayHover(); }`. Don't set state-hashed fields from the guard — just play the sound and return. See `BeastiaryPanel.OnCardHover()` for the canonical implementation. Source: Beastbook rewrite, 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Tooltip on hover inside a grid/scroll container — use a positioned child div with `:hover` parent.** s&box does NOT render native `title="..."` attributes as tooltips. For trait chips / card hover hints, use `position: absolute; bottom: Npx; opacity: 0;` on a child div and override to `opacity: 1;` inside `&:hover`. Add `pointer-events: none` on the tooltip so it doesn't intercept hover. `transition: opacity 0.15s ease, transform 0.15s ease` so it fades in. Works inside scroll containers as long as the chip's `position: relative`. Source: Beastbook trait chips, 2026-04-14. Risk: if the parent has `overflow: hidden`, the tooltip clips — in that case, hoist the tooltip to a sibling of the scroll parent and compute position in code.

- **2026-04-14:** **MonsterRosterPanel is the canonical layout reference for ALL collection-style panels in Beastborne.** When building or revising a panel that shows a filterable grid + selected-item detail (Beastbook, Inventory, Marketplace, etc.), mirror the roster silhouette: full-screen panel with `background-image: url(ui/menus/animated-bg.webp)` directly on the root, floating filter row header (not a 60px bar — just `.roster-header` with `rgba(8,6,18,0.5)` + purple border-bottom 0.1 alpha), `.view-container` → `.collection-layout.with-detail` → `.collection-main` (grid) + `.detail-sidebar` (~400px wide, `margin-left: -20px; padding-left: 28px` so it overlaps the grid container). Grid is `flex-wrap: wrap; justify-content: space-evenly; overflow-y: scroll` with direct 150-180px card children. Do NOT wrap in a `.beastbook-popup` / floating 1440x880 modal — that's the HUD-popup vocabulary (Chat/Radio/Notifications), reserved for transient overlays. Collection pages are full-screen. Source: Beastbook correction pass 2026-04-14 where a previous pass had used the HUD-popup chrome by mistake.

- **2026-04-14:** [OUTDATED — filter chaining breaks in s&box, see 2026-04-14 polish pass entry below] **Undiscovered beast silhouette filter = `brightness(0) opacity(0.55)`, NOT `brightness(0) saturate(0)`.** The latter renders a dark grayscale that still leaks color at the edges. For a pure-black pokedex-style silhouette of a pixel-art sprite, `brightness(0)` alone forces every opaque pixel to black, then `opacity(0.55)` softens the hard black so the form reads as "mystery" rather than "missing asset." Keep animation playing on the silhouette — animated shadows are free juice and signal "there's a creature here to find." Apply the same filter in both grid cards and detail portraits for consistency. Source: Beastbook fix pass 2026-04-14, user explicitly said "i still see the sprites in this view not blacked out of the ones i havent seen yet."

- **2026-04-14:** [PROMOTE] **s&box CSS `filter:` property accepts ONLY a single function — chaining is a parse error.** `filter: brightness(0) opacity(0.55)` logs `Generic brightness(0) opacity(0.55) is not valid with filter` and the entire declaration is dropped (the sprite renders as-if no filter at all, leaking the full-color pixel art for undiscovered beasts). Same for `grayscale(1) brightness(0.85)` and any other multi-function chain. **Fix:** pick the single most-important filter function (`brightness`, `grayscale`, `blur`, `hue-rotate`, etc.) and apply sibling properties (`opacity`, `color` overlays, `background-color` dim layers) for the rest. Beastbook silhouettes now use `filter: brightness(0.02); opacity: 0.72;` — near-black retains a hint of form ("shape in mist") and the separate opacity softens the hard edge. Source: compile errors from BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:318,328,590 during deep polish pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Rarity "wash" tint pattern for grid cards — nested absolutely-positioned gradient div, not the card background.** To subtly tint a whole card in its rarity color without flooding it, add an inner `<div class="card-rarity-wash">` with `position: absolute; top/left/right/bottom: 0; pointer-events: none;` and apply `background: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(colorR, colorG, colorB, 0.10-0.12) 0%, rgba(...0) 70%);` only for epic+ rarities. Common/uncommon stay plain to avoid noise — only the "meaningful" rarities pick up the wash so epic/legendary/mythic genuinely pop at grid density. Rule: wash opacity stays ≤ 0.12 or it starts competing with the card content. The rarity accent strip on the left still provides the primary at-a-glance cue; the wash is atmospheric reinforcement. Source: Beastbook deep polish pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Detail-pane title hierarchy: 26px/800 weight uppercase with a subtle text-shadow beats 20px/800.** First draft of the Beastbook detail pane had the name at 20px — too close to section body text (13-14px) to register as "the title of this page." Bumping to 26px with `letter-spacing: 0.8px` and `text-shadow: 0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.6)` makes the name land as the primary read target when the pane opens. The portrait remains the hero image (144×144, 3px rarity-colored border, 8px drop shadow) but the eye travels portrait → name → type pills → body — a real hierarchy instead of a soup. Rule of thumb for detail panes: title should be 2x the body font-size, minimum. Source: Beastbook deep polish pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Grid card portrait zoom pattern — scale the sprite inside an `overflow: hidden` rectangle.** When pixel-art sprites float in negative space inside a card, they look detached and weak. Fix: make the portrait container fill the card width (e.g. 128×110 on a 150×180 card), set `overflow: hidden`, and apply `transform: scale(1.35)` to the sprite. The sprite clips cleanly to the rounded rectangle and the beast becomes the hero of the card. Bump to `scale(1.42)` on card hover with a `transition: transform 0.2s ease` for a subtle zoom-in juice. Don't use `object-fit: cover` — pixel art needs `image-rendering: pixelated` + explicit transform. Source: Beastbook fix pass 2026-04-14, user feedback "the boxes for each beast feel a bit odd. maybe we can make them fit the rectangles and zoom in on them?"

- **2026-04-14:** **Toggleable section reveal via diamond button.** For detail panels where a section (e.g. moves list) bloats the view but isn't always needed, gate it behind a diamond in the action column with a count badge. Pattern: third diamond next to Cry + AI-warn, `onclick` toggles a `private bool showMoves` field, an `.active` class shows a glow + brighter bg, and the inline section is wrapped in `@if (showMoves)`. Always add the bool to `BuildHash()`, and reset it to `false` when the selected item changes so every new beast starts with a clean short view. Add a small badge child `<div class="bb-diamond-badge"><span>@count</span></div>` absolutely-positioned at top-right of the diamond wrapper (NOT the rotated inner shape — the badge must not rotate). Source: Beastbook fix pass 2026-04-14, user said "maybe the moves is a separate diamond."

- **2026-04-14:** **Diamond container = Beastborne's canonical button shape.** Pattern: `.detail-diamond` wrapper (column flex, `cursor: pointer`, `transition: transform`) holding a `.diamond-shape` inner div rotated `transform: rotate(45deg)` with 6px border-radius and 2px border, and an `iconify` (or `<img>`) child counter-rotated `transform: rotate(-45deg)`. For vertical stacks next to a portrait use `.portrait-actions` (`flex-direction: column; gap: 14-18px`). For list rows (moves, actions) use the overlapping pattern: `.action-v2 { padding-left: 32px; margin-left: 18-20px; position: relative }` with `.action-v2-diamond { position: absolute; left: -26px; top: 50%; transform: rotate(45deg) translateY(-70%) }`. Standard sizes: 32px for compact, 36px for detail-column, 30px for overlapping list. Element-color gradients on the diamond bg + hover box-shadow glow matching the border color are the standard treatment. No shared `.diamond-btn` class exists — each panel defines its own. Reference: `MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss` `.detail-diamond`/`.action-v2-diamond`/`.move-diamond-icon`. Source: Beastbook correction pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** [PROMOTE] **s&box CSS `scrollbar-color` and `scrollbar-width` are non-functional — remove them.** `scrollbar-color: rgba(...) rgba(...)` throws `Generic rgba(..) rgba(..) is not valid with scrollbar-color` (two-value syntax unsupported). `scrollbar-width: 6px` throws `Generic 6px is not valid with scrollbar-width` (only keyword values `auto`/`thin`/`none` accepted, and those don't actually style the scrollbar either). Both properties should be deleted entirely from SCSS — the default s&box scrollbar rendering is the only option. Do NOT try `thin` as a fallback; it still doesn't produce the tame violet chrome you'd expect on the web. Source: BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:555-557 live error report 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Pixel-art sprite hover juice — use `transform: translateY(-Npx)`, NOT `transform: scale(1.0X)`.** Even with `image-rendering: pixelated`, fractional-scale transforms smear pixel boundaries on scale-up in s&box. A hover scale of 1.06 on a 128×128 pixel sprite produces visibly blurry edges. Use a translate bounce (`translateY(-2px)` or `translateY(-3px)`) instead — zero resampling, same "lift on hover" feel. Only use integer scales (2x, 3x) if you must scale pixel art; fractional scales always degrade. Source: user feedback "the sprite loses quality in the detailed view sometimes" on BeastiaryPanel detail portrait, 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Unified pill pattern — one `.bb-pill` base + variant modifiers, not N different chip classes.** When a detail pane shows multiple "chip" style elements (rarity badge, stat total, element tags, personality, traits) the temptation is to style each separately. Result: 5 visually different components, all "pills" in name only — user says "they look so different, make them similar." Fix: define ONE `.bb-pill` class with the base spec (`height: 22px; padding: 4px 10px; border-radius: 6px; border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.1); bg rgba(255,255,255,0.04); font-size: 10px; letter-spacing: 1.2px; font-weight: 800;`) and add variant classes for color only: `.rarity-epic`, `.element-fire`, `.stat`, `.personality`, `.trait`. Apply `.bb-pill` to every chip in razor. Now they read as one component family — only color differs. Nested selectors for sub-spans in stat variant (`.bb-pill.stat .bb-pill-label`) have higher specificity than the base `.bb-pill span`, so they override cleanly. Source: BeastiaryPanel detail pane polish, user feedback 2026-04-14. [PROMOTE candidate — this is a reusable pattern for ALL detail panes in Beastborne, not just Beastbook.]

- **2026-04-14:** **Diamond action row placement — BELOW the identity block, not to the side.** When the identity block contains a long beast name (e.g. "Terrabramble") and the diamond action column sits to the RIGHT of it in a flex row, the name squeezes into too narrow a space and wraps onto two lines. Fix: make the diamonds a horizontal row as a SIBLING of the portrait-section, positioned between the identity block and the flavor description. The name now has the full sidebar width and reads as a single clean title. Bonus: a horizontal row of 4-5 diamonds looks like a "hotbar" of actions (cry, favorite, moves, evolution, lore) — feels more like a character sheet than a button column. Source: BeastiaryPanel detail pane polish, user feedback "the name wraps weirdly because of the diamonds, maybe diamonds move below?" 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** [PROMOTE] **`border-left: 3px solid rgba(...)` DOES work in s&box — CLAUDE.md quirk table is wrong.** The quirks table in CLAUDE.md says per-side border shorthand with the `style` keyword (e.g. `border-left: 3px solid rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.5)`) is rejected by the parser. Empirically this is FALSE: `MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss:4298` has exactly this form on `.section-title` and it renders correctly as the live purple accent bar on every section header in the roster detail pane. The quirk may have been specific to a different context (per-side with non-uniform color, maybe `border-left-color` combined with shorthand?), or the s&box parser fix was made after the rule was written. When porting accent bars from roster to another panel (e.g. Beastbook), use the same shorthand directly — don't reach for a nested absolutely-positioned div as a workaround. Source: Beastbook → roster parity pass 2026-04-14, comparing CLAUDE.md quirks to live roster SCSS.

- **2026-04-14:** **Roster-style `.section-title` spec for "siblings" feel across panels.** When a new panel needs to feel like a sibling of `MonsterRosterPanel`'s detail pane, port these exact values on the shared section title class — do not invent new ones: `font-size: 13px; font-weight: 700; color: rgba(255,255,255,0.45); letter-spacing: 1.5px; text-transform: uppercase; margin-bottom: 10px; padding-bottom: 6px; padding-left: 10px; border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.1); border-left: 3px solid rgba(139,92,246,0.5); width: 100%;`. The left purple accent bar + bottom hairline + 10px indent is the Beastborne "framed section header" fingerprint. Pair with: section card having `background-color: rgba(0,0,0,0.2); border-radius: 10px; padding: 12px; flex-direction: column`. Every roster detail section (ACTIONS, STATS, INFO, MOVES, TRAITS) uses this — Beastbook now does too. When converting a section-title to flex (to host an inline count badge), wrap every text child in a `<span>` — bare text inside flex renders vertically in s&box. Source: Beastbook → roster parity pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Roster identity badge spec: filled gradient + dark label/value pair.** Roster's identity block uses two complementary badge shapes on the level-power row: `.level-badge` is a filled purple gradient `linear-gradient(135deg, #a78bfa 0%, #8b5cf6 100%)` with 4px 12px padding, white bold 12px text, 6px radius. `.power-badge` is the dark complement: `background-color: rgba(0,0,0,0.4)`, 1px rgba(255,255,255,0.08) border, same padding/radius, with a dim 12px label `rgba(255,255,255,0.5)` + white 16px value inline. This pairing — one filled, one dark — is the Beastborne "stat badges" pattern. Beastbook reuses the exact spec for `.bb-rarity-badge` (filled, gradient per rarity tier) + `.bb-bst-badge` (dark + label BST + big value). Rule: any panel showing two identity-level stats should pair a filled colored badge with a dark label/value badge — never two matching shapes. Source: MonsterRosterPanel.razor.scss:4121-4154 analyzed for Beastbook port 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Collection grids: uniform dark card bg beats per-element gradient floods.** First pass of Beastbook grid cards used a full-bleed element-color gradient on every card — result was a visually chaotic "rainbow wall" that's impossible to scan. Fix: every card gets the same flat `#0c0a18` background, with a SUBTLE bottom-up element hint (absolute gradient layer, `linear-gradient(0deg, rgba(element, 0.14) 0%, rgba(element, 0) 55%)`) as a whisper of type. Element icon(s) live inline in the bottom name strip (14×14, primary + optional secondary). Rarity moves to the border: 1px for common/uncommon/rare, 2px for epic/legendary/mythic — thicker borders on top tiers naturally "ring" the card without glow halos. Add a 6px corner dot pip just above the bottom strip as a reinforcing secondary rarity cue. NEVER use the `transparent` keyword inside `linear-gradient()` in s&box — it's rejected. Use `rgba(R, G, B, 0)` explicitly. Source: Beastbook grid calm-down pass, user feedback "gradients should be from the bottom up and it looks visually distracting, maybe background should be the same color for all" 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Card number pill — port roster's `.mini-lv-pill` for collection "#003" badges.** When a collection panel (Beastbook, etc) needs a small number badge on each card, use roster's mini-lv-pill vocabulary verbatim: `background-color: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.85); border: 1px solid rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.12); border-radius: 4px; padding: 3px 7-8px;` with an inner `<span>` at `font-size: 11px; font-weight: 800; color: #ffffff; letter-spacing: 0.3px; line-height: 10;`. Place at `position: absolute; top: 6px; left: 6px;` on the card. The same spec is used inline in the detail-pane identity block right before the beast name — visual continuity: the player sees "#003" in the same typography on the card AND next to the big title. Source: user feedback "should we do it like the lv pill we have for my beasts" referencing the roster LV100 pill, 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Long-name wrap fix: split rarity/BST off the name row.** If a detail-pane identity block has `name + rarity + BST` all on one flex row next to a portrait, long names (HOLLOWGALE, TERRABRAMBLE) run out of horizontal space and wrap/letter-space-collapse. Fix: split the identity into TWO rows — row 1 is `#num pill + name + element pills` (name has `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0; white-space: nowrap;`), row 2 is `rarity badge + BST badge`. Gives the name the full identity-block width to breathe. Drop the name font-size one step (e.g. 22 → 20px) as belt-and-braces insurance. NEVER add `overflow: hidden` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` to the name span — the s&box quirks table warns this can collapse the element in flex layouts. Relying on the 20px + nowrap is enough for all current beast names. Source: user feedback "still some text issue, HOLLOWGAL E on two lines" on Beastbook detail pane 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Swappable detail sections via state string, not multiple booleans.** When a detail pane has diamond action buttons that toggle different content sections (stats, moves, evolution, lore), use a single `string detailView = "stats"` state instead of separate `bool showMoves`, `bool showLore` etc. Clicking a diamond calls `OnDiamondClick(view)` which toggles: if already showing that view, snap back to "stats"; otherwise switch to the new view. Add the state string to `BuildHash()` and reset to "stats" on species change. The `.active` class on each diamond (`detailView == "moves" ? "active" : ""`) gives clear visual feedback about which section is expanded. This avoids conflicts (two sections open simultaneously) and simplifies the state machine. Source: user feedback "for the diamonds i want them to be the popups or have the bottom part of the panel transform" on Beastbook 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Rarity strip at bottom > rarity badge in header for collection grids.** For dense grid cards, a thin 2px absolute-positioned rarity-colored strip at the card bottom (`left: 8px; right: 8px; bottom: 0;`) communicates rarity without visual noise. It replaces the header-corner rarity label+pip that competed with the number pill and portrait. The strip is hidden for undiscovered beasts (razor guard). SCSS: use simple `background-color` per rarity class, `opacity: 0.6` for subtlety, `z-index: 3` to sit above the gradient floor. Source: user feedback "the rarity should be the strip from my beast, not the rarities in the top right" on Beastbook cards 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Detail pane split: fixed top (portrait + identity + description) above a scrollable body.** When a detail pane has swappable bottom views (stats/moves/evolution/lore) driven by diamond actions, pinning the hero read (portrait, name, element pills, rarity/BST badges, flavor description) in a non-scrolling `.bb-detail-fixed` above the scrollable `.bb-detail-scroll` keeps the beast + its identity stable as the player flips through views. Pattern: `.detail-sidebar { display: flex; flex-direction: column; overflow: hidden }` with two children — `.bb-detail-fixed { flex-shrink: 0; border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.06) }` and `.bb-detail-scroll { flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow-y: scroll }`. Crucially, the description belongs in the fixed top (it's core identity, not view-specific content). Source: user feedback "many things feel out of order, like the description should be higher up" on Beastbook detail pane 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Portrait frame should be a calm neutral, not a rarity/element signal.** Beastbook detail portrait originally had `border-color` variants per rarity (green/blue/purple/gold/pink strokes) + a `.bb-portrait-tint` div with low-opacity element color behind the sprite. User feedback: "i don't like the rarity stroke and element color behind the beast." Fix: strip both — use a clean dark frame (`background-color: rgba(10, 8, 20, 0.92); border: 1.5px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.1); border-radius: 12px`) with a soft drop shadow for depth. Rarity reads from the identity rarity pill (solid colored pill next to BST), element reads from the inline element pill next to the name. This keeps the beast itself the hero instead of making the frame compete for attention. Rule: the frame holds the image, it doesn't classify it. Source: BeastiaryPanel detail pane polish 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Personality + traits belong INSIDE the stats view, not in the fixed top.** First pass of the Beastbook detail split had personality + traits as always-visible chips in the fixed top (between mastery and swappable view). Problem: when the player flips to moves/evolution/lore the chips clutter the fixed area. Better placement: inside the stats view (`@if (detailView == "stats")`), under the stats bars + BST total row. Reason: personality and traits are *identity metadata that reads alongside raw numbers* — they belong in the "stats" category. Moves/evolution/lore views don't need them. Bonus: the stats view now reads as a complete "character sheet page" (bars → total → personality → traits), which gives that view its own narrative weight vs the bare bars. Source: Beastbook detail polish pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Strict vertical identity stack > cramped horizontal row when portrait space is tight.** Reversed the previous "two-row" layout decision after user feedback on Beastbook. With a 180px portrait eating half the sidebar, even splitting `#num / name / element` across two rows leaves no horizontal room and the element pill collapses to an icon-only 22×22 chip that loses the word. Better: full vertical stack — number on line 1, name on line 2, element pill(s) on line 3 with the FULL uppercase word ("FIRE"), rarity + BST on line 4. Each row gets its own horizontal bandwidth so the pills can read as proper labeled tags. The name no longer competes with sibling pills for the same line, so font-size can stay at 20px without wrapping. Razor: wrap each row in its own tiny `.bb-identity-row-*` div. Source: user feedback "the number should be stack on top of the name and then the element and its full name stacked below" on BeastiaryPanel detail pane 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Per-tier color on the word itself is stronger than bg-tint + white text for mastery-style progression rows.** Original mastery tier list had white text on a tinted background per tier (and unlocked rows were all gold `#fde68a`, locked all faded gray). User flagged that "all the levels for the mastary are white" — the tint is too subtle, the white text dominates, the rank progression doesn't read. Better: put the TIER COLOR directly on the `.tier-name` span (`#9ca3af` Novice → `#4ade80` Adept → `#60a5fa` Veteran → `#a855f7` Elite → `#fbbf24` Master → `#ec4899` Grandmaster), and dim locked rows with `opacity: 0.35` rather than recoloring to gray — locked is the same hue faded, so the progression still reads as a color ramp. Apply the same tier color to the current-title pill's text (dark background + tier-colored span) so "Blazefang Veteran" reads as a blue-labeled chip when you're at Lv3. Razor pattern: add a `tier-@tier` class alongside `unlocked`/`locked` so SCSS can target `.bb-mastery-tier.tier-3 & { color: #60a5fa; }`. Source: user feedback "all the levels for the mastary are white" on Beastbook mastery view 2026-04-14.


- **2026-04-14:** **[PROMOTE candidate] FLATTENED selectors are safer than nested `&.class span { color }` in s&box for critical legibility rules.** Mastery title pill in Beastbook was defined as `.bb-pill.bb-mastery-title-pill { &.level-0 { span { color: #9ca3af; } } ... }`. SCSS compiles this to `.bb-pill.bb-mastery-title-pill.level-0 span { color: ... }` which SHOULD win over `.bb-pill span { color: #ffffff; }` by specificity. User reported "for mastery the text is black so can't read" — the override wasn't taking effect despite correct specificity. Possible cause: s&box CSS engine is inconsistent with deeply-chained combinator resolution, OR the span's color was being inherited from some other rule entirely. **Fix:** flatten to fully-qualified top-level selectors per variant (`.bb-pill.bb-mastery-title-pill.level-0 span { color: ... }` as its own rule) instead of nesting. Apply the same pattern to lore tier pills and mastery-tier-name rows. **Heuristic:** for any color/visibility rule where incorrect rendering would be a critical bug, prefer flat specific selectors over deep nested overrides. Source: Beastbook mastery title fix 2026-04-14, user screenshot showed black unreadable text on "Lochmaw Unbound" Lv0 title.

- **2026-04-14:** **s&box `border-left: Npx solid rgba(...)` is rejected, use `box-shadow: -Npx 0 0 rgba(...)` for a left accent strip.** Already in CLAUDE.md quirks table but re-confirmed on the Beastbook lore redesign — the unlocked-tier accent strip on each lore row is implemented via `box-shadow: -3px 0 0 rgba(<tier-color>, 0.80)` (offset-only, no blur). Produces a visually identical 3px colored edge without running into the border-style parser bug. Each tier-class gets its own rule: `.bb-lore-entry.unlocked.tier-1 { box-shadow: -3px 0 0 rgba(156, 163, 175, 0.80); }` etc. Source: Beastbook lore polish pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Headshot-crop technique for small portrait tiles: `object-fit: cover; object-position: 50% 25%` on an image sized 140% of its container.** The Beastbook evolution chain needed larger readable portraits but 100×100 is still too small to show the whole sprite. Enlarging the sprite to 140px (40% overflow) inside a 100×100 clipped container (`overflow: hidden`) and setting `object-position: 50% 25%` shifts the visible crop toward the top of the image, framing the pixel art face. Works for 128×128 pixel-art sprites because their bodies typically fill the lower two-thirds of the frame; the top half is the head/face. `image-rendering: pixelated` keeps the zoom crisp. If a given beast's sprite doesn't read well headshot-cropped, the fallback is `object-position: 50% 50%` (dead center) or shrink the sprite to 100×100 with no crop. Source: Beastbook evolution chain redesign 2026-04-14, user feedback "we could also use headshots cause trying to show the whole beast is hard".

- **2026-04-14:** **Fixed-width column pattern for row-of-stats cards: diamond + flex name + fixed pill + fixed stats.** Beastbook move rows previously used an absolute-positioned diamond overhanging a single flex child with name-row + meta-row stacked inside. Looked cramped and rows didn't align — the meta-row's pill+power+accuracy had variable gaps depending on which were present. Rebuilt as a 4-column flex grid: `.bb-move-diamond-col { flex: 0 0 40px }` | `.bb-move-info { flex: 1 1 0; flex-direction: column }` (name + level stacked left) | `.bb-move-cat-col { flex: 0 0 50px }` | `.bb-move-stats-col { flex: 0 0 60px; align-items: flex-end }` (power + accuracy stacked right). Every row's columns line up regardless of which stats exist, name length, or category presence. Key: the stats column uses `align-items: flex-end` so the right-justified numbers look aligned, and the cat column uses `justify-content: center` so the pill sits consistently centered even when the move has no category. General principle: when a card layout looks "uneven" row-to-row, reach for fixed-width columns before fiddling with margins. Source: Beastbook moves alignment fix 2026-04-14, user feedback "moves need to be aligned on the left".

- **2026-04-14:** **Per-species grid-card offsets (`CardScale` + `CardOffsetX` + `CardOffsetY`) are composed into one transform with `translate(...) scale(...)`** on `.bb-card-portrait`, with `transform-origin: center bottom` so feet stay planted when scaling. Fields live on `MonsterSpecies.cs` (defaults 1.0 / 0 / 0). Inline styles MUST format floats with `InvariantCulture.ToString("F2")` — s&box CSS needs `.` not `,` regardless of host locale. Offsets apply ONLY to the grid card; detail portrait, evolution chain sprites, and move icons ignore them so data tweaks never cascade. Example: Manehelm (003) head clips top edge at 1.3x, so `CardOffsetY = 12f` pushes the portrait down; Lochmaw (006) wants `CardScale = 1.25f, CardOffsetX = 6f, CardOffsetY = 8f` for a grounded-but-bigger read. Source: BeastiaryPanel grid-card polish 2026-04-14, user screenshot feedback on Manehelm/Lochmaw framing.

- **2026-04-14:** **Razor `@(expression) to ...` inside a plain text span can render literally in s&box razor (pre-compiled output shows the source, not the value).** Observed in BeastiaryPanel lore-locked-text where `Lv@(loreIdx) to unlock` was emitting the literal `@(loreIdx)` text. Workaround that fixed it: hoist to a named local immediately above (`var lockedLv = loreIdx;`) and emit as `Lv@lockedLv to unlock` — the simple `@identifier` form is unambiguous to the tokenizer. Prefer named locals over `@(expression)` whenever the surrounding text could confuse the parser (trailing letter `t` etc).
