Source: BeastiaryPanel lore rows, user report "text renders literal" 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Pill family uniformity is load-bearing for detail-pane calm.** When rarity-badge, BST-badge, element-pills, personality-pill, trait-pills, and mastery-title-pill share one pill family (22px height, 4-5px vertical padding, 8-12px horizontal padding, 10-11px/800 uppercase text), the whole pane reads as "chips on a page." When one pill is 24px tall, another is 20px, they look like three unrelated UI systems colliding. Rule: define the pill height explicitly (`height: 22px`) on every pill variant AND on any sibling badge that lives in the same row. Source: BeastiaryPanel polish pass 2026-04-14 — mastery-title-pill was 24px while rarity/BST were 22px; aligning everything to 22px visibly tightened the identity row.

- **2026-04-14:** **Progress-bar tier color should match CURRENT tier, not NEXT tier.** Earlier iteration had `.bb-mastery-progress-fill.level-1` render green (Lv2's color) on the reasoning "you're progressing toward Lv2." Looks arbitrary in practice — the user reads the bar as "my rank" not "my next rank". Match the fill color to the current tier's canonical color map (Lv1 gray, Lv2 green, Lv3 blue, Lv4 purple, Lv5 gold, Lv6 pink). Lv0 stays purple as a motivational starter color since the tier map has no Lv0 entry in the checklist. Source: BeastiaryPanel mastery polish 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **`font-size: Npx; line-height: N` at 16px or 20px can clip in s&box (not only the 30px+ rule).** Observed `BST` value at `font-size: 16px; line-height: 16` — descenders visibly clipped. Rule of thumb: set `line-height` to `font-size + 2` as a baseline for any font >= 14px, not just for the 30px+ large-font case mentioned in CLAUDE.md. For tiny text (9-11px) `line-height = font-size + 1` or even equal works. Source: BeastiaryPanel BST badge review 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Per-element hover-sound guard pattern: one `lastHoveredXId` field per element family, each with its own Hover handler.** BeastiaryPanel grew to 4 independent families (grid cards, detail diamonds, evolution stages, move rows) each with many children that share the same hover-sound semantics but are mutually exclusive. Using one global `lastHoveredId` field would mis-deduplicate when the cursor crosses family boundaries (hovering a move row then a diamond with the same-ish id would silently fail to play a sound). Pattern: `lastHoveredCardId`, `lastHoveredDiamondId`, `lastHoveredEvoStageId`, `lastHoveredMoveId` — each with `OnCardHover`, `OnDiamondHover`, etc. taking a string id, guarding `if (lastX == id) return`, else update and `SoundManager.PlayHover()`. Why: the guard's job is "only fire once as we enter a new element of this family" — crossing from one family to another is a legitimate re-entry and should re-fire. Per-family state is the correct granularity. Source: BeastiaryPanel feel pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Snappy diamond feel recipe — cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.8, 0.3, 1.3) overshoot + `:active` compress + `:active:hover` nudge.** The diamond action row in Beastbook felt dead when every state used `ease` or `ease-out`. Replacing the outer transition with `transition: transform 0.14s cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.8, 0.3, 1.3)` produces a tiny spring overshoot on hover-in (feels like a pop), and adding `&:active { transform: scale(0.93); }` gives a physical-weight press feedback. For selected-state diamonds, `&.active { transform: scale(1.06); }` keeps the "you are here" visible, and `&.active:hover { transform: scale(1.12); }` allows further hover nudge without collapsing back to baseline. The inner color/border transition on the `.shape` child must be tightened to match the outer scale (0.15s ease-out, not 0.2s ease) or the colors visibly trail the scale. General pattern: when a button family feels flat, reach for spring-curve bezier + distinct press + distinct active/selected retain before adding glow. Source: BeastiaryPanel diamond feel pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **Stat-bar / progress-fill overshoot easing = `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.4, 0.64, 1)`.** When a stat bar fill transitions to a new width (e.g. switching species in the Beastbook detail pane), a plain `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` ease-in-out looks like a lerp. Swapping to `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.4, 0.64, 1)` makes the bar slide to near-target then elastic-settle back slightly — feels alive, reads as "stat arriving" rather than "value crossfaded." Apply the same curve to the mastery progress bar for consistent motion vocabulary. Don't use it on widths that scrub in real time (live progress during a battle) — the overshoot will look jittery on continuous input. Good for one-shot species-swap / reward-increment transitions only. Source: BeastiaryPanel stat bar feel pass 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-14:** **`filter: brightness(1.1)` on pill hover is a cheap way to "lift" every chip without adding a new color.** Single-function filter (s&box can't chain filter functions). Added to `.bb-pill`'s base `transition: filter 0.15s ease-out` and `&:hover { filter: brightness(1.1); }`. When a child variant already has its own `transition` (e.g. `.bb-pill.trait` transitioned bg + border), the child's rule overrides the base — so the filter property must be re-declared in the child's transition list or the brightness won't animate smoothly. Principle: if a variant overrides the base pill's transition, append `filter Xs ease-out` to the override rather than relying on cascade. Source: BeastiaryPanel pill hover polish 2026-04-14.

- **2026-04-16:** **Sibling-panel selection parity: Beastbook grid selected state should mirror roster's `.monster-card.selected` purple ring, not invent a new white-border convention.** Beastbook was using `border-color: #ffffff; box-shadow: 0 12px 28px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)` for selected — weaker than the hover state (white hover border almost as bright) and unrelated to roster's ring vocabulary. Ported roster's `border-color: #8b5cf6; box-shadow: 0 0 28px rgba(139,92,246,0.6), 0 8px 24px rgba(0,0,0,0.7); translateY(-4px)` directly. Rule of thumb: selection-ring color for a primary interactive grid should be the purple primary (`#8b5cf6`) across the entire game — reserve white/color-specific rings for mode-specific signals (fusion parent = gold in roster.mini, release marker = red in roster). Source: Beastbook finishing pass 2026-04-16, parity audit vs `MonsterCard.razor.scss:58`.

- **2026-04-16:** **Trait-tooltip center-anchor pattern for narrow side panels.** Beastbook's detail pane is 480px wide and trait chips wrap into a row that can place a chip near the right edge. Tooltip anchored `left: 0; width: 240px` clipped past the pane for right-edge chips. Fix: `left: 50%; width: 210px; margin-left: -105px;` (center-anchored via negative margin instead of `transform: translateX(-50%)` because the existing fade transition already occupies the transform slot). Trade-off: tooltip can still clip on the last chip in the rightmost ~40px of the pane, but the clip is on average half as bad and symmetric. Better than one-sided clipping that always hits the same chip. Source: Beastbook finishing pass 2026-04-16, trait-tooltip audit.

- **2026-04-16:** **Stylistic halo gradients on empty states read as AI-looking — prefer bare centered icon + text.** `.bb-empty` had `background: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.04) 0%, rgba(139, 92, 246, 0) 60%)` intended to suggest "held open waiting for you." In practice it reads as a generic AI-output purple halo — removing makes the empty state feel intentionally clean rather than atmospherically dressed. The surrounding chrome (detail-sidebar gradient border + drop shadow) already provides the "pane is here and ready" affordance. Applied rule: empty states lean on iconify + copy hierarchy, not color washes. Source: Beastbook finishing pass 2026-04-16, style-guide "no AI halo" directive.

- **2026-04-16:** **Prefer `box-shadow: -Npx 0 0 color` over `border-left: Npx solid` for accent strips on rows that share horizontal alignment with neighbours.** Even though `border-left: 3px solid rgba(...)` works in s&box, swapping a uniform 1px border to a 3px-left-side border shifts the element's box model — a 2px content shift appears on any "unlocked" row while "locked" rows keep their 1px border. The visual misalignment reads as a layout bug. box-shadow with offset `-Npx 0 0` and no blur produces a visually identical N-pixel strip without affecting the box model, so sibling rows stay aligned. Use border-left when the whole family shares the accent (section titles), use box-shadow when only a subset of siblings get the accent (lore-entry unlocked/locked mix). Source: Beastbook finishing pass 2026-04-16 — considered migrating lore accents from box-shadow to border-left for idiom consistency, reverted after realizing the layout-shift implication.

- **2026-04-17:** **Single-line stat row pattern for compact detail panes: LABEL (36px fixed) | BAR (flex:1 1 0 + min-width:0) | VALUE (30px fixed right-aligned).** Roster uses a two-line stat row (header row with label+value spaced, bar below) because its left column is ~320px and the two-line rhythm fits the bigger pane. Beastbook's 480px detail pane + 6 stats benefits from a denser single-line row: per-stat values tabular-align vertically which makes scanning across species much easier. Quirk check: only ONE child gets `flex: 1 1 0`; the others pin explicit widths — matches the working `.bb-move-row` pattern. Add per-stat soft-tinted label (HP → soft green `#86efac`, ATK → soft red `#fca5a5`, etc) to group label with bar color without shouting — the eye reads color before the text so grouping is instant. Add row hover `background-color: rgba(255,255,255,0.03)` + 4px border-radius: subtle "this is inspectable" Tier 1 feedback even though the row is non-interactive. BST TOTAL row gets visible upgrade vs each stat row (18px purple value + text-shadow, thicker padding+border-top) so it reads as CONCLUSION not another row. Source: BeastiaryPanel stats polish pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Element pill icon bump 12→14px + gap 6→7px + 1px text-shadow.** The base `.bb-pill` 12px icon reads fine on the 22px pill for dense grids, but in detail-pane identity-block contexts (next to rarity/BST badges + under 22px beast name) the icon feels undersized vs its neighbours. Bumping to 14px keeps it comfortably inside the 22px pill height while matching the optical weight of the adjacent badges. `text-shadow: 0 1px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.35)` on the FIRE/SHADOW label lifts it off saturated fills (red, green, yellow) without adding a border — the letters read as "printed on" instead of "flat decal," which is the small shift that makes the pill feel like a made object rather than a css rectangle. Apply only to identity-block element pills (`.bb-element-pill-full`), not the base `.bb-pill` — grid cards / move rows still use the smaller 12px glyph by design. Source: BeastiaryPanel typing pill refinement 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-16:** **Trait tooltip via code-behind hover state, not CSS `:hover .descendant` cascade.** Repro: `.bb-pill.trait:hover .bb-trait-tooltip { opacity: 1 }` intermittently only fires on mouseout in s&box — the tooltip would appear as the cursor *left* the pill rather than while it was hovered. Fix pattern: track `hoveredTraitId` in the razor code-behind, wire `onmouseover={() => OnTraitHover(id)}` + `onmouseout={() => OnTraitOut(id)}`, render the tooltip conditionally with `@if (hoveredTraitId == id)`. The pill itself also takes an `.is-hovered` class mirroring the CSS `&:hover` rule so bg/border tint still matches. Add `hoveredTraitId` to `BuildHash()` so the framework re-renders. The tooltip becomes a mount/unmount element with no `opacity`/`transform` transition needed. Also applicable to any other s&box tooltip where `:hover .child` cascade feels laggy. Source: Beastbook trait-tooltip bug fix 2026-04-16, BeastiaryPanel.

- **2026-04-16: [PROMOTE]** **Personality pill (and any other "static identity info" pill) should explicitly override `.bb-pill:hover { filter: brightness(1.1) }` by neutralizing the filter.** Base `.bb-pill` applies a brightness lift on hover to make interactive pills feel alive. Personality is identity, not interactive — the hover lift makes it feel clickable, user flagged this as "feels weird." Pattern: inside `&.personality { &:hover { filter: brightness(1); background-color: <base>; border-color: <base>; } }`. Re-declare the base bg/border so the hover state holds identical visual state. General rule: if a `.bb-pill` variant is non-interactive, give it an explicit `:hover` override that neutralizes the filter AND re-pins bg/border, otherwise the base rule leaks through. Source: BeastiaryPanel personality hover fix 2026-04-16.
  - **UPDATE 2026-04-17:** **s&box rejects `filter: none` — runtime error "Generic none is not valid with filter".** Use `filter: brightness(1)` as the identity-equivalent neutralizer instead (same visual effect, parser-safe). Verified at `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:844` with user-reported runtime log. Applies to ANY place that wants to cancel an inherited filter rule. [PROMOTED to CLAUDE.md quirks table 2026-04-17]

- **2026-04-16:** **Canonical element palette = FilterBar.razor.scss filter pill active state (lines 86-95).** Beastbook, MonsterRoster, and FilterBar disagreed on element colors previously (Beastbook used softer `rgba(239,68,68,0.9)` reds, FilterBar used `#b91c1c` / `#dc2626`). User directed the FilterBar palette as the source of truth for the whole game — all backgrounds dark/saturated, white text on all, no dark-text-on-light exception. Apply to any `.bb-pill.element-*`, `.bb-card-element-badge.element-*`, or similar element-identity surface. Rim-light accents (e.g. `.bb-move-row` border at 0.65 alpha over neutral dark row bg, `.bb-evo-stage.current` portrait outline) may keep the lighter "game palette" values since they are glows, not identity fills. Style-guide's Element color map now reflects this. Source: BeastiaryPanel unify-colors pass 2026-04-16, `FilterBar.razor.scss:86-95`.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTED to CLAUDE.md quirks table]** **s&box `overflow-y: scroll` / `overflow: hidden` on a grid/list container does NOT clip: (1) descendant `box-shadow`, (2) descendant `transform: translateY/translateX` lifts, OR (3) absolutely-positioned descendant CONTENT.** Applying `transform: translateY(-Npx)` to a selected/hovered card inside a scroll grid LIFTS THE CARD OUT OF THE SCROLL CLIP — the card visually teleports above the grid, rendering on top of (or behind) the header. User report: "Gothsire name floating under the ONLINE tab" — Gothsire was the selected card, its `transform: translateY(-4px)` pulled its name strip + element pill into the header territory.

  **The correct pattern for hover/selected states on scroll-grid cards:**
  - NO `transform: translateY(...)` (escapes the clip).
  - NO `box-shadow` with colored outer glow (leaks past clip).
  - NO `box-shadow` with dark outer drop (also leaks, visible against page bg).
  - USE: `border-width` bump (1px → 2px) + brighter `border-color` + subtle `background-color` shift. Entirely "in the box" styling that can't escape.
  - Animated shimmer/pulse on the border color is fine (color alone, no geometric change).

  **Supplementary rule:** chrome above/below scroll grids (headers, top/bottom HUD bars) MUST be opaque (`rgba(..., 0.95+)`) so any unavoidable leak from other sources (tooltips, filters at edges) is covered.

  **Critical supplementary rule 2026-04-17:** the panel-root container for a scroll-grid page (e.g. `.roster-panel`, `.beastbook-panel`) MUST use `gap: 0` between the header and the grid container — NOT `gap: 16px` or any visible spacing. A visible gap is a transparent window onto the panel's animated bg where scrolled-past card name strips render as ghost rows. With `gap: 0`, the header sits flush above the grid and there is no gap region for leaks to occupy. Vertical breathing room should come from header padding + grid inner padding, not a flex gap. User report trigger: "ghost name strips above the grid" in My Beasts 2026-04-17.
  
  **Wrong fix** (first attempt): strip all `box-shadow` from scroll-grid cards. Preserves leak-free rendering but flattens the cards and still doesn't fix the absolutely-positioned content (number pills, element badges) leak.
  
  **Right fix:** keep card shadows/decoration; make the **chrome opaque** so leaks are covered. Specifically:
  - `.roster-header` / `.beastbook-header` / any floating header above a scroll grid → `background-color: rgba(8, 6, 18, 0.98)` (was transparent or 0.5 alpha).
  - `.unified-top-bar` / `.bottom-bar` → `background-color: rgba(8, 6, 16, 0.95)+` (already done earlier this session).
  - Ensure chrome `z-index > grid z-index` (roster-header uses `z-index: 500`).
  
  This trades a "floating translucent header" aesthetic for leak-free rendering. The aesthetic was lost either way because leaks were visible; opaque chrome gives up the less-used intent (see-through header) and keeps the more-used one (depth on cards).
  
  **Still true:** box-shadows leak past scroll clip. But if the leak lands behind opaque chrome, nobody sees it. Apply the opaque-chrome rule to any panel that has a scrollable grid + a header/footer adjacent to it.
  
  Source: roster `.monster-card` + `.roster-header`, Beastbook `.bb-card` + `.beastbook-header`, user reports 2026-04-17 ("purple leaking into the tab" + "black outlines of the cards in the back" + "number pill and element icon" leak). Initial shadow-stripping reverted; instead bumped both panel headers to `rgba(8, 6, 18, 0.98)`.

- **2026-04-17:** **"You are here" recipe for tier-chain views (Evolution, Rank, Skill Tree).** When a panel shows a horizontal chain of equal-sized tiles (evolution stages, rank tiers, etc) and needs to signal WHICH one the player is currently at, the single-layer signal (soft border tint + 2px translate) is too quiet — user can't tell apart 2-stage chains. The proven hero recipe:
  1. **Hero scale + lift combined:** `transform: translateY(-2px) scale(1.05)` on `.current` — physical pop, not just a nudge.
  2. **Hero border + wider glow:** bump border alpha 0.65 → 0.9, add `0 0 22px rgba(element, 0.42)` outer glow alongside existing drop shadow. Wider + stronger than baseline.
  3. **Dim siblings:** non-current portraits drop to `opacity: 0.78`. Current pops by contrast, not just by color. `.current` wins by overriding to `opacity: 1`.
  4. **Hero typography:** current-stage name bumps `font-size: 12 → 13px`, color `rgba(255,255,255,0.7) → #ffffff`, adds `text-shadow: 0 1px 4px rgba(0,0,0,0.6)`. Stays white not element-colored — border+glow+badge already do color work.
  5. **Explicit "CURRENT" micro-chip:** absolutely-positioned 60×16px pill at top-center of the portrait (`top: -8px; left: 50%; margin-left: -30px`). 8px/800/uppercase/1.4px letter-spacing. Dark bg + element-tinted border + element-tinted text. Pure state cue — no player ever has to scan. Adds `position: relative` to the stage wrapper (only for the portrait's absolute positioning).
  
  Result: "you are here" reads in one glance even on 2-stage chains. Source: Beastbook evolution-view hero pass 2026-04-17, BeastiaryPanel.razor/scss.

- **2026-04-17:** **Grouped grid inside a scroll container: use flex-wrap AT the scroll-root with `flex-basis: 100%` section-title break rows, NOT nested flex-wrap grids.** For a shop-style layout with section headers alternating with item rows inside a single scroll pane, the proven-safe pattern is: `.scroll-root { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; overflow-y: scroll; ... }` with direct children being either (a) `.section-title { width: 100%; flex-basis: 100%; flex-shrink: 0; flex-grow: 0; }` to force a line break, or (b) item cards with fixed `width: Xpx; flex: 0 0 Xpx`. The featured/hero card is also a direct child with `flex-basis: 100%`. This avoids the nested-flex-inside-scroll collapse (CLAUDE.md quirk). Originally I had `.items-grid` wrapper divs with their own `flex-wrap: wrap` nested inside the scroll — removed them, pulled items up to the scroll root, added `flex-basis: 100%` to section headers to force new rows. Source: ShopPanelContent full rework 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Reward-flyer pattern for purchase/claim moments — port from DailyPanel.** Full recipe: (1) `<div class="burst-layer burst-@version">` as sibling of the scrolling content area (to escape `overflow: hidden`), mounted only when `burstActive` is true. (2) 10-12 `<div class="burst-particle ppN">` children for the radial burst, each with its own `@keyframes` animation fanning outward over 0.6-0.9s with slight delay stagger. (3) One `<div class="reward-flyer fly-gold">` (or `fly-token`) that translates from the burst origin toward the viewport top-right corner over ~0.5s. (4) C# side: `async void PurchaseX()` calls layered sounds immediately (`PlayClick + PlaySuccess + PlayGoldReward`), sets overshoot flag (cleared 120ms later), sets `burstVersion++; burstActive = true`, then `await GameTask.DelaySeconds( 0.35f )` before calling the actual `ShopManager.PurchaseItem(...)`. The deferred manager call means the GameHUD currency roll-up + icon pulse fires when the flyer "lands" in the top-right, creating a crisp cause-effect link. After another 0.5s the burst layer is dismounted. BuildHash must include `burstActive` and `burstVersion`. The particle origin is at `top: 85%; left: 80%` (near the detail-pane buy button); the flyer final keyframe is `translate(200px, -380px)` — hardcoded because measuring the GameHUD pixel position from Razor is fragile in s&box. Source: ShopPanelContent rework 2026-04-17 adapting DailyPanel.razor.scss `.claim-burst-particle` + `.claim-reward-flyer`.

- **2026-04-17:** **Button press overshoot spring — `transform: scale(1.05)` with cubic-bezier transition.** For click feedback on a primary action button, add a short-lived `.overshoot` class toggled by C# for ~120ms. SCSS: `transition: transform 0.12s cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.8, 0.3, 1.3)` on the button, `&.overshoot { transform: scale(1.05); }`. The overshoot-easing cubic-bezier overshoots the target and settles back, giving the button a springy press feel. Must be added to BuildHash so the class flip re-renders. Pairs nicely with `:active { transform: scale(0.97); }` — press compresses, then release springs up. Source: ShopPanelContent buy-button juice pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Featured/hero card treatment for grid panels — ribbon + sheen + enlarged icon + gradient icon bg.** For promoting one item to "hero" status in a grid: (1) full-width card with 14-16px radius, distinct bg (dark warm tint for gold tier), 1px border in the item's theme color (45% alpha idle → 75% alpha on hover); (2) absolute-positioned `.ribbon` in the top-right corner with the accent color bg + dark text + small icon + label like "DAILY SPOTLIGHT"; (3) `.sheen` layer as an absolute `linear-gradient(120deg, ...)` diagonal highlight at opacity 0 idle, fading to 1 on `&:hover`; (4) enlarged icon (~72px vs 56px for regular cards) inside a 96px container with its own gradient bg (`linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(gold,0.25), rgba(gold-deep,0.1))` + matching 1px border); (5) larger name text (20px/800). On hover the icon gets `transform: translateY(-2px) scale(1.04)` for subtle parallax. No infinite pulse/breathing — the sheen-on-hover is the only dynamic element, keeping the card restful until interacted. Source: ShopPanelContent featured-card design 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Deleted legacy panel pattern — grep, delete, update inventory in one pass.** When a duplicate/legacy panel file exists alongside the live version: (1) grep for the class name across `Code/` to confirm zero references (not just the `.scss` self-reference); (2) delete both `.razor` and `.razor.scss`; (3) mark the inventory entry as `_[DELETED YYYY-MM-DD]_` rather than removing the entry — preserves the history that "there used to be a ShopPanel, it's gone now" for anyone reading the inventory later. Bonus: removing dead code from the project means future searches/greps don't surface it as a misleading result. Source: ShopPanelContent rework 2026-04-17 deletion of legacy ShopPanel.razor.

- **2026-04-17:** **Scroll-fade hinter pattern for dense grids — absolute overlay, not a mask.** For a scrolling item area that may contain more than is visible (shop grid, inventory, roster), add a small `<div class="scroll-fade scroll-fade-bottom">` sibling of the scroll element inside the `position: relative` parent. SCSS: `position: absolute; left: 0; right: 0; bottom: 0; height: 56px; pointer-events: none; z-index: 20; background: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(bgcolor, 0) 0%, rgba(bgcolor, 0.98) 100%);`. The parent's `border-radius + overflow: hidden` clips the fade to the rounded bottom automatically — no per-side radius needed on the fade itself. Don't add a top fade unless the scroll can actually start mid-content (top section-title already reads as a boundary in most cases). `pointer-events: none` is critical — the scroll area underneath must stay interactive right up to the visible edge. Anti-gacha note: hinting "more below" is fine; never use the fade to hide/obscure items as a reveal gimmick. Source: ShopPanelContent polish pass 2026-04-17, ShopPanelContent.razor.scss `.scroll-fade-bottom`.

- **2026-04-17:** **Active-state juice for running boosts/buffs — Tier 1 ambient breath, not a flash.** When a card represents a currently-running timed state (boost active, buff running, etc.), use a slow 3.2s `@keyframes` border-color pulse (alpha 0.45 → 0.75 → 0.45) as ambient "this is alive" feedback. NOT a fast pulse (that reads "attention needed"); NOT a glow halo (reads AI-tell). Hover MUST set `animation: none` to prevent the hover-border and the pulsing-border from fighting — hover takes precedence as the primary interactive state. Combine with a foreground `.item-active-check` overlay (size 30 lucide:check icon on a `rgba(251, 191, 36, 0.3)` warm tint) so the "running" state reads at peripheral glance, not just from the corner pill. And for boost cards specifically, replace the price line with a live time-remaining readout (`.item-time-remaining` chip) so the player can read "45m left" without opening the detail pane. Three layered signals (breath + check overlay + time readout) make the state unmistakable without being loud. Source: ShopPanelContent active-boost polish pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Bundle-math value callouts are anti-gacha-safe when grounded in per-unit price, not RNG framing.** For shop/store panels: "SAVE 15%" / "BEST VALUE · 40% OFF" pills computed from `(1 - thisPerUnit / cheapestSiblingPerUnit)` are fine because the math is transparent and deterministic. A player can compute the savings themselves by dividing. This is different from "lucky pull!" / "guaranteed drop!" framing which is gacha-shaped. Gate the callout by `if (savePct < 5) return null;` so tiny fake savings don't appear — the callout must earn its space. Also worth two copy tiers: `SAVE X%` for modest savings (5-29%), `BEST VALUE · X% OFF` for substantial (30%+). The tiered copy avoids the "BEST VALUE!" tag devaluing across the shop. Source: ShopPanelContent featured-card value-callout 2026-04-17, `GetFeaturedSavings()` helper.

- **2026-04-17:** **Hover nudge pattern for primary action buttons — `iconify { transform: translateX(Npx); }` on hover.** A stylistic improvement that makes buy/confirm buttons feel tactile without adding glow: on hover, translate the button's leading iconify glyph right by 3px via a cubic-bezier spring (0.18s, 0.2/0.8/0.3/1.3). The button also gets a 1px lift via `translateY(-1px)`. Combined effect: the icon subtly "leans forward" toward the press, signaling the action is primed. Works for shop "Purchase", daily "Claim", confirm "Accept" and similar. Doesn't replace `:active { scale(0.97); }` or the overshoot spring — those handle the press + release; this is the resting-to-hover transition. Source: ShopPanelContent buy-btn polish 2026-04-17, ShopPanelContent.razor.scss `.buy-btn:hover iconify { transform: translateX(3px); }`.

- **2026-04-17:** **Roster-of-patterns: when the feel critique is a list of many small things, resist the urge to redesign.** This polish pass responded to 11 bullet points ranging from "featured card dead gradient" to "keyboard shortcuts." The right move is surgical — address 5-7 of the highest impact-per-effort items, flag the rest as "skipped because X." Don't bundle every suggestion into a mega-rework that lands 80% flat. Specifically: DO ship the scroll-fade, value-callout, boost-active breath, hover feel. DON'T take on keyboard shortcuts (s&box input quirks + low gain), description-copy changes (out of scope for UI agent, flag for product), or justify-content rearrangement (changes the whole grid rhythm for one edge case). Restraint preserves the panel's stability. Source: ShopPanelContent polish pass 2026-04-17 critique response.

- **2026-04-17:** **Deficit helper pill for can't-afford cards.** When a player can't afford an item, instead of just dimming the card, show a small inline pill under the price reading "need +X,XXX" in amber-red. SCSS: `background-color: rgba(239, 68, 68, 0.15); color: #fca5a5; font-size: 9px; font-weight: 700; padding: 2px 6px; border-radius: 4px;`. This tells the player EXACTLY how much more they need — turning a "can't click" moment into a "I know what's between me and this purchase" moment. Much more actionable than silent dim. Same pattern works on the grid card AND the detail-pane price line. Source: ShopPanelContent rework 2026-04-17, inspired by AAA games like Hades and Marvel Snap.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **CRITICAL DIAGNOSIS: a full-width "hero" card inside a `flex-wrap: wrap; overflow-y: scroll` grid can COMPLETELY VANISH if the card has `overflow: hidden` on itself.** Re-confirms the CLAUDE.md quirk ("overflow: hidden collapses flex children") but specifically in the `flex-wrap` scroll-container context where a `width: 100%; flex-basis: 100%` forces-a-new-row hero card. Symptoms: the conditional renders in Razor (logic returns a valid item) but the card paints to zero height — the grid jumps straight from the preceding element to the next section-title below. Fix: `overflow: visible` on the card; move clipping responsibility to the child that actually needs clipping (e.g. a `.sheen` overlay gets its own `border-radius` matching the card). Also: add `min-height` on the hero card as belt-and-suspenders so if s&box still miscalculates, content-height still wins. Source: ShopPanelContent.razor.scss `.featured-card` 2026-04-17 regression — card was invisible despite GetFeaturedItem() returning valid data. Same root cause as the flex-child collapse quirk, but in a new form.

- **2026-04-17:** **Owned/Equipped visual separation for cosmetics — color, not just text.** When a cosmetic grid shows many OWNED items with one EQUIPPED, relying on chip-text distinction ("OWNED" vs "EQUIPPED" in the same green chip) is too weak — players scan chip color before reading text. Fix: EQUIPPED = gold (canonical "active/current" across the game — matches in-world boosts, daily streak completion, etc); OWNED = slate/neutral (dormant). Add a 2px top-bar "chrome" accent on equipped cards so the card itself differs from owned, not just the chip. Background color also shifts: equipped uses warm `rgba(32, 24, 8, 0.92)`, owned uses neutral-cool `rgba(18, 22, 30, 0.92)`. Three layers of distinction (border, bg, chrome bar) mean zero ambiguity at a glance across an 8-card grid. Source: ShopPanelContent 2026-04-17, themes + titles cosmetic grid polish.

- **2026-04-17:** **Inline-style border-color fights hover border-color — use background-color for cosmetic tints instead.** Tried setting per-theme card tint via inline `style="border-color: rgba(...)"` so each theme card gets its signature color as border hint. Problem: inline styles beat stylesheet selectors in s&box's cascade, so the `&:hover { border-color: ... }` SCSS rule never wins — hover border goes dead. Fix: put the tint on `background-color` at very low alpha (`0.06`) instead. Background tint doesn't fight hover border, and it's subtle enough that the card still reads as dark-neutral but is identifiable by color in the grid. Alternative would be CSS custom properties but s&box doesn't support those. Source: ShopPanelContent BuildThemeCardStyle helper 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Level-req chip should suppress for over-levelled players.** Chips that say "LV X required" + green checkmark are useful when the player is close to the gate ("I just unlocked this," "I'm almost there"). For a lv 158 player browsing items with req 10, the chip becomes visual noise — every card gets a green check that adds zero info. Rule: only show the chip when `RequiredLevel > 1` AND `playerLevel < RequiredLevel + 5`. The 5-level buffer ≈ one play session of catch-up, so new players still see "I reached these recently" chips but veterans see clean cards. Always show locked chips — those ARE actionable. Source: ShopPanelContent ShouldShowLevelChip helper 2026-04-17, user feedback on lv 158 test account.

- **2026-04-17:** **Hex → rgba helper for inline-style dynamic colors.** s&box CSS doesn't support `color-mix()` or CSS custom properties, so tinting inline styles at arbitrary alpha requires manual conversion. Pattern: `HexToRgba(string hex, float alpha)` that parses `#RRGGBB` substrings to ints, returns `rgba(r, g, b, a)` with InvariantCulture alpha formatting ("F2"). Graceful fallback for malformed input → `rgba(0,0,0,a)`. Never throws. Use for theme/title preview tints where the color is user-data (cosmetic definitions) and alpha needs to be CSS-controlled. Source: ShopPanelContent HexToRgba helper 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Fallback-ladder for "featured item" logic to cover the full player lifecycle.** Early-game featured picks should highlight storage expansion (a meaningful big-ticket purchase). Late-game players have maxed storage — a hero card still featuring storage is dead space. Fix: ladder through `if (player can still expand storage) → MonsterSlot hero`, `else → highest-priced personal boost`, `else → highest-priced non-server item`. Never feature server-wide boosts as the hero (they're a communal action, not a personal-progression moment; gacha-shaped to frame them as personal "big pick"). Gate with `currentCategory == "all" && goldSort == "default"` so filtered/sorted views don't fight the player's filter intent. Source: ShopPanelContent GetFeaturedItem 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Trailing "more coming" placeholder to tease future content without hiding dead space.** For flex-wrap grids with odd item counts, the final row often has visible empty space to the right. Options: (1) invisible flex filler, (2) a "coming soon" card matching item footprint, (3) tighter card widths. Option 2 is the best product signal — turns dead space into a tease rather than hiding it. Implementation: `.shop-item.shop-item-coming-soon { pointer-events: none; opacity: 0.65; background-color: rgba(20, 20, 35, 0.4); border: 1px solid rgba(196, 181, 253, 0.15); }` with a `lucide:plus` icon + "More coming" label + context hint ("Stay tuned" / "Beat more bosses"). Same width as real cards so it slots cleanly. No hover state — it's structure, not interactive. Only render when there ARE items (not as an empty-state replacement). Source: ShopPanelContent trailing placeholder 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Sibling-panel spacing rhythm: 16px between sections, 0 before first.** When multiple sections (Supplies / Boosts / Storage) stack inside one scroll container, tight 4-8px spacing between `.shop-section-title` reads as a dense undifferentiated wall. Bumping to 16px (style-guide `lg` token) with `&:first-child { margin-top: 0; }` gives sections breathing room without creating a half-empty grid. This matches the roster detail-pane section rhythm — use the same token everywhere for predictable hierarchy. Source: ShopPanelContent.razor.scss `.shop-section-title` 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **`radial-gradient(circle, ...)` works; `radial-gradient(ellipse, ...)` is REJECTED.** s&box parser accepts the `circle` form (see `MonsterDetailPanel.razor.scss:624` working precedent) but fails on `ellipse` entirely — even without positional args — per DailyPanel's explicit comment at line 1442. For ambient glows, use `radial-gradient(circle, color1, color2)` with color-stop percentages to shape the falloff. If a true ellipse shape is needed, fall back to multi-layer `box-shadow` spread blurring on a small centered div (see `.hero-beast-spotlight` in DailyPanel). Source: ShopPanelContent `.featured-ambient-glow` 2026-04-17, cross-referenced with DailyPanel + MonsterDetailPanel precedents.

- **2026-04-17:** **Fake strike-through pattern (s&box doesn't reliably support `text-decoration: line-through`).** To show a struck-through "was" price: wrap the text span in a `position: relative` container, then add a sibling `<div class="price-struck-line"></div>` that's `position: absolute; left: 0; right: 0; top: Npx; height: 2px; background-color: rgba(...);` drawn across the middle of the text. The "top" value needs to be ~half the text line-height minus 1px — for font-size 13 that's top: 7px; for font-size 15 that's top: 8px. Pair with muted red-gray text color (`rgba(252, 165, 165, 0.7)`) and a smaller size than the "now" price so the strike + downsize together read as "old price." Add an iconify `lucide:arrow-right` between was→now to reinforce the direction. Source: ShopPanelContent `.featured-price.discounted` + `.detail-price.discounted` 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Real sale vs bundle-math: don't double-surface savings signals on the same card.** If a card already displays a true discount (e.g. "15% OFF TODAY" with struck-through price), the existing bundle-math chip ("SAVE 12%" from per-unit price comparison) becomes noise — two competing "save money" claims compete for attention. Rule: when a real discount is active, suppress the bundle-math calculation. Bundle math is still valuable on non-discounted cards where it's the only value signal. Implementation: `var featuredSave = isSpotlightSale ? null : GetFeaturedSavings(featured);` — trivial gate at the UI layer. Source: ShopPanelContent 2026-04-17 Daily Spotlight + GetFeaturedSavings interaction.

- **2026-04-17:** **Spotlight/sale state belongs on the manager, not the data class.** When adding a discount feature like "one item per day is on sale," keep the sale metadata (which item id + discount percent) on the singleton manager (ShopManager) rather than stamping `FeaturedDiscountPercent` on every ShopItem. Why: ShopItem stays pure data; the "which one is featured today" concept lives where that kind of orchestration naturally belongs. UI syncs back to the manager via a `SetSpotlight(itemId)` call so the manager's discount applies to whatever the UI actually shows (critical for players who hit state gates like "storage maxed" — the UI ladders to a different item, and the manager follows). Purchase flow already routes through `GetDiscountedPrice` → charges via SpendGold → refunds on failure → mission-tracks the discounted amount. Adding a new discount layer there applies to every hook for free. Source: ShopManager `SpotlightItemId` + `SetSpotlight` + `GetDiscountedPrice` integration 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`CanAfford` in a shop panel MUST use the effective (post-discount) price.** Pre-existing pattern was `CanAfford(item) => GetGold() >= item.Price` — breaks the moment any discount logic is introduced (skill discount, spotlight sale, etc.). The player has enough gold to buy at the discounted price, but the card shows cant-afford / the Purchase button is disabled. Route through ShopManager: `GetEffectivePrice(item) => ShopManager.Instance?.GetDiscountedPrice(item) ?? item.Price` and use it for affordability, deficit calculation, and the struck/now display. All downstream "can the player actually buy this?" checks get consistent behavior. Source: ShopPanelContent `CanAfford` + `GetEffectivePrice` refactor 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Ambient motion on the hero card: ONE layer, not a stack.** Temptation when adding "this card is special" motion is to layer multiple effects (radial glow + linear shimmer + sparkles + hover sheen). Result: a busy ambient soup where nothing reads cleanly. Rule: if a card already has ambient sparkles + hover sheen + icon lift, a single slow breathing radial glow is enough to mark it as "hero." Tier 1 restraint: one ambient signal, not four. Test: if removing a layer makes the card feel flatter, keep it. If it doesn't, cut it. Source: ShopPanelContent spotlight card 2026-04-17 — started with breathe + shimmer, dropped shimmer after realizing the sparkles + hover sheen already carry the motion budget.

- **2026-04-17:** **Ambient motion with `@keyframes` on a `swap-@version`-keyed parent IS safe.** CLAUDE.md warns `@keyframes` only play once on mount, not on panel re-open. But if the parent element uses a version-keyed class (e.g. `items-scroll.swap-@swapVersion`) that forces a remount on state change, the child's `@keyframes` animation re-fires fresh each remount. So for the spotlight card — which is a child of the swapVersion-keyed scroll area and thus re-mounts on category/sort change — `@keyframes spotlightBreathe 4.5s ease-in-out infinite` works as expected. This is distinct from the modal-open case where the panel stays in the DOM; re-mount via version key is a valid pattern for child animations. Source: ShopPanelContent `.featured-ambient-glow` 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **`linear-gradient(180deg, ...)` is mis-parsed as left→right by s&box in some element geometries.** Found while adding a vertical scroll-fade overlay to the shop: `background: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(5,5,12,0) 0%, rgba(5,5,12,0.98) 100%);` rendered as a LEFT-to-RIGHT sweep instead of TOP-to-BOTTOM. Substituting the keyword form `linear-gradient(to bottom, ...)` renders correctly. The roster uses `180deg` successfully in other places — the bug appears to be element-dimension-dependent (tall-narrow vs wide-short), though root cause isn't pinned down. **Defensive rule: prefer `to top` / `to bottom` / `to left` / `to right` keyword forms over angles for simple axis-aligned gradients.** Reserve `Ndeg` form for actually-diagonal gradients (like 135deg button gradients, where it already works). Source: ShopPanelContent `.scroll-fade-bottom` 2026-04-17, user-reported + manually fixed before agent handoff.

- **2026-04-17:** **One-shot card-pulse via conditional overlay + version key.** s&box CSS transitions on class toggles re-fire on every add/remove — but a `@keyframes` animation defined on a persistent element only plays once. To fire a one-shot pulse on every click of a persistent card: (1) track a `selectPulseVersion` counter that increments on each click, (2) track `justSelectedItemId` to know which card is the target, (3) conditionally render `<div class="select-pulse pulse-@selectPulseVersion" />` as a child only when `item.Id == justSelectedItemId`, (4) style `.select-pulse` with a `@keyframes cardSelectPulse 0.4s ease-out forwards` that scales + fades from 0→1→0. The overlay mounts fresh each click (the version key ensures the DOM node changes), so keyframes fire. Use for handshake feedback — card confirms click while detail pane fades in. Source: ShopPanelContent `.select-pulse` 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Directional slide transitions between sibling views via render-key + direction class.** When a panel has two main views (e.g. gold ↔ boss shop toggle) and you want the new view to slide in from a direction appropriate to the toggle direction: (1) track the last switch direction in C# (`"forward"` / `"back"` / `""` for initial), (2) emit both a render-key class AND a direction class on the container: `<div class="shop-layout layout-@swapVersion dir-@viewSwitchDir">`, (3) define three keyframes in SCSS — `shopLayoutEntrance` (default fade for initial mount), `shopLayoutSlideForward` (translateX 40px → 0), `shopLayoutSlideBack` (translateX -40px → 0). The render-key remounts the container so the direction's @keyframes fires fresh each toggle. Keep slides short (0.34s) with ease-out so content settles fast — longer slides feel sluggish when the player is scanning. Source: ShopPanelContent `.shop-layout` gold↔boss swap 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`@keyframes` animation on an element with `animation-delay` DOES fire in s&box.** Verified by staggering `.shop-section-title.stagger-0/1/2/3` with `animation-delay: 0s/0.06s/0.12s/0.18s` each pointing at the same `sectionEntrance` keyframe — all four sections enter in stepped sequence. Previous learnings flagged `transition-delay` as broken but animation-delay is fine. Use explicit numbered stagger classes (not `:nth-child`) since selector-based staggering is less reliable in s&box. Cap stagger at ~4 steps / 240ms spread — any longer and dense grids create a visible pause before late sections arrive. Source: ShopPanelContent `.shop-section-title` stagger 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`ShopPanelContent` is a tab-switched child inside `GameHUD.razor` — ShopPanelContent remounts each time the shop tab opens.** This means `@keyframes` on the panel root (e.g. `animation: shopPanelEntrance 0.35s ease-out both` on `.shop-panel-content`) DO fire on every tab open. Other tab-switched panels that live inside GameHUD's `switch (currentTab)` pattern have the same property — you can rely on first-mount keyframes for tab entrance animations without a version key. Contrast: modal panels that toggle `IsVisible` on a persistent DOM element (DailyPanel-style) do NOT remount, and need either a `pop-@version` render-key OR transition-based reveal. Know which you're dealing with before deciding. Source: ShopPanelContent entrance animation 2026-04-17 + GameHUD.razor:169.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **Razor `@if / else if / else` chains that swap entire `<iconify>`/`<span>` child blocks can leave ghost children during rapid state flips in s&box.** User-reported bug on the Shop panel: spam-clicking Purchase caused the buy-button to briefly render BOTH the default `lucide:shopping-bag` icon AND the `lucide:check` icon with "Purchase" text between them. The four branches (`IsPurchaseFlash` / `!CanAfford` / `!lv` / else) were mutually exclusive in logic but s&box's Razor diff didn't cleanly swap the icon child between frames during rapid re-renders. **Fix: compute a single `(icon, label, color, size)` tuple in code-behind and render ONE `<iconify>` + ONE `<span>` with interpolated attributes** — s&box only swaps attribute values on re-render, not child slots, which avoids the DOM-thrash race. Pattern: `GetBuyButtonContent(item) => (string, string, string, int)` returning the right glyph + label based on state. Belt-and-suspenders: add `pointer-events: none` via an `.is-flashing` class during the flash window so rapid clicks can't re-enter even before C# guards fire. Source: ShopPanelContent.razor buy-btn fix 2026-04-17, user bug report.

- **2026-04-17:** **Multi-buy pattern: keep ShopManager single-unit, loop in the UI.** Adding qty picker to the Shop for stackable items (ContractInk + boss consumables). Tried two approaches mentally: (1) add a new `PurchaseItemBatch(id, qty)` API, (2) call single-unit `PurchaseItem` N times from the UI. Went with (2): simplest, no data-layer churn, HUD currency roll-up and mission tracking both already fire per-unit so no special-casing needed. Atomicity: break the loop on first failed `SpendGold`/`SpendBossTokens` and report partial success (`bought` counter). Sound fires ONCE per multi-buy (Click + Success + GoldReward layered), plus 1-3 extra chimes capped at 3 for qty ≥ 10/20/30+ — anti-annoyance. Particle count scales `Math.Min(30, 10 + qty)`, flyer scales `1.0/1.3/1.6` at qty thresholds `1/10/50`. Reset `purchaseQty = 1` on successful multi-buy + on every selection / view / category / sort change so a stale count never leaks into a singleton's affordability. Source: ShopPanelContent multi-buy feature 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Audit lesson: every selection-reset code path also needs qty reset.** In the Shop multi-buy feature, `purchaseQty` was the new field, and it had to be reset in SEVEN separate code paths to avoid stale-qty bugs: `SelectItem`, `SelectTheme`, `SelectTitle`, `SelectConsumable`, `SelectCategory`, `SelectBossCategory`, `CycleGoldSort`, `CycleBossSort`, `SwitchShopView`. Rule of thumb: if a panel has multiple mutually-exclusive selection states and you're adding a new piece of per-selection state, grep for every existing `selectedX = null` / `hoveredX = null` / `swapVersion++` site and add the reset there too. Missing even one creates a ghost-state bug that only appears under specific nav paths. Source: ShopPanelContent qty-picker 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Stable-DOM alternative to the shop's tuple-helper ghost-child fix.** When a button's @if/else needs to swap between DIFFERENT tag types (e.g. `<img>` vs `<iconify>`, which can't be unified into one element), the tuple helper doesn't apply cleanly. Fallback pattern: **always mount both children, toggle visibility via a state class on the parent**. Concretely: set `@class="... @(hasX ? "has-x" : "no-x")"` on the button, render both `<img>` and `<iconify>` as always-present children, and in SCSS use `.parent.has-x .imgSlot { display: block } .parent.has-x .iconifySlot { display: none }` (and inverse on `.no-x`). The DOM stays stable across state changes — s&box only re-evaluates a class attribute, not a child-slot diff — so no ghost-child leak. Applied to `MonsterRosterPanel.razor` `.action-v2.item` (held-item button) and `MonsterDetailPanel.razor` `.action-btn.item` 2026-04-17. Trade-off: minor SCSS cost (8 extra lines per button), a broken-src image briefly mounted but hidden. Guard against broken-image fallback with `onerror="this.style.display='none'"` on the `<img>`. Source: cross-panel audit following shop ghost-child fix.

- **2026-04-17:** **Lesson 3 (glow halos inside scroll containers) applies to the `.current` evolution-stage portraits in Beastbook.** `BeastiaryPanel.razor.scss:1779-1789` painted 22px blur colored element halos at 0.42-0.45 alpha on `.bb-evo-stage.current .bb-evo-portrait`, which lives inside `.bb-detail-scroll` (overflow-y: scroll). s&box does NOT clip outer box-shadow against scroll containers, so these halos would leak past the viewport onto the page when the evo row scrolls into/out of view. Removed the colored halo — kept the 0.9-alpha colored BORDER + dark offset drop shadow (`0 4px 14px rgba(0,0,0,0.55)`) + the existing `transform: translateY(-2px) scale(1.05)` hero lift on `&.current`. Those carry the "you are here" signal cleanly. Pattern matches how the Beastbook grid `.selected` card bug was fixed earlier. Source: Cross-panel audit 2026-04-17 applying shop-pass Lesson 3.

- **2026-04-17:** **Audit negatives matter — don't over-apply lessons.** During the roster/detail/beastbook robustness pass, the seven shop-pass lessons were candidate fixes. Clean outcomes: only 3 of 7 had real targets (Lesson 1 ghost-child on 2 buttons, Lesson 3 glow-in-scroll on evo portraits). Lessons 2/4/5/6/7 had ZERO applicable targets: (a) all `linear-gradient(180deg, ...)` matches were on tall-or-square elements where the misparse doesn't trigger, (b) the `flex-shrink: 0 + width: Npx` pattern without `flex: 0 0 Npx` is proven-working in roster/beastbook detail panes, (c) no parent-overflow-hidden + child-height-100% starvation, (d) headers already use `margin-left: auto`, (e) no stray backslash-comment lines. Explicit "no target found" is a valuable audit outcome — prevents cargo-culting fixes onto unrelated code. Rule for future audits: every grep hit must be VERIFIED in context before editing; a text match is a candidate, not a bug. Source: Cross-panel audit 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **Avoid CSS attribute selectors like `[class*="foo-"]` in s&box — use a dedicated state class instead.** Inherited pattern from SkillTreePanel's `.hex-body[class*="unlock-"]` targeted any body with a `unlock-N` version-keyed class. Attribute selectors aren't documented as supported in s&box's CSS engine and carry silent-drop risk. The safer idiom for "fire a keyframe whenever the version increments" is: (a) mount a **separate overlay element** (`<div class="hex-unlock-pulse pulse-@N" />`) that is conditionally rendered only during the pulse window, the overlay naturally unmounts/remounts on each invest and its `@keyframes` fires fresh each time, OR (b) toggle a plain state class (`.invested`) on the existing element whose `@keyframes` fires once — combined with a changing version key on the same element to force remount on repeat invests (`class="hex-body invested bump-@N"`). Both patterns are Razor-idiomatic and avoid the attribute-selector risk. Source: SkillTreePanel rework 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Colored box-shadow halos on "available" state hex nodes leak past `overflow: hidden` skill-body.** Same class of leak as the Beastbook evo-stage portraits (learning above). SkillTreePanel's inherited `.hex-body.available` had `box-shadow: 0 0 14px rgba(branchColor, 0.35)` on 5 hex variants, and the enclosing `.skill-body` is `overflow: hidden` to prevent cluster column overflow. s&box does not clip descendant box-shadow against `overflow: hidden` either — these halos WILL leak into the header strip and outside the panel bounds. Fix: remove colored halos, carry the "clickable" signal through a stronger branch-colored border (`border-width: 2px; border-color: #branchPrimary`) + a tinted bg fill. Same treatment applied to `.capstone-glow` — rewritten from external 28px colored halo to a contained `radial-gradient(circle, gold, rgba(branch, 0))` INSIDE the hex with capstone pulse animation on opacity, so the glow never extends past the hex bounds. Source: SkillTreePanel rework 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **SkillTreePanel — canonical hex-cluster pattern.** Each branch is a `.branch-cluster` column (260px wide flex 0 0 260px), 5 of them side-by-side with 12px gap inside `.clusters-row`. Each cluster contains: (1) `.branch-header` with branch-tinted top-bar gradient + name + branch-points pill + optional gold crown when mastered, (2) `.tier-gates` thin strip showing T1 (always met) / T2·5 / T3·10 with green state when threshold crossed, (3) `.cluster-canvas` at `position: relative; height: 360px` with each hex absolutely positioned via `node.GridX/GridY` (cluster-local pixel coords), (4) `.branch-footer` tagline. Nodes use `.hex-slot` wrapper (58x58, position absolute) → `.hex-body` (52x52 rounded-square with `border-radius: 12px` — approximates hex without clip-path which s&box doesn't support). Tier-gate reveal via `transition: opacity 0.4s` with `stagger-0..3` classes adding `transition-delay: 0/80/160/240ms`. One-shot unlock pulse via conditional overlay child with `pulse-@version` key. Capstone setpiece glow as contained radial-gradient INSIDE the hex (not external halo). Rank badge absolutely positioned bottom-right, branch-colored border. Source: SkillTreePanel `Code/UI/Panels/SkillTreePanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-17 rework.

- **2026-04-17:** **MainMenu title-screen rework — two-column layout + ambient motes + P5-style button accents + circular starter ring.** Task was "redesign title screen to not feel stagnant" referencing Flow (drift) + Persona 5 (kinetic accents). Key patterns worth reusing: (1) **Hero/button two-column layout** — logo drifts 62% left, button column pinned 6% from right (380px wide). Gives the background room to read. (2) **Ambient element motes** — 7 fixed-color `<div>`s with `box-shadow` blur + three shared `@keyframes mote-drift-a/b/c` tracks (26-40s durations, varied `animation-delay` per mote). Pure CSS, fires once on mount which is fine because the main menu panel remounts on each GameState.MainMenu entry. (3) **P5-style slide-in accent bar** — `.btn-accent` as an `position: absolute; right: -120px; width: 6px` child of each `.menu-btn` (which has `overflow: hidden`); hover transitions `right: 0`. Color varies per button (purple for standard, gold for PLAY, red for QUIT). Result: on hover a solid color bar snaps in from the right edge, very P5. (4) **Starter ring** — equilateral triangle (top/bottom-left/bottom-right) inside a `640×520` relative stage. Slots are `position: absolute` with explicit pixel positions and transforms for entrance. Hover = `scale(1.22)`, selected = `scale(1.40)` + element-tinted `radial-gradient(circle, ...)` ring-glow (circle not ellipse — s&box rejects ellipse). Dimmed siblings use single-function `filter: brightness(0.6)` — cannot chain filters in s&box. (5) **Silhouette backdrop layer** — artist-provided `_whiteoutline.png` variants rendered as low-opacity (0.07-0.13) ambient decor with slow drift `@keyframes`. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor(.scss)` rework 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **MainMenu cohesion pass — shared-axis centering beats two-column split.** Earlier pass had logo on 55% left column + buttons pinned `right: 6%` in a 380px right column. Each was fine in isolation but read as two disconnected islands — their vertical centers didn't align, a huge dead middle zone opened between them, and the eye had nothing to rest on. Fix: **stack logo and buttons on a shared vertical axis through screen center.** Logo at `top: 14%; left: 50%; width: 760px; margin-left: -380px`; buttons at `top: 44%; left: 50%; width: 360px; margin-left: -180px`. Now logo and PLAY button share the same vertical centerline, and the eye path is top→down instead of left↔right. Separately: **LANG is a toggle, not a destination** — demoted from full 46px button to a 30px `.menu-chip` utility row alongside QUIT. PLAY / TUTORIAL / ROADMAP are the true primary/secondary stack. **Bottom chrome unified to one visual language** — Discord / Event / Version all now share 32px height, 8px radius, 1px border, same dark bg token; only border color differentiates (discord blue, event purple, version neutral). Event toast slimmed from 66px heavy notification to a peer chrome pill. **Tighter halo around logo** (560×260 from 820×420) stops the glow from competing with the wordmark. **Added `.menu-content-wash`** — a `radial-gradient(circle at 50% 52%, rgba(4,6,16,0.72), ..., rgba(4,6,16,0))` over the monster bg pattern, concentrated under the content axis so bg pattern recedes where content lives but still reads at the page edges. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor(.scss)` cohesion pass 2026-04-17. Cohesion principle: **if two hero elements aren't on a shared axis, they aren't cohabitating — they're just in the same window.**

- **2026-04-17:** **MainMenu Persona accent-bar idle state MUST be invisible, not off-screen-with-hope.** Initial design had `.btn-accent { right: -120px }` at rest and `right: 0` on hover, relying on `.menu-btn { overflow: hidden }` to clip the off-canvas resting position. In screenshots the accent bars leaked into the 10px gap BETWEEN buttons as vertical stripes — s&box's `overflow: hidden` does NOT reliably clip absolutely-positioned descendants (this matches the known scroll-container quirk: "scroll containers do NOT clip descendant box-shadow / transform / edge-overhanging children"). Belt-and-suspenders fix: set the idle state to `opacity: 0; transform: translateX(6px)` FLUSH against the button's right edge (`right: 0`), and animate `opacity: 1; transform: translateX(0)` on hover. Nothing paints in the gap regardless of how s&box handles clipping. Source: MainMenu.razor.scss 2026-04-17 polish pass.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **`overflow: hidden` on a `position: relative` flex container does NOT clip absolutely-positioned descendants in s&box.** Confirmed on `.menu-btn` with `overflow: hidden; position: relative` — an inner `.btn-accent { position: absolute; right: -120px }` rendered FULLY VISIBLE in the gap between buttons, despite the parent `overflow: hidden`. This is the same class of bug as the earlier-documented scroll-container quirk (`overflow: hidden` does not clip descendant box-shadow / transform / overhanging badges). Generalizing: **`overflow: hidden` in s&box is unreliable for clipping absolutely-positioned children, full stop.** If you need something hidden off the edge, use `opacity: 0` + `transform` at the child, or size the child to sit inside the parent's bounds. Never rely on parent `overflow: hidden` to hide an absolutely-positioned descendant that physically extends past the parent edges. Source: MainMenu accent-bar bug 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **MainMenu composition pass — layered atmospheric washes beat single-vignette backdrops.** Prior pass used ONE `.menu-content-wash` radial at center with rgba(4,6,16,0.72→0) combined with a `.menu-vignette` at 0.68 alpha — the overlap killed the bg pattern entirely (pitch-black bg bug). Better pattern: separate wash layers by role. (1) `.menu-vignette` at `0.55` solid — uniform dim that keeps bg pattern as a texture, NOT a blackout. (2) `.menu-content-wash` as a COLORED bloom behind the hero zone (purple at 0.18 center, falling off at 62% radius) — this gives the logo "placed on something" warmth without darkening anything. (3) `.menu-warm-wash` at the PLAY-button axis (gold at 0.12 center, 55% falloff) — separate layer so the purple hero halo and gold CTA halo don't have to compete inside the same radial stop. Three low-alpha wash layers > one high-alpha blackout. Keeps the bg pattern visible at ~20-30% everywhere while giving the central content zone atmospheric depth. Source: MainMenu composition pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Persona-style graphic rails flanking a button column give a static stack visual spine.** Thin vertical gradient rails (1px wide, purple→gold vertical gradient, 75% opacity) placed `left: -28px` and `right: -28px` on a 360px button column, terminated by 8px horizontal gold "caps" at the corners. Pure decorative chrome — no hover state, no animation beyond fade-in with the column. The caps are the Persona move: they make the rails read as an intentional frame instead of stray vertical lines. This was one of three composition upgrades to the main menu (along with layered atmospheric washes and a splash-text flourish) that fixed "feels characterless" — adding graphic elements, not animation, was the cure. Important: absolutely-positioned rails inside a flex-column parent work fine in s&box — they're removed from flow and positioned relative to the parent. Source: MainMenu composition pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17: [PROMOTE]** **Impact vs style lesson on the MainMenu — the answer was graphic structure, not more motion.** User feedback "needs much more improvement" came after a pass that had added warmth washes and animations. My instinct was to add MORE animation (ambient motes were already killed once). Correct read: the menu was FLAT GRAPHICALLY, not motion-starved. Persona 5's signature isn't hover animations — it's bold graphic frames, rails, decorative caps, typographic flourishes. Fix was thin rails flanking the button column, short horizontal decorative flourishes flanking the splash text, and a fading horizontal rule tying the bottom-chrome pills together. Zero new animations. The menu immediately read as "designed" rather than "assembled". Rule of thumb: when a screen feels characterless, try GRAPHIC CHROME (lines, caps, frames, typographic rules) before trying MORE MOTION. Static visual design has a high ceiling that agent-brain under-samples. Source: MainMenu feedback loop 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Inline two-stage confirm pattern — replace destructive-action modals with in-place button state machines.** User asked for the skill-tree "Reset all skills" modal (full backdrop + card + Cancel/Reset buttons) to be replaced with an inline flow on the button itself. Resulting primitive has three states rendered from a tuple-returning helper (mirrors `ShopPanelContent.GetBuyButtonContent` to dodge the Razor `@if/else` ghost-child bug): **default** (red rotate-ccw icon + "Reset"), **armed** (amber alert-triangle icon + "Click again to confirm" + pulsing amber glow overlay), **flashing** (green check icon + "Reset!" + scale-up payoff). Essential plumbing: (1) `resetConfirming: bool` state + `resetConfirmExpires: float` (RealTime.Now timestamp) + `resetFlashUntil: float` + `resetBtnVersion: int` render-key. (2) `Tick()` clears `resetConfirming` when `RealTime.Now > resetConfirmExpires` (3-second default window — long enough to decide, short enough that a misclick doesn't trap). (3) Click-elsewhere escape: panel-level `onclick` handler disarms, but the button's own onclick runs first and sets `resetClickGuardUntil = RealTime.Now + 0.1f` to block the immediate-bubble race (child handler runs first, then bubbles to parent — without the guard you arm-then-immediately-disarm in the same event). (4) BuildHash MUST include `resetConfirming`, `IsResetFlashing`, `resetBtnVersion`. (5) Confirmed click uses `SoundManager.PlayBack()` not `PlaySuccess()` — destructive action, audio should read as "undoing progress" not "something gained". Result is much lower friction than a modal: no attention-break, no cancel-button to aim at, player keeps scanning the skill tree throughout. Source: SkillTreePanel inline-reset task 2026-04-17. [PROMOTE] — this is a reusable primitive for any destructive header-button action (delete card, release monster bulk, reset setting).

- **2026-04-17:** **Armed-pulse infinite glow MUST mount as a conditional child overlay, not as a class-driven animation on a persistent button.** s&box `@keyframes` play once on DOM mount — flipping `.armed` class onto an already-mounted button does NOT start the pulse animation reliably. Correct pattern: wrap the button in a flow where the pulse is its own element — `@if (resetConfirming) { <div class="reset-armed-glow" /> }` as a child of the button. The glow is conditionally mounted, so each time `resetConfirming` flips to true the overlay mounts fresh and its infinite `@keyframes` starts clean. When `resetConfirming` flips false, the overlay unmounts. Pointer-events off on the glow so clicks still hit the parent button. Button solid-color shifts (bg / border / text color) can stay on the `.armed` class because those transitions re-fire cleanly — only the keyframed pulse needs the mount trick. Source: SkillTreePanel inline-reset task 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Canonical one-shot select-pulse pattern (shop → roster → beastbook).** Card-click feedback is now a shared primitive across the three big grid panels. Required ingredients every time: (1) two C# fields — `justSelectedXId` (nullable-matching-id-type) + `selectPulseVersion` (int); (2) a razor conditional `@if (justSelectedXId == card.id) { <div class="select-pulse pulse-@selectPulseVersion"></div> }` as a direct child of the `position: relative` card root; (3) increment both fields in the click handler — `justSelectedXId = card.id; selectPulseVersion++;`; (4) SCSS `.select-pulse { position: absolute; top/left/right/bottom: 0 (or -2px to bleed over edges where the parent has no overflow:hidden); border: 2px solid <accent>; border-radius: <match-card>; pointer-events: none; opacity: 0; animation: pulseName 0.42s ease-out forwards; z-index: high; }` plus `@keyframes { 0%: opacity 0 scale 0.94; 40%: opacity 1 scale 1.03; 100%: opacity 0 scale 1.08 }`; (5) BuildHash MUST include BOTH fields or re-click never repaints (common s&box trap). The `pulse-@selectPulseVersion` class is cosmetically unused but it's the render key that forces a fresh DOM mount — s&box only plays @keyframes on initial mount, so keying on the version is what makes it re-fire. **Parent overflow matters:** if the card has `overflow: hidden` (like `.bb-card`), use `top/left/right/bottom: 0` (flush) and accept that outward-scale is clipped to card shape — that's actually on-brand for the scroll-clip guardrail. If the parent doesn't clip (like `.monster-card-wrapper`), use `-2px` bleed so the pulse edges into the gap. Ports: `ShopPanelContent.razor` → `MonsterRosterPanel.razor` (purple `#8b5cf6` at 0.85α, 12px radius matching `MonsterCard`) → `BeastiaryPanel.razor` (rarity-tier colored border that flows the beast's rarity class through to the overlay — rare blue, epic purple, legendary gold, mythic pink, with purple/lavender fallback for common/uncommon). 420ms duration is the tested sweet spot — longer feels sluggish, shorter feels twitchy. Source: Shop-pattern port 2026-04-17. [PROMOTE] — this is now the standard feedback primitive for any grid card click across the game.

- **2026-04-17:** **Steam avatars via `<img src="avatar:@steamId" />` — canonical for per-player imagery, replaces ad-hoc gender-avatar assets.** s&box exposes a native `avatar:` URL scheme that resolves to the Steam avatar for a given steam ID (see `ChatPanel.razor:65/103/153/238`, `LeaderboardPanel.razor:84/113`, `GuildPanel.razor:1237`). Pattern: `private long LocalSteamId => (long)(Connection.Local?.SteamId ?? 0);` + `<img src="avatar:@LocalSteamId" />`. For disconnected state (SteamId == 0) s&box shows a default silhouette. Display name pattern: `Connection.Local?.DisplayName ?? "Fallback"`. **Design note on where to put the avatar:** when rendering multiple cards tied to one Steam player (e.g. save slots), putting the same avatar on every card is redundant. Better pattern: "Signed in as [avatar] [name]" strip ONCE at the top of the selector, then each card differentiates via its OWN content (slot number, starter beast, stats). Used in MainMenu save-slot takeover 2026-04-17 — replaced per-slot gender-avatar images with a single signed-in header + beast-driven slot identity. Source: MainMenu save-slot redesign 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`overflow: hidden` on a flex CHILD container (even with `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0;`) can still collapse it to zero width in s&box.** The well-known quirk is "overflow:hidden collapses flex children" — it applies even when the child has proper `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0;` sizing. I tried this on a news-ticker `.ticker-content { flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0; overflow: hidden; }` meant to clip overflowing text during slide-in — the entire ticker content disappeared. Removed `overflow: hidden` and the layout stayed fine; any over-width content just lives in-flow (rarely visible on a fixed-width 460px ticker anyway). Rule confirmed: **never put `overflow: hidden` on a flex child** — even with explicit sizing, s&box collapses it. For clipping needs on flex children, consider (a) trusting the parent's bounds since s&box doesn't anti-alias past the parent rect anyway, or (b) using a fixed `width: Npx` instead of `flex: 1 1 0`. Source: MainMenu news-ticker 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Full-screen takeover vs modal-overlay: use `fading` class parity.** Rule of thumb for slot/flow selection UI on a title screen — if the current state is a "step in the main-flow" (pick a save, pick a starter, pick an expedition), use a full-screen takeover where the menu content fades out via the `.fading` class and the new UI replaces it inline. If the current state is a "detail dialog" (reset confirmation, what's-new tooltip, settings), use a bordered modal overlay centered on the darkened menu. The signal is ritual: takeover = "we are moving forward through the game's opening"; modal = "we are pausing for a confirmation". Applied to MainMenu: slot-select was a modal, now takeover (matches starter-select). Roadmap stays modal because it's informational. Reset-confirmation stays modal because it's a decision gate. Source: MainMenu slot-select redesign 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Pillar-card atmospheric frame (four corners) for title screens with marketing pillars.** When a game has a small number of named pillars/verbs (Beastborne's Tame/Fuse/Expedition/Ascend, or Monster Hunter's verbs), placing small atmospheric "pillar cards" in the four quadrants of the title screen does triple duty: (1) fills out the empty edges that otherwise read as dead space, (2) teaches new players what the game IS in 4 scannable phrases, (3) frames the central menu stack like a Persona graphic grid. Implementation: `.pillar-ring { position: absolute; top/left/width/height: 100% }` as a non-interactive parent, four cards positioned `top: 16% + left/right: 5%` and `bottom: 18% + left/right: 5%`. Each card is a 252×96 panel with a per-pillar color accent (small angled top-left stripe), an iconify icon (lucide), label ("PILLAR I"), title ("TAME"), one-line desc. Idle drift via @keyframes margin-top/bottom for gentle liveliness (11-14s cycles, 6-8px amplitude). Hover state lifts 3px + brightens icon. Non-clickable — atmosphere, not menu. Colors per pillar: Tame=gold, Fuse=purple, Expedition=blue, Ascend=pink. This is a big readability win: before, someone opening the game saw "PLAY / TUTORIAL / ROADMAP / LANG / QUIT" and had zero context on what Beastborne IS. Now the four verbs are immediately visible. Source: MainMenu major pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Beastborne button language = warm bronze/copper bevel over deep navy-purple body.** User feedback: TUTORIAL / ROADMAP / LANG / QUIT felt "not Beastborne" — cold thin purple borders on dark pills read as "generic modern SaaS UI" against a logo that's all chunky warm bevel + fantasy weight. Fix crystallized into a three-tier button system that shares ONE bevel DNA. Core recipe for every non-hero menu button: (1) `background-color: rgba(16, 12, 36, 0.88)` deep navy-purple base, (2) `background-image: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(48, 34, 92, 0.55) 0%, rgba(10, 8, 26, 0.10) 100%)` subtle lit-from-above vertical gradient, (3) `border: 2px solid rgba(168, 114, 46, 0.55)` warm bronze rim, (4) `border-top-color: rgba(218, 162, 82, 0.80)` brighter copper-gold HIGHLIGHT on the top edge — this is the signature "Beastborne bevel" (ties back to the logo's warm top/dark bottom bevel), (5) cream text `rgba(255, 232, 196, 0.82)` with text-shadow darkness for depth, (6) hover heats the rim toward pure gold `rgba(236, 182, 98, 0.90)` + brightens the top highlight to `rgba(255, 218, 140, 0.95)` + shifts bg gradient purple a notch deeper. PLAY (hero) takes the gold variant of this (full gold rim at `rgba(255, 193, 7, 0.55)`), secondary buttons take the bronze base, utility chips take the bronze at thinner + lower alpha. Result: three button sizes, one visual family — all three read as cast-metal plates lit from above rather than software rectangles. **Why it works:** the bronze picks up the "BEAST" warm-orange/gold from the logo, the purple body picks up the "BORNE" dark-navy-blue. Every button is a compressed restatement of the logo's palette. Before: cold thin purple borders. After: warm chunky metal bevel that feels carved from the same world as the logo. Source: MainMenu BIG pass 2026-04-17. [PROMOTE] — this is the canonical Beastborne menu-button language; any future menu/utility button anywhere in the game should use this DNA instead of plain purple-bordered dark pills.

- **2026-04-17:** **"Make everything BIG" composition rule — target ~20-33% growth, not scale(1.05).** User feedback after a video-backdrop pass: "i really want everything to feel BIG!" — content felt dwarfed on a full-screen video bg. Lesson: don't just `transform: scale()` the whole stack (blurs sprites, breaks pixel alignment). Grow each element's NATIVE values. Rough ratios that worked across the main menu: logo `540→720px` (+33%), hero container `760→960px`, PLAY button `360×72→440×92` (+22%/+28%), secondary buttons `360×46→440×62` (+22%/+35% — height grew more because the old secondary was cramped), utility chips `30→40px` (+33%), news ticker `460×30→560×38` (+22%/+27%), splash text `14→18px` (+29%), bottom HUD pills `32→38px` (+19%). Fonts: primary label text `15→20px`, splash `14→18px`, chip text `11→13px`, ticker text `11→13px`. Letter-spacing scaled proportionally (`3px→4px` on secondary, `8px→10px` on PLAY). Border radii grew too (`12→14` on secondary, `14→16` on PLAY, `6→10` on chip). **Positioning adjustment:** also moved the hero UP (14%→8%) and button column slightly DOWN (44%→46%) to create more breathing room between logo and buttons at the new size — without this the stack felt crammed. **Rule of thumb:** when a user says "feels too small," don't `scale()` — grow every element's actual pixel values by ~25%, scale fonts + letter-spacing proportionally, THEN check composition breathing room and adjust positions to re-center. A big logo that's squashed against a big button feels smaller than two medium elements with 200px of breath between them. Source: MainMenu BIG pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`radial-gradient(circle, ...)` error was persisting from MonsterDetailPanel, not MainMenu.** User reported `Cannot read a color from 'circle'` still logging at menu time despite a prior pass removing 9 instances from MainMenu.razor.scss. Root cause: the SCSS parser logs errors for ALL loaded stylesheets, not just the one mounted. `Code/UI/Panels/MonsterDetailPanel.razor.scss:624, 692` still had `radial-gradient(circle, rgba(...), ...)` even though the panel is orphaned (not mounted anywhere per panel-inventory — grep for `<MonsterDetailPanel` returns zero references). The SCSS still gets bundled + parsed at boot. Fix: drop the `circle` keyword from both entries. **Lesson:** when a runtime CSS error persists after fixing the "obvious" source file, grep the ENTIRE `Code/UI/` tree — even orphaned panel SCSS runs through the parser. The error message gives NO hint about which file it came from. Also: MonsterDetailPanel remains a ghost file — candidate for deletion after confirmation, but keeping in sync with roster-panel move-picker patterns for now. Source: MainMenu BIG pass 2026-04-17, error hunting phase.

- **2026-04-17:** **Bg compositing for title screens — pattern opacity ≥ video opacity, not the reverse.** Main-menu had `pattern opacity: 0.35` + `video opacity: 0.85`, which meant the purple bokeh video effectively REPLACED the chibi beast pattern. User immediately perceived "multiple art styles" — abstract video bokeh reads as a different aesthetic than hand-painted chibi. Fix: the pattern (game's visual DNA) stays at 0.55-0.65 so the silhouettes read through, and the video drops to 0.40-0.45 as an atmospheric MOTION layer on top. Rule: **for compositing a game-art backdrop with a generic motion video, the game-art layer must be ≥ the motion layer's alpha** so the game's identity dominates the frame. Video becomes "ambient purple weather" on top of the beasts, not a replacement layer. Confirmed visually on MainMenu 2026-04-17 PM — once pattern opacity was raised to 0.62 and video dropped to 0.42, the "multiple art styles" feedback was resolved and the menu read as Beastborne with motion, not purple-particle-generic-video. Source: MainMenu creative pass 2026-04-17.
