- **2026-04-17:** **Button bevel = three absolutely-positioned decorative divs, not CSS `box-shadow: inset` (which isn't supported).** Chunky Beastborne logo-style buttons need: (1) warm cream highlight BAND just inside the top border (mimics the logo's beveled top-lit feel), (2) dark inner-rim 1px line tucked inside the colored border (double-struck cartoon outline feel), (3) optional pointer/selector mark that slides in on keyboard-selected state. s&box doesn't allow `box-shadow: inset`, `inset` shorthand, or per-side border shorthands with `style` keyword — so the cleanest way to stack these is via three child divs: `.btn-bevel { position: absolute; top: 3px; left: 10px; right: 10px; height: 2px; background-color: rgba(255, 228, 170, 0.30) }` (the highlight band), `.btn-inner-rim { position: absolute; top/left/right/bottom: 0; border: 1px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55); border-radius: 11px }` (dark inner edge), `.btn-selector { position: absolute; left: 12px; width/height: 12px; background-color: #daa252; transform: translateX(-10px) rotate(45deg) }` (diamond pointer). The button itself keeps its colored border + multi-stop background gradient. Hover state brightens each layer independently via `.btn.entered:hover .btn-bevel { background-color: rgba(255, 244, 200, 0.55) }`. Works cleanly in s&box; reads as a hand-painted logo-style button instead of a flat rectangle with a color border. Source: MainMenu big creative pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **`.menu-btn` in column with `align-items: stretch` forces ALL buttons to match parent width.** If PLAY button is sized wider than secondary buttons (460 vs 440), `stretch` will either shrink/grow PLAY to match the column width. Use `align-items: center` + explicit `width: Npx` on each button instead — each button keeps its declared width, and flexbox centers them horizontally. The column's `width` should equal the widest child. Source: MainMenu PLAY-vs-secondary width mismatch 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Featured beast portrait at ~0.22 opacity is a better "monsters live here" signal than whiteoutline silhouettes.** Existing `.ambient-silhouette` elements use `whiteoutline` sprites at `opacity: 0.06` with `filter: brightness(2.2)` — they read as ghost-outline-art, decorative but abstract. Adding a SECOND layer of FULL-COLOR starter art at `opacity: 0.18-0.22` drifting on the title screen edges is a different signal: it shows the player actual painted beast art (lit, textured, with wing detail, fire glow, etc.), telling them "this game HAS real monsters you'll see up close" in a way ghost outlines can't. Two layers coexist fine: silhouettes are atmospheric outline texture, featured-beast is a sample of the actual game art. Positioning: one on right edge near the bottom (large, anchoring), one on left edge higher up (smaller, asymmetric bookend). Drift animations with slow rotation (26-32s cycles) keep them alive. Source: MainMenu creative pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Slot-card fallback mark should be thematic (lucide:flame in warm bronze), not generic (lucide:user-round in purple).** The previous slot-card fallback for non-active saves showed `lucide:user-round` in soft purple — read as "user account" UI, clashing with the Beastborne fantasy theme. Replaced with `lucide:flame` in warm bronze (`#daa252`) sitting in a painted navy circle with a bronze rim + cream inner ring (echoes the `.btn-bevel` pattern). Reads as "this save is waiting for a beast" rather than "this is an empty user profile". Rule: **for any save/slot/profile UI, avoid generic user/account iconography — always use a game-world icon (paw, flame, beast silhouette, spirit mark) in the game's accent color.** Source: MainMenu slot-card polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Chrome element unification — all bottom-HUD pills (discord, version, update, event) should share the bronze-rim border family.** Prior pass had 4 different border colors on the bottom HUD: discord (Discord-blue), version (neutral white), update-badge (gold), event-toast (purple). Reading the row: four separate islands, no unity. Fix: standardize the AT-REST border to bronze (`rgba(168, 114, 46, 0.38-0.45)`) with a warmer top-edge (`rgba(224, 168, 90, 0.52-0.78)`) across all four. HOVER states keep role-specific colors (discord → blue on hover, event → purple on hover, update → gold on hover). Idle reads as ONE unified bronze chrome row; hover feedback still differentiates affordance. Matches the menu-button bronze-rim family so the whole menu reads as one visual vocabulary. Source: MainMenu creative pass 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Per-slot element-tinted fallback mark > single-color fallback.** Previous pass upgraded slot-card fallback to a single warm bronze flame (all three slots identical). User feedback: still reads as placeholder. Better pattern is a slot-UNIQUE element mark: Slot 0 = fire (gold `#fbbf24` flame), Slot 1 = water (blue `#60a5fa` droplet), Slot 2 = wind (green `#4ade80` wind icon). Each mark gets a matching element-tinted radial wash inside the circle + element-colored rim + soft element glow shadow. Rule: **when a UI shows 3+ slots of "the same thing waiting to be filled", give each slot a distinct element color pulled from the game's palette**. Makes three empty slots feel like "three different bonds" vs. "three clones of the same placeholder", and subtly teases the starter trio to new players. Source: MainMenu polish pass 2026-04-17 (second pass).

- **2026-04-17:** **Idle-animation budget rule — count infinite `@keyframes` on a screen before adding more.** The main menu had ten concurrent infinite animations at idle (nebula-shift, halo-breath, logo-float, play-breath, three presence-drifts, ticker-badge-pulse, update-badge-pulse, plus conditional toast-pulse). Each was intentional individually; together they produced ambient visual noise that diluted the PLAY button (the actual Tier-2/3 hero animation the page should revolve around). Killed `ticker-badge-pulse` (2.2s border flicker — redundant with gold text + iconify) and `update-badge-pulse` (4s border flicker — competed with PLAY breath for "look here" gold signal). Rule of thumb: **a title screen should have ≤ 5 concurrent infinite animations**, and ideally only one of them should be the hero. If a breath/pulse is on chrome (ticker badge, version corner, utility pill), ask whether it's carrying identity/affordance OR whether solid styling could carry it — if styling suffices, drop the animation. Restraint on ambient cadence makes hero animations land harder. Source: MainMenu audit 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **UX rule: the DATA in a label/value pair should be visually heavier than the LABEL.** MainMenu `.version-badge` had `ALPHA` label at gold 0.72 alpha / weight 800 + `v0.8.0` value at white 0.50 alpha / weight 700 — the role-tag was SHOUTING and the actual data was a whisper. Flipped: label is a subdued bronze context chip (0.62 alpha, 10px), value is cream at the menu's canonical `rgba(255, 232, 196, 0.82)` / weight 800 / 12px. Same principle applies anywhere role-labels accompany actual data — "LVL 12", "GOLD 4.2K", "ENDS IN 2h". The number/value is the payload; the label is the context. If the label out-shouts the value, the user has to work harder than necessary to extract the actual info. Source: MainMenu audit 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Cream-on-bronze beats white-on-bronze for headline text in Beastborne-styled modals.** MainMenu had bronze-rim popup frames (roadmap, starter-overlay, slot-select) with pure `#ffffff` headline text — looked "generic SaaS modal header bolted onto a Beastborne frame". Switching the title color from `#ffffff` to `rgba(255, 232, 196, 0.96)` (cream) + swapping the text-shadow from cool purple glow to warm bronze glow (`0 0 18px rgba(224, 168, 90, 0.22)`) made the title read as "born in the same palette as the frame". Applied to `.roadmap-title`, `.starter-title`, `.ring-center-name`. Related: `.panel-header-line` under the title should also be bronze (not purple) so the underline matches the title warmth. Rule: **whenever a frame uses the bronze-bevel border family, the headline inside it should use cream + bronze-glow, never pure white + purple-glow.** Source: MainMenu audit 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Contrast by pulling DOWN siblings, not pushing UP the target, when filter chaining isn't available.** Starter ring-slot selected state originally used `scale(1.20)` + `filter: drop-shadow(...)` — but on a busy purple video backdrop the dimmed siblings at `opacity: 0.35` / `filter: brightness(0.6)` still competed. You can't stack `brightness(1.1)` on the selected ring-slot because its filter slot is occupied by the warm drop-shadow (s&box only accepts ONE filter function). Fix: push the UNSELECTED siblings deeper — opacity `0.35 → 0.25`, brightness `0.6 → 0.5`. Same visual goal ("selected stands out"), achieved by widening the gap instead of raising the target. Rule: **on busy/high-contrast backdrops where filter-chaining is off the table, dimming siblings more aggressively is often a bigger win than boosting the selected element**. Source: MainMenu polish pass 2026-04-17 (second pass).

- **2026-04-17:** **Stacking ambient art layers — pattern + silhouettes + featured trio — exceeded the noise threshold.** Earlier in the session I added three full-color starter whiteoutline overlays (Wispryn/Embrik/Pagefin) drifting on the main menu edges as "consistent ambient presence" (learning L633 above). After living with it, user reverted: *"maybe we don't have the trio in the main screen… i feel like it's all too busy."* Root cause: main menu already had (a) beast-pattern bg with chibi silhouettes, (b) ambient whiteoutline silhouettes at 0.06, (c) nebula wash, (d) video bokeh — adding a FOURTH identifiable-beast layer pushed it from "alive" to "cluttered". Rule: **when a title screen already shows monster silhouettes via the bg pattern, adding a second monster-sprite layer (even at low opacity) is redundant** — the pattern bg alone carries the "monsters live here" signal. Reserve featured-beast overlays for screens where the bg isn't already doing that job. This amends L633: the "featured beast at 0.22 vs. silhouettes at 0.06" comparison was right in isolation, but when the bg *also* shows beasts, stacking both reads as noise, not depth. Source: user revert on MainMenu 2026-04-17 self-audit.

- **2026-04-17:** **`transition: all` audit — most MainMenu uses were animating 2-5 properties; only one (.event-toast) was dead code.** Replaced ten `transition: all 0.2s ease` with explicit property lists. Typical hover change sets: bronze-rim chrome (update-badge, discord-link, roadmap-close) → `background-color, border-color, border-top-color [, box-shadow]`; big CTAs (slot-confirm, reset-confirm, back-btn) → adds `transform` (translateY on hover); reset-cancel → adds `color`; slot-reset-btn → adds `opacity` (fades in from 0 on parent card hover). `.event-toast` had `cursor: pointer` + `pointer-events: all` but no onclick handler AND no `:hover` rule — its `transition: all` was animating nothing, so dropped entirely (the `animation: toast-pulse` keyframes handle border-top-color independently). Lesson: **when auditing `transition: all`, look for elements with no `:hover` / `.selected` / `.active` rules — those are dead-code transitions, just delete them**. Source: MainMenu deferred-hygiene pass 2026-04-17.

---

## SkillTreePanel batch polish — 2026-04-17

- **2026-04-17:** **Connector-line pattern for prereq-chain visualization (rotated div, no SVG).** When two nodes have a parent→child gating relationship (keystone unlocks petals), draw the connection as an absolutely-positioned 2px-wide div anchored at the parent center, rotated to point at the child via `transform-origin: 50% 0%; transform: rotate(±θdeg)`. Math: `θ = atan2(dx, dy)` where dx/dy are parent-center → child-center. Length = `√(dx² + dy²)`. For a faint default state use `opacity: 0.7` with low-alpha gray bg; bump to `opacity: 1` + branch accent at full color when the prereq is met (active state). z-index: 0 keeps it BEHIND `.hex-slot` (z-index 1 needed) so the line "plugs into" the node visually. Cheaper than SVG path, no quirks. Reference: `Code/UI/Panels/SkillTreePanel.razor.scss:445-472` `.hex-connector`. Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Locked-state legibility wants TWO signals, not just opacity.** A 0.28 opacity dim was the only locked-petal indicator on the skill tree — at small icon sizes against the dark bg, "locked" read as "kind of dim, maybe just unselected?" Added a 12px lucide:lock corner overlay AND raised opacity to 0.4 — the lock icon carries the "gated" semantic, opacity becomes the secondary visual reinforcement instead of doing all the work. Pattern reusable for any "you can't interact with this yet" UI: opacity dim + a small icon (lock, padlock, snowflake for cooldown, etc.) is more legible than opacity alone. Bonus: pair the visual change with a hover sound swap (PlayDeny instead of PlayHover) so the audio confirms the gate too. Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Combined "X / Y unit" pill with internal progress fill > separate "spent" + "available" pills.** Original SkillTree header had `[SPENT 24]` and `[★ 24 SP AVAILABLE]` — two pills forcing the player to mentally add to figure out budget. Replaced with one `[★ 24 / 55 SP]` pill with a thin gold fill across the bottom 2px representing spent/total. Single chip, less header chrome, communicates both numbers AND progress in one glance. Implementation: `position: relative` on pill, `position: absolute; bottom: 0; height: 2px; width: <pct>%` for the fill, with `transition: width 0.35s ease-out` so it animates smoothly when SP is invested or refunded. Pattern generalizes to any "X out of Y total" UI (currency progress toward a cap, mastery-rank bars, achievement progress). Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Migration v3 mindset: only wipe what's necessary, preserve what survived.** Original skill-tree v2 migration wiped ALL ranks because the talent IDs changed during the launch-set redesign. v3 (this pass) is gentler: enumerate the player's existing `SkillRanks`, drop only the IDs that no longer exist in `SkillTree.CreateDefault()`, clamp surviving ranks to the new MaxRank, then recompute the SP pool as `totalEarned - spentOnPreserved`. Atomic and forgiving — playtest investment for talents that survived the cut is preserved instead of being slammed back to zero. Pattern for any future schema-change migration: prefer per-entry preservation + selective pruning over wholesale clear-and-regrant. The only time wholesale clear is correct is when the data shape itself changed (rank semantics flipped, currency type renamed). Source: `Code/Core/TamerManager.cs:177-225` `[Migration v2]` rewrite, SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Three-state count display (default / partial / complete) signals progression at a glance.** Branch SP counter `0 / 12` was white when partial, gold when mastered — "in progress" was undifferentiated from "untouched". Added a middle `partialBranch = !mastered && spent > 0 && spent < max` state in Razor that applies a `.partial` class, which tints the value to amber `#fde68a` (between cool-default white and mastered-gold `#fbbf24`). Subtle but communicates "you've started this branch, keep going." Pattern generalizes anywhere a counter goes through a 3-stage life (collection progress, mastery rank, expedition completion). Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Locked-element hover should swap PlayHover → PlayDeny.** When a UI element is gated (prereq unmet, branch locked, premium-only), the standard hover ping ("ding") communicates "available, click me" — wrong signal for something the player can't actually use. Swap to `SoundManager.PlayDeny()` instead, guarded by the same per-element id state used for hover-spam prevention. Audio reinforces the visual lock state without forcing the player to read the tooltip. Pattern reusable for any UI with mixed available/locked elements (skill trees, shop items requiring level/quest, expedition tiers). Source: `SkillTreePanel.razor:OnNodeHover` 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Don't add "coming soon" placeholder UI to a tight cluster.** Considered putting dim ghost-hex silhouettes below the petals as "MORE IN UPDATE 1.1" reassurance. Skipped after thinking through the visual: the cluster-canvas is 180px tall, already filled by keystone + 2 petals + connectors + lock overlays + rank badges — adding ghost shapes below would crowd the layout and likely read as "broken/half-loaded" rather than "future content." Better to communicate roadmap content via a dedicated patch-notes / roadmap surface (which Beastborne already has) than to clutter the active UI with low-fidelity placeholders. **Rule:** placeholder content is for empty layouts to teach the user what goes there. If a layout already feels populated and balanced, future-content UI should live in a different surface. Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Subtle keystone differentiation = 4px size bump + 0.5px border weight, not glow halos or extra icons.** Keystone hex needed to read as "the start" of each branch chain. Considered: an inner ring accent, a "circle-dot" corner glyph, a glow halo. Picked the simplest — bumped `width/height: 52→56px` and `border-width: 2→2.5px` on `.hex-body.is-keystone`. Subtle on its own; combined with the connector lines literally emerging from it, the brain reads "trunk → branches" without needing extra markup. Less-is-more wins when the surrounding layout already does the heavy lifting (in this case, the connectors). Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Pruning dead enum values + dead switch arms keeps the search surface clean.** SkillTree.cs had `Slot.Capstone / T2Left / T2Right` + their coord constants intended for post-launch outer-ring slots; `SkillTreePanel.GetNodeIcon()` had switch arms for ~14 cut talent IDs. Both were unreachable in production. Removed them entirely with a "re-add when post-launch updates introduce these nodes" comment in their place. Better than leaving dead code as "documentation of intent": dead arms inflate `Find Symbol` results, suggest features that don't exist, and leak into AI-generated suggestions when the agent grep'd the file for talent IDs (e.g. tooltip would show `power_devastating` as a real talent name). **Rule:** if it's not referenced by anything, don't ship it as forward-prep — write a comment-only marker for the future add-back instead. Source: SkillTree polish 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-18:** **Three-tier section grouping for menu popups beats a flat option list.** MenuPopup originally was a 2x2 grid + divider + danger button + footer — every option had equal visual weight. Switched to `[Game section] / [Community section] / [Utility chips row] / [Danger]` with `.menu-section-title` headers (3px purple `box-shadow: -3px 0 0` left bar + uppercase 10px label, ported from style-guide canonical). Result: scanning the popup is parsing groups, not searching a list. Generalizes to any settings/menu/options panel with > 5 actions. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **In-app quick mute is a 1-click utility, not an Options trip.** Options panels have many sliders for fine control; routine "I need it quiet RIGHT NOW" doesn't deserve that ceremony. Added a `.utility-chip mute-chip` that toggles `SoundManager.MasterVolume` between 0 and a remembered prior value (`_volumeBeforeMute` static field, falls back to 1.0 on first use). Icon swaps via Razor ternary (`lucide:volume-off` ↔ `lucide:volume-2`), color swaps red↔purple. Pairs naturally with a session-scoped EN/FR language toggle in the same row. Pattern: any setting that's BOTH (a) high-frequency for some players AND (b) zero-config (no slider, no picker, single bool) deserves a chip-level utility shortcut, not just an Options entry. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **In-flow `<iconify>` swap pattern for state-driven icons (volume, lock, etc) — interpolate the `icon` AND `Color` attribute strings on a SINGLE `<iconify>` element, never branch with @if/else.** Razor `@if/else` cascades that swap whole `<iconify>` elements trip the same ghost-child bug as the shop's old `BuyButtonContent` (both icons render simultaneously on rapid state changes — see CLAUDE.md quirk). Pattern that works: `<iconify icon="@(IsMuted ? "lucide:volume-off" : "lucide:volume-2")" Size=@(14) Color="@(IsMuted ? "#f87171" : "#c4b5fd")" />`. s&box's diff cleanly swaps attribute values on a stable element; it only trips on child-slot replacement. Use this anywhere a single icon/color toggles based on state (mute, lock, favorite, sound on/off). Source: MenuPopup mute toggle 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **The version chip doubles as a Credits/Changelog entry-point — saves a discrete "Patch Notes" button.** Added `.version-chip` (gold-tinted, `lucide:sparkles` + `v0.8.0`) to the popup header right side. Clicking it opens CreditsPanel (where the changelog naturally lives). One affordance, two signals: ambient "you're on this version" + clickable "what's in it." Avoids menu bloat (no separate "WHAT'S NEW" button). General principle: when two pieces of UI serve the same player intent (curiosity about the build), merge them — don't add another button. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Per-icon tile color tinting on a 2x2 grid prevents "stripe of identical buttons."** Each grid-item's `.option-icon` got a subtle bg+border tint matching its icon's accent: sky for help, green for tutorial, gold for achievements, purple for options/feedback. Without this, six iconify icons in matching dark squares read as a spreadsheet. With per-icon tinting (10-15% bg alpha + 25-30% border alpha), the grid becomes scannable — the eye can target "the gold tile" without reading labels. Standard alpha range: bg 0.08-0.12, border 0.22-0.32. Don't go higher or the tile competes with the icon glyph. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Discord brand glyph stays as `<img>` — lucide doesn't have a reliable Discord brand mark.** When swapping emojis to iconify, the urge is to replace the Discord PNG too with `simple-icons:discord` or similar. Stick with the existing `pixel--discord-white.png` asset (already used on MainMenu) — keeps brand recognition consistent across the project, dodges any iconify set inconsistency. Sized to match the iconify glyph dimensions (22px) so it reads as the same component family in the tile. Rule: brand marks (Discord, Steam, Twitch) prefer the brand's official image asset over a vector substitute, even in an iconify-first redesign. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Esc-to-close on a popup goes through `Tick()` + `Input.Pressed("Escape")`, NOT a global key handler.** Added Esc-to-close to MenuPopup by overriding `Tick()` and gating with `if (!IsVisible) return;` so the key isn't intercepted when the popup is closed (would otherwise compete with battle/UI escape elsewhere). Combined with `Input.Pressed` (not `Input.Down`) so it fires once per press. Mirror pattern in FeedbackPanel (which already does this). Pattern reusable for every modal/popup that should be Esc-dismissible. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Scoped `flex: 0 0 188px` + `width: 188px` + `min-width: 188px` triple-lock for grid-item buttons inside a flex row.** When two buttons sit in a `.grid-row { display: flex; flex-direction: row; gap: 6px }` parent, declaring just `width: 188px` is not enough — s&box still shrinks them. The flex-cards quirk requires all three: `flex: 0 0 188px; width: 188px; min-width: 188px`. Without all three, the buttons collapse to thin strips and the iconify+text inside reflows one character per line (or stacks vertically). Confirmed working in MenuPopup grid-row pairs. Reference: CLAUDE.md "Flex cards need explicit width + flex: 0 0 Npx" quirk. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **`@(IsVisible ? "visible" : "")` class on `<root>` toggles transition-driven popup reveal — DON'T also add element-name selector rules.** Several panels (DailyPanel, FeedbackPanel) use the element-name selector (`MenuPopup { ... &.visible { ... } }`) for the outer panel state. New MenuPopup uses ONLY the `<root class="menu-container @(IsVisible ? "visible" : "")">` pattern with the `MenuPopup { ... &.visible .menu-popup { transform: scale(1); opacity: 1; } }` selector. Both work; the key is to pick ONE and stay consistent. The element-name selector IS automatically toggled by s&box panel system in this case — `.visible` propagates to descendants via the SCSS cascade. Don't double-up by also adding `.menu-container.visible` rules — they're equivalent and one will silently win. Source: MenuPopup redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-17:** **Mid-sequence state-change transitions (MainMenu → Hub) need to keep the source panel mounted through the exit animation, AND hide its content under the cover.** When the player clicks PLAY on MainMenu, `GameManager.StartGame()` flips `GameState` to Hub, which flips `IsVisible` to false on the MainMenu component, which applies a `.hidden` (`display: none`) class and kills any in-flight transition. Pattern that works (see `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` Bonded Sweep): (1) gate the `.hidden` class on `IsVisible || transitionPhase > 0` so the panel stays mounted through the transition, (2) add a separate `.covering` class on root while the bars are on-screen (phase ≥ 2) and use it to `opacity: 0` every MainMenu visual layer (`.menu-content`, `.menu-background`, `.menu-video-background`, `.menu-nebula`, `.menu-vignette`, `.menu-content-wash`) so when the bars sweep off they expose the Hub, not the menu. Call `StartGame()` DURING the cover window (phase 2) so the Hub is already mounted behind the bars before they exit. Source: MainMenu Bonded Sweep 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-17:** **Multi-phase `@keyframes` transitions need `animation-fill-mode: both` AND a base `transform` at the resting state — not the entry state.** When a single element swaps between phase-1 (slide in) and phase-3 (slide out) animations, using `forwards` fill and a base `transform` at the entry position causes a one-frame snap-back when the `animation` prop changes (the old animation's `forwards` stops, base transform takes over until new animation's 0% applies). Fix: (1) set the base `transform` at the rest position (mid-screen), (2) use `animation-fill-mode: both` on both phase-1 and phase-3 animations so the 0% keyframe applies before the animation starts and the 100% persists after — this smooths the handoff at both the pre-animation state (uses 0% = off-screen for phase 1 enter) and post-animation state. Applied to MainMenu Bonded Sweep bars. `both` is not in the quirks-broken list; if s&box rejects it, fall back to dual animations on different elements. Source: MainMenu Bonded Sweep 2026-04-17.

- **2026-04-18:** **Charter-compliant Center Modal scaffold = bg-color modal + flex-column root + flat scroll child + edge-line accents.** OptionsPanel redesign distilled the Center Modal pattern: (1) outer `<root class="options-container @(IsVisible ? "visible" : "")">` with `display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center` so the modal centers in viewport, (2) overlay sibling `<div class="options-overlay" onclick=@Close>` for click-outside-to-close + dimming, (3) modal `<div class="options-modal">` with `display: flex; flex-direction: column; transform: scale(0.94); transform-origin: 50% 50%; transition: transform/opacity` flipped to `scale(1)` on `.visible` parent for transition-driven reveal (NOT @keyframes, so re-opening replays), (4) top/bottom 2px gradient accent lines as in-flow flex children for the lit-edge 3D feel, (5) header (`flex-shrink: 0`) + scroll-wrap (`flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow: hidden`) with scroll-content child (`flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow-y: scroll`) and section divs as DIRECT children of scroll, (6) footer (`flex-shrink: 0`). Re-usable for HelpPanel, AchievementPanel, BeastiaryPanel, etc. — only the inside content changes. Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Diamond-thumb toggle = pill track + 45deg-rotated square thumb. Reads as "Beastborne UI" not generic OS toggle.** Replaced the generic round-thumb pill toggle in OptionsPanel with a diamond thumb to extend MenuPopup's diamond-icon-frame vocabulary into form controls. Pattern: `.bb-toggle` (50x26 pill) with absolute `.bb-toggle-track` (full size, bg + border) and `.bb-toggle-thumb` (18x18 rotated 45deg, slides L→R via `left: 4px → 28px` transition). On variant turns track purple and thumb purple-bordered. Why diamond: the rotated-square language is now project-wide (MenuPopup tile icons, OptionsPanel section diamonds, header diamond) so even sub-controls reinforce it. Avoid `border-radius: 50%` on the thumb — round thumbs are everywhere; the diamond is the differentiator. Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Iconified stepper (number ± control) reads better than chunky text buttons for low-frequency numeric settings.** OptionsPanel had two `+/-` controls (contract warning threshold, purchase threshold) using big text-as-symbol buttons. Replaced with `.bb-stepper` pill: tiny rounded-square `lucide:minus` / `lucide:plus` iconify buttons (24x24, purple tint) flanking a centered `.step-value` span. Track has its own dark recessed bg + border so the value reads as "in a slot." Cleaner than text symbols (text "+" can render misaligned in s&box at small sizes), and the icon vocabulary stays consistent with the rest of the panel. Pattern reusable for any low-frequency numeric control (volume sliders should still use a real slider when one is implemented). Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Per-section diamond color identity (one diamond per section header, tinted to category) prevents "wall of identical headers" in long settings panels.** OptionsPanel has 6 sections; if every header diamond was purple, the panel would scan as one beige strip of titles. Per-section tone classes (`.tone-purple` `.tone-sky` `.tone-gold` `.tone-green` `.tone-rose` `.tone-danger`) give each section a distinct diamond color matching the section's "feel" (gold = gameplay/rewards, sky = display/visuals, rose = confirmations/safety, green = accessibility/health, purple = neutral utility/language, danger = destructive). Player can scroll-skim and find "the gold section" by color. Same `.section-icon-frame` shape, six color overrides — minimal CSS, large UX win. Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **`RenderFragment` helpers using `__builder =>` lambda + closure-captured params work in s&box for parameterized row templates.** OptionsPanel had ~10 toggle rows with identical structure. Inlining all of them = ~250 lines of duplicated markup. Pattern that works (mirrors HelpPanel's parameterless RenderFragment helpers but with parameters): `private RenderFragment RenderToggleRow(string icon, string label, ..., string hoverKey) => __builder => { <div class="setting-row" onmouseover=@(() => OnHover(hoverKey))> ... </div> };`. The lambda captures the method args as C# closure variables, and Razor compiles the markup with those interpolated. Verified: parameter capture works, attribute interpolation works, the markup compiles cleanly. End the lambda with `};` (not `;};`). Reduces ~250 lines to ~80 across the panel and keeps per-row tweaks in one place. Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

- **2026-04-18:** **Center modal needs `display: flex; flex-direction: column` BOTH declared on the modal element — `flex-direction: column` alone won't apply.** Subtle s&box quirk that almost bit OptionsPanel: copying `.menu-popup` style for a center modal with just `flex-direction: column` (no `display: flex`) doesn't lay out children as a column — they default to row OR collapse. MenuPopup gets away with it because something upstream (or the .razor structure) implicitly applies flex; for a fresh center modal, declare both explicitly. Charter quirk #1 says "no `display: block`" — `display: flex` is the only safe choice for non-leaf containers. Always pair `display: flex` with the direction. Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18 (caught during self-audit before testing).

- **2026-04-18:** **Settings sections collapse into 5 groups, not 7 — Notifications folds into Gameplay, Reset-to-Defaults moves into header chip + Danger Zone subsumes the bottom of the panel.** The original OptionsPanel had Battle / Display / Notifications / Confirmations / Accessibility / Language / Danger Zone (7 sections) plus a footer "Reset to Defaults" button. That much chrome makes the panel feel sprawling. Better grouping: Gameplay (auto-contract, skip anims, level-up notif, discovery alerts, contract warning) / Display (visual on-screen toggles) / Confirmations (release/fusion/purchase) / Accessibility (text/contrast) / Language / Danger Zone. Reset-to-Defaults moves up to a small `.reset-chip` next to the Close button (less commitment than a footer, signals reversibility). Reset Game Data stays in Danger Zone with its existing two-stage confirm. General principle: when a settings panel grows past 5 sections, audit whether any cross-cutting concern (notifications) actually has its own identity or is just a substitute for grouping by where the player will look (gameplay flow). Source: OptionsPanel redesign 2026-04-18.

## FeedbackPanel charter rebuild — 2026-04-19

- **2026-04-19:** **Per-tag color identity carried across rail + pill + dot creates "a battle bug" / "a fusion bug" / "a shop bug" scannable identity in a long list.** Original FeedbackPanel had a single purple `.fb-tag-pill` for every tag — the list scanned as a uniform purple wash. Rebuild: each entry has a 3px tag-color rail on the left edge (inline-style `background-color: @color`), the tag-pill body uses an inline `Hex2Rgba` helper to tint bg/border/text to the same color, AND the toolbar tag-chip's active state inline-overrides to the same color. Three signals lock the category to the eye. Implementation: small `Hex2Rgba(string hex, float alpha)` C# helper in code-behind that splits `#rrggbb` into 8-bit components and returns `rgba(r, g, b, a)` strings — usable in `style=` attributes. Pattern reusable anywhere a list has many small categorical entries (notification types, achievement categories, etc). Source: FeedbackPanel charter rebuild 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **Composer goes in a SIBLING modal at the same z-stack as the parent, NOT nested inside the parent's scroll container.** Original FeedbackPanel had the composer as a `<div class="fb-composer">` rendered conditionally INSIDE the main panel's scrollable body. Two problems: (1) overflow:hidden on the parent clips the composer's drop-shadow, (2) the composer needs its OWN scroll for the field stack, and a scroll-inside-scroll inside `flex: 1 1 0` containers is fragile in s&box. Rebuild: composer is a sibling element at the same DOM level as the main modal — both share the same `FeedbackPanel` root, but the composer has its own `position: absolute; top: 50%; left: 50%; margin-top/-left` centering and its own z-index. State-driven via `.visible` class on always-mounted markup so the slide-in transition replays every open. Since it's outside the parent's scroll container, it can use box-shadow halos on its primary submit button without leak risk. Pattern: any composer/dialog opened from inside a scrollable list panel should escape to sibling-level rather than render inline. Source: FeedbackPanel charter rebuild 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **`translateY(-2px)` on a button INSIDE a scroll-container card is risky — bounded the lift to a CHILD `<iconify>` instead.** Original instinct was to `translateY(-2px)` the entire vote button on hover for a "lift" feel. Per the s&box scroll-clip quirk (charter #16/#23), transforms on cards-inside-scroll can leak past the viewport. Mitigation: leave the button's `transform` unchanged on hover (just bg + border-color flips) and translate the inner `iconify` chevron up by 2px — the chevron is small and bounded by the button's 38×38 padded box, so it can't escape the card's content. Player still gets the "the button is reacting to my hover" feedback because the chevron motion is the dominant cue. Generalizes: when you want a "lift" feel on a button inside a scroll container, animate a child element (icon, text), not the button itself. Source: FeedbackPanel vote-btn during quirk audit 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **A simple inline `Hex2Rgba(hex, alpha)` C# helper in code-behind unblocks per-instance dynamic color theming on s&box panels.** No CSS variables, no `var()`, no hex+alpha shorthand (`#rrggbbaa` is unreliable in s&box gradient parsing). When you have a per-record color (per-tag, per-element, per-rarity) and need the same color at multiple alphas (bg 0.18, border 0.55, text 1.0), the cleanest s&box-friendly path is: data layer holds the color as `#rrggbb`, code-behind has a tiny helper that splits to ints and reformats as `rgba()`, Razor markup uses inline `style="background-color: @Hex2Rgba(c, 0.18f); border-color: @Hex2Rgba(c, 0.55f);"`. Helper signature: `private string Hex2Rgba(string hex, float alpha)` returning `rgba(r, g, b, a)` with the alpha formatted via `InvariantCulture` so `0.55f` stays `0.55` (NOT `0,55` under FR locale). Source: FeedbackPanel charter rebuild 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **Tab strip of 4+ items with active-state color identity = each tab carries its own border-bottom rail in the category color, container has NO bottom border.** A common segmented-tab pattern is to put a horizontal line under the whole tab strip and let the active tab "interrupt" it. In s&box without z-index and overlay finesse, that produces a doubled line where the inactive tabs' bottom border + the container's border render adjacent. Cleaner pattern: each tab has its own `border-bottom: 1px solid X`, default state uses a neutral `rgba(255,255,255,0.08)`, active state OVERRIDES the bottom-border color via the longhand `border-bottom-color: <category-color>`. The container element has no border-bottom — the tabs' bottoms ARE the divider. Active tab visually "joins" to the toolbar below via its category-tinted bottom rail. Source: FeedbackPanel tab strip 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **Custom empty-state per tab beats a single "nothing here" — each tab gets its own icon + title + body + CTA tuned to that tab's purpose.** Original had a generic `GetEmptyText() switch` returning short strings + a single "Be the first to post" button. Rebuild has `GetEmptyIcon()` (per-tab lucide) + `GetEmptyTitle()` (e.g. "All clear — no open bugs", "Be the first idea-haver") + `GetEmptyBody()` (encouraging body copy specific to the tab) + a CTA button that varies (Bugs/Ideas → "REPORT FIRST BUG"/"POST FIRST IDEA"; My Posts → "BROWSE BUGS"; Resolved → no CTA, just a passive explanation). Each empty state authored as a person, not generated. Wrapping the icon in the same diamond-frame language used in the panel header keeps visual coherence. Combined with the ambient breathe animation on the diamond, the empty state becomes a deliberate moment instead of a "loading…" leftover. Pattern: every list-driven panel deserves N tab-specific empty states, not one generic. Source: FeedbackPanel charter rebuild 2026-04-19.

## MainMenu editorial-cinematic redesign — 2026-04-19

- **2026-04-19:** **Editorial bare-typography menu pattern (type IS the interface, no pill chrome).** Replaced the Beastborne chunky-pill main menu (bronze-rim 4px borders, painted gradients, beveled ::after highlight strips) with a Hades II / Xenoblade DE / Metaphor-style bare-text menu. Structure: each entry is a flex `<button>` with NO background, NO border, NO shadow at rest. Three elements inside: a positioned `.entry-arrow` (hidden at rest, fades in on hover), a `.entry-text` (carries the tier's size + color), and a `.entry-underline` (0-to-N-px width transition on hover). Hover/selected state = color warm (cream → gold), letter-spacing kern (3px → 5px), text translateX(+16px), arrow opacity 0 → 1 with 0.1s LINEAR snap (not ease — snap reads as "stamped into place"). Tiers: PLAY 54px/cream, ROADMAP 30px/dim cream, utility 20px/dimmest cream. All weights 800 Exo2 uppercase. This is now a second valid main-nav aesthetic alongside the pill family — choose per panel intent. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss:509-702` main menu redesign 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **`@keyframes` on an always-mounted root element (ambient infinite loops) continues to work reliably.** The light-beam drift (`.menu-light-beam` with 18s `@keyframes light-beam-drift translate(-40%,-40%) → translate(40%,40%)`) plays continuously once the menu mounts and never needs to replay. Same pattern as `.menu-nebula` / `.feature-float` / `.halo-breath` on the main menu. This is the "allowed" keyframes use case per css-quirks.md — ambient motion on elements that stay in the DOM. The "don't use keyframes for state reveals" rule is about class-toggle-triggered replays, which is a DIFFERENT use case. When in doubt: state reveal = transition, always-on ambient = keyframes. Source: MainMenu light-beam craft layer 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **Column-level "dim non-hovered siblings" via `:not()` + parent `:hover` is risky in s&box — lean on color-contrast hover state instead.** Considered `.menu-button-column:hover .menu-entry:not(:hover):not(.selected) { opacity: 0.7 }` to focus-pull the active entry in an editorial menu stack. Skipped — s&box's selector engine has historically struggled with `:not()` chains + parent-state compounding. Workaround: the active entry's color shift (cream → gold) + letter-spacing kern + arrow fade creates enough contrast that dimming the siblings wasn't needed. Generalizable rule: when a menu wants "the active item pops, siblings recede," solve it with the active item's positive signal rather than the siblings' negative signal. Source: MainMenu redesign 2026-04-19, rejected `.menu-button-column:hover .menu-entry:not(...)` approach.

- **2026-04-19:** **Splash "magazine subtitle rule" pattern — thin 1px warm gold line, 40px wide, fading to zero-alpha at one end.** Replaced the Persona-style dual-flourish splash treatment (two gradient rules flanking the italic line) with a single narrow gold rule BENEATH the italic splash text. CSS: `width: 40px; height: 1px; background-image: linear-gradient(to right, rgba(251, 191, 36, 0.82) 0%, rgba(251, 191, 36, 0) 100%);`. Reads as a restrained editorial anchor — the accent line signals "this is a subtitle" without flanking ornamentation. Reusable for any panel that wants an italic tagline under a hero element. Stays firmly subservient to the headline. Source: `MainMenu.razor.scss:389-394` splash redesign 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **`line-height: 72` on `font-size: 54` passes the ≥font-size+2 quirk and gives text room to breathe without visible whitespace.** For monumental main-menu text (PLAY at 54px), line-height = font-size + 18 (72) is comfortable. The CLAUDE.md quirk warns "30px+ needs line-height ≥ font-size" — the +18 margin is safety headroom against any obscure clipping condition, plus it creates ~9px of visual padding above and below the letter bounds that reads as intentional editorial spacing, not dead whitespace. For inline body copy under the same line-height rule, keep it tight (font + 4 to 8); for hero typography, give it more breath. Source: `.menu-entry-play .entry-text` 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-20:** **s&box `object-position` rejects BOTH percentage pairs AND keyword pairs ("right center" fails the same way "50% 25%" does).** Previous quirk entry only noted percentage pairs as broken. Runtime: `right center is not valid with object-position`. Empirical fix: drop the property entirely and rely on `object-fit: contain` default centering. Worked cleanly for the main-menu featured portrait — Pagefin (widest starter) no longer clips even without an explicit position. Default centering is almost always what you actually wanted anyway. Generalizes: if you find yourself writing `object-position` in s&box, try removing it first before hunting for a valid value. [PROMOTE] — the CLAUDE.md entry currently says only "percentage pairs rejected"; should be updated to "all pair forms rejected (percentage OR keyword); use single keyword or drop entirely". Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss:239-244` 2026-04-20, live log `right center is not valid with object-position [/ui/mainmenu.razor.scss:201]`.

- **2026-04-20:** **Multi-mount Panel pattern — when a shared modal (OptionsPanel, HelpPanel, etc.) needs to open from BOTH MainMenu and GameHUD, mount TWO instances and use a `List<T>` + `PickActiveInstance()` visibility walk rather than the single-Instance singleton pattern.** Problem: the canonical `public static Panel Instance; Instance = this on firstTime;` pattern breaks when a modal is mounted under two parents that use `.hidden { display: none }` to gate screen state — whichever mounts last owns Instance forever, so calling `Show()` from the other screen routes to an invisible panel. Fix: (1) add `private static readonly List<T> _instances = new();` and push on `OnAfterTreeRender firstTime`, remove in `OnDeleted`. (2) keep `Instance` set on every OnAfterTreeRender for back-compat. (3) add `PickActiveInstance()` that walks each instance's `this.Parent` chain and returns the first one with no ancestor holding `HasClass("hidden")`. (4) `Show()` targets the picked instance; `Hide()` iterates all instances so stale panels never get stuck visible after a screen switch. (5) add `using System.Collections.Generic` to the `.razor` imports. Works because s&box's `.hidden { display: none }` class on screen roots is a consistent signal across the project. Source: `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor:321-407` OPTIONS-on-MainMenu wiring 2026-04-20.

- **2026-04-19:** **Percentage vertical margin on absolutely-positioned children resolves against the parent's WIDTH, not height — in s&box AND in standard CSS.** The main-menu featured portrait was cropping to just the top ~20% of the starter artwork (ears + halo visible only). Root cause: `.feature-art` was using `top: 50%; margin-top: -45%` to vertically center a 90%-tall box inside `.menu-feature-portrait` (62% viewport wide, 100% viewport tall). Since `margin-top` percentages resolve against the containing block's INLINE size (= width), `-45%` of a 62%-viewport-wide parent translates to ~-535px on a 1920px viewport — pushing the art box half the viewport off the top. `object-fit: contain` then fit the image inside a box mostly off-screen, cropping everything but the top of the figure. **Fix:** skip the `top: 50% + margin-top: -X%` vertical-centering dance entirely. Use `top: 0; left: 0; width: 100%; height: 100%; object-fit: contain;` and let `contain` handle centering against the full parent. For breathing animation, animate `transform: translateY(...)` only with `transform-origin: center` so layout doesn't drift. Generalizes: **never use percentage vertical margins to center vertically — use flexbox centering, `top: 50%; transform: translateY(-50%)`, or full-fill + object-fit.** Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss:232-258` portrait crop fix 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **Editorial "LIVE ·" typographic prefix beats Unicode dot character (`●`) for ticker signals.** The news ticker initially used `●` (U+25CF BLACK CIRCLE) as a pulsing signal prefix, but at 10px s&box's text rasterizer rendered it inconsistently — sometimes as a faint dot, sometimes invisibly. Replaced with `<span class="ticker-live-label">LIVE</span> <span class="ticker-sep">·</span>` — a typographic prefix reads cleaner, scales reliably, and matches the `.toast-sep` mid-dot pattern already used on `.event-toast`. Pattern: 11px 900-weight warm-gold "LIVE" + 13px mid-dot separator + body text. For editorial ticker/strip UIs, prefer typographic signal labels over single Unicode glyphs. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss:425-453` ticker prefix swap 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-19:** **"DEVELOPED BY [STUDIO]" credit beat is an under-used composition slot — use it to fill editorial dead zones between logo and bottom HUD.** KH3/Persona/Metaphor all reserve a sub-region of their title screen for a small "DEVELOPED BY" credit, left-anchored on the same axis as the menu stack. It adds weight without chrome: 10px uppercase label + thin warm gold hairline + 16px studio name. Pattern lives at `.dev-credit` in `MainMenu.razor.scss` — reusable for any editorial layout that has a taller-than-needed left column. Ships with a commented `<img class="dev-credit-logo">` fallback so swapping to a branded PNG later is a 2-line change. When designing for a tall-format menu layout, the gap between the functional controls and the bottom HUD is NOT waste — it's a composition slot. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss:722-785` dev-credit addition 2026-04-19.

- **2026-04-23:** **Rotated diamond buttons should keep the ROTATION on an inner wrapper, not the outer clickable box.** Roster + Beastbook diamonds apply `transform: rotate(45deg)` to the outer container and counter-rotate the icon inside — that works fine when there are no absolute-positioned overlays (tooltips, badges that need upright text in a flat coord system). For the GameHUD Option C corner rails I needed tooltips above the diamond + badges on its corner, and positioning absolute children inside a 45°-rotated parent gives surprising geometry (the child's `top/right` coords are in the rotated coord system, so "above" visually means "up-and-to-the-right" of the un-rotated shape). Cleaner pattern: outer `.corner-diamond` is a 44×44 UN-rotated wrapper (flat coord system, hosts the tooltip + badge + hover scale transition), inner `.corner-diamond-inner` is a 32×32 child with `transform: rotate(45deg)` carrying the background/border/box-shadow. The icon counter-rotates `rotate(-45deg)` inside the inner shell. Trade-off: the rotated 32×32 has a ~45px bounding box (32×√2) that slightly overhangs the 44×44 wrapper — visually acceptable (the tips read as shape, not clipping), and the trade-off is worth it to get simple absolute-positioning for badges/tooltips. Use this pattern whenever a rotated diamond needs overhang decorations. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss:667-775` 2026-04-23 Option C corner rails.

- **2026-04-23:** **"Corner rails outside scroll containers" is the license to use colored halo box-shadows freely.** CLAUDE.md's Lesson 3 (scroll containers don't clip descendant box-shadow) forbids colored halos on grid-card hover states. But HUD corner-rail buttons are direct children of `<root>`, siblings of `.content-area`, with `overflow: visible` all the way up — they are NEVER inside a scroll container. So the constraint flips: colored halos are GOOD feedback here. The corner diamonds use `box-shadow: 0 0 22px rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.5)` on hover as tactile "this is pressable" feedback, which would leak everywhere if applied to a grid card. Rule of thumb: if the element is a HUD chrome child (not inside any panel's scroll), freely use colored halos for hover + selected states. If it's inside any scroll-grid, it's bordered-only territory. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss:679-690` 2026-04-23 Option C corner rails.

- **2026-04-23:** **Tier 1 ambient breath on HUD chrome costs almost nothing and erases the "dead screen" feeling.** The Option C pass added two subtle ambient loops to the HUD: avatar scale breath (1.0 → 1.03 over 3.2s) and floating-cluster card 1px translateY breath (2.8s). Each is a single `@keyframes` + `animation:` pair, maybe 6 lines of SCSS total. Result: the HUD's left edge and bottom-center now feel alive even when the player isn't interacting, which is Balatro's core insight — constant subtle motion reads as "alive", no motion reads as "dead". Key calibration: keep these movements UNDER visual-noticeable-threshold per frame (1-3px, 2-4s period). They're felt more than seen. Don't apply to every HUD element — pick 2-3 key anchors so the ambient motion has rhythm. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss:156-161 + 635-640` 2026-04-23.

- **2026-04-23:** **Floating pill clusters + corner rails are a valid replacement pattern for a full bottom bar on games that need vertical screen reclamation.** Option C (replacing the 90px bottom bar with a center floating cluster + two corner-rail diamond pairs) reclaims ~90px of vertical content space without sacrificing any functionality: all 6 primary actions remain visible, all badge counts preserved, hover tooltips replace the permanent text labels on utility buttons (chat/radio/effects/notifications). The floating cluster stays labeled because it's for primary actions (Quests/Menu/Bag) and glanceability matters for those; the corner rails go icon-only because they're utility surfaces where the badge is the important signal (unread counts, active effect count). Trade-off: no bottom-edge solid band means content can appear edge-to-edge, which can look bare on empty panels — mitigate by giving panels their own bottom padding if they have footer content, and accept that the floating cluster's drop shadow creates visual separation from content below it. This is a pattern worth considering for any HUD redesign where screen space matters more than bar-as-visual-anchor. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor` + `.scss` 2026-04-23 Option C rework.

- **2026-04-23:** **"The chrome shrunk but the content didn't" is the real reason players say the UI is "too tiny."** Option C compressed the GameHUD top bar from 60 → 44px and the user's immediate feedback was "it is so hard to see everything because it is all so tiny." Diagnosing the feedback: the compression was fine; the failure was that I kept content sizes (24px icons, 12px labels, 28px avatar, 14px resource icons) at their pre-compression values, so they read as tiny in both absolute terms and relative to the reduced chrome. Lesson: when you shrink a container, you must ALSO re-scale its children proportionally, OR you must justify the child sizing independently. The floor for readable UI icons is closer to 32px than 24px, and labels should sit at ≥13px when they carry meaning. When a rework ask is "reclaim space," the answer is usually a structural shift (rail instead of bar), not a content squeeze — structural shifts give you the budget to go BIGGER on content while still reducing total chrome. Source: user feedback on Option C 2026-04-23 + Option B rebrand.

- **2026-04-23:** **Steam avatars in s&box UI use the special `avatar:{steamId}` URL scheme in `<img src=>`.** Confirmed pattern: `<img src="avatar:@steamId" />` works across ChatPanel, LeaderboardPanel, GuildPanel. `Connection.Local?.SteamId ?? 0` gives you the local player's id; when it's 0 (dev playtest without Steam session) you need a fallback — my pattern is `@if (GetSteamId() > 0) { <img src="avatar:..." /> } else { <img src="character-portrait.png" /> }`. Size the image with CSS (width/height: 48px + border-radius: 50%) since s&box will scale it to fit. Do not put quotes around the URL (s&box quirk: URL quotes in inline styles aren't supported; this applies to `src` attributes too). Source: `Code/UI/Components/ChatPanel.razor:65` + `Code/UI/Panels/LeaderboardPanel.razor:84` + `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor` rail 2026-04-23.

- **2026-04-23:** **Vertical rail + hub is a stronger HUD pattern than horizontal tabs for a panel-first game.** Horizontal space at the top of the screen is precious (panels want height), vertical space on the side is cheap (panels rarely need full screen width). When every screen in the game is a panel (roster, beastbook, shop, expedition map), a rail on the left trades 64px of width for ALL the vertical real estate back, and gives the icons a 32–36px render surface instead of cramming them into a compressed 44px bar. Modern tool pattern — Discord / Slack / VSCode / Linear / Figma — means muscle memory transfers in. Trade-offs: mobile gestures (thumb at the edge) point the opposite direction; for games shipping on mobile, the rail might need to flip sides or move bottom. For PC/Steam-first games with panel-heavy UI, the rail is the right bet. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor` Option B rebrand 2026-04-23 after Option C compression got "too tiny" feedback.

- **2026-04-23:** **"Home hub" destination as the game's default landing makes the game feel like a THERE, not a spreadsheet.** Before: Beastborne opened into the monster grid. First impression was utility, not identity. After: Beastborne opens into HomeHubPanel, which is a calm composition of (1) big "WELCOME BACK, {NAME}" line, (2) conditional active-expedition ribbon if a run is in progress, (3) daily streak + unclaimed missions card, (4) favorite monster showcase with idle-animated sprites drifting, (5) quick-jump shortcuts. Content is glanceable, state-aware, anti-gacha — no "lucky" language, no RNG reveals, no FOMO. Total authoring effort: ~280 lines of Razor + ~420 lines of SCSS + zero data layer changes (all data sourced from existing managers). Pattern reuse: section-title from roster, card bg from style-guide primitives, sprite animation from MonsterCard's `SpriteAnimator.GlobalFrame` integration. When adding a new hub-like surface to any game, the content recipe is: hero/greeting, status-ribbon (only when relevant), 2–3 glanceable cards pulling from existing data sources, context-aware quick-jumps. Don't overload with widgets — every added section dilutes the feeling of "this is a place." Source: `Code/UI/Panels/HomeHubPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-23 Option B rebrand.

- **2026-04-23:** **Iconify inside rotated diamonds: counter-rotate a wrapping div, not the `<iconify>` element itself.** Static SVG `<img>` tags take a `transform: rotate(-45deg)` directly without issue, but the `<iconify>` component in s&box renders its glyph in a way that doesn't always honor a direct `transform` on the element (hit in the GameHUD Option B pass when I tried `.corner-icon { transform: rotate(-45deg) }` on the iconify and the glyph rendered tilted). Wrap the iconify in a plain `<div class="corner-iconify-wrap">` and apply `transform: rotate(-45deg)` to the wrapper. Inside the 45°-rotated diamond shell, the wrapper's counter-rotation cancels out and the glyph sits upright. Pattern lives in `.corner-iconify-wrap` — reuse whenever you need an iconify inside a rotated parent. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss:836-843` 2026-04-23 Option B corner rails.

- **2026-04-23:** **`margin-left` (not `transform: translateX`) when you need to offset an element that already owns `transform` via an animation.** The GameHUD floating cluster in Option B needs to center over the CONTENT AREA (which starts at left: 64 because of the rail) rather than the full viewport. The natural move was `left: 50%; transform: translateX(calc(-50% + 32px))`. But the cluster also has a Tier 1 breath animation (`transform: translateY(-1px)` at mid-loop) — and the two `transform` declarations collide: the animation overwrites the centering transform. Fix: use `left: 50%; margin-left: -178px;` where the margin-left value encodes both the centering shift AND the rail offset (`-cluster_width/2 + rail_width/2 = -210 + 32 = -178`). Reserves the `transform` property for animation-only use. Rule: any element that has an ambient breath/float animation should centre via margin math, never via transform offset. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss` `.floating-cluster` 2026-04-23.

- **2026-04-23:** **For panel-heavy games, a single unified bottom bar beats vertical-rail or top+bottom-bar splits.** After 4 HUD reworks in one week (horizontal top+bottom → Option C compact top + corner rails → Option B vertical rail + corner rails + floating cluster → Unified Command Bar), the clear winner for Beastborne is a single floating bottom bar holding ALL chrome (avatar + currency + 7 destinations + actions + utilities). Why the vertical rail lost: every grid panel (Beasts/Beastbook/Shop/Online) fought the 64px left claim — either the first column clipped or each panel needed bespoke padding like `padding-left: 120px`. Why top+bottom split lost: two chrome surfaces that each need their own visual weight compete; content gets sandwiched. Why the unified bar wins: one chrome element means content is full-bleed horizontal (grids love this), one place to tint for themes, no competing anchor points for popups, and — because it's the ONLY chrome — the bar gets to be visually confident (88px tall, bigger labels, more padding) instead of apologetically compressed. Rule of thumb: **when every game screen is a grid, your HUD chrome should be on ONE edge, not two, and it should be horizontal not vertical.** Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-23 Unified Command Bar rework after 3 prior layouts.

- **2026-04-23:** **Active-tab underline that slides in via width transition is a surprisingly satisfying substitute for sibling-morphing underlines.** In real browsers you can use `position: relative` on the tab row and a single floating underline that `left/width`-transitions between siblings. s&box doesn't do cross-sibling morph cleanly. Workaround: give each tab its own underline div (initial `width: 0`, centered via `margin-left: -half-width`) and transition `width` + `margin-left` with a cubic spring `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)`. The newly-active tab's bar grows from 0 → 52px and "pops" into place with the overshoot. Visually feels like a shared underline slid over — in reality it's 7 independent underlines, 6 of them collapsed to 0. Costs nothing at runtime. Pattern: `.cb-tab-underline { width: 0; margin-left: -18px; transition: width 0.25s cubic-bezier(...), margin-left 0.25s cubic-bezier(...) }` then `.cb-tab.active .cb-tab-underline { width: 52px; margin-left: -26px }`. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss` `.cb-tab-underline` 2026-04-23.

- **2026-04-23:** **Absolutely-positioned "top hairline accent" on a filled parent will render BEHIND the parent's background-color in s&box.** Tried to add a thin bright-purple hairline at the very top of the command bar as a premium accent: `<div class="cb-top-accent" style="position: absolute; top: 0; left: 40; right: 40; height: 1px; background-image: gradient;">`. Per the existing quirk "absolute children intended as bg layers paint BEHIND parent background-color," this is the same failure mode even for a 1px accent. Fix: don't use a separate child layer. Either (a) just bump the bar's uniform `border:` color to something slightly brighter, or (b) render the accent as a plain in-flow first-child flex item with `height: 1px; width: 100%;` which is visually wrong (takes layout height), or (c) accept you can't have a separate top accent distinct from the bar's own border. Chose (a) — the bar's `1.5px solid rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.36)` already gives the premium ring. Rule: don't spend a child div on a 1px accent — use border tricks instead. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss` `.command-bar` 2026-04-23 (removed `.cb-top-accent` after learning the quirk applies to sub-pixel accents too).

- **2026-04-24:** **Interactive elements should be meaningfully larger than passive info readouts — if the action diamond is within 15% of the HP tab diamond, the cluster reads as background.** Battle HUD had `.action3d-diamond` at 86x86 while the passive `.panel3d-diamond` HP tab at top corners was 72x72. After the game-hud background-image fix exposed the full 3D arena, the action cluster read as "tiny pixel stamps" against the newly-visible space. Fixed by scaling action diamonds to 108x108 (+26%, now 50% larger than HP tabs) with icons 52x52 (from 40) and iconify Size=36 (from 28). Labels bumped 15 → 17px. Rule of thumb: **primary-action surfaces should be 1.4–1.6× the size of nearest passive info surface.** The HP tab reads "here is information"; the action diamond says "here is agency" — agency must win the size fight. Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss` `.action3d-diamond` 2026-04-24.

## MainMenu "Concept C — Launcher Hub" rebuild — 2026-06-04

- **2026-06-04:** **The main-menu `<root>` AND `.menu-content` are both `pointer-events: none` — every interactive child in the menu screen MUST declare `pointer-events: all` or it's dead to the mouse.** s&box uses the keyword `all` (not `auto`). The whole menu chrome is click-through by default so the ambient bg layers don't eat clicks; only the buttons/cards opt back in. When I rebuilt `.menu-content`'s interior as the launcher hub, every clickable surface (`.lh-play`, `.lh-item`, `.lh-discord`, `.lh-util`, `.lh-feat-cta`, `.lh-card`) needed `pointer-events: all`. Pre-existing menu buttons (`.menu-entry`, `.discord-link`) all had it — grep `pointer-events: all` to confirm the convention before assuming a new clickable will work. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` launcher-hub rebuild 2026-06-04; convention at `.menu-entry` line ~1336.

- **2026-06-04:** **CSS-border triangles (`width:0; height:0; border-color: transparent transparent transparent #xxx`) are UNPROVEN in s&box — use a text glyph (▶/◀) instead.** The Concept C prototype used CSS triangles for the PLAY caret, CTA caret, and card corner arrows. There is ZERO existing usage of the border-triangle technique anywhere in the Beastborne codebase, but the `▶` text glyph is used heavily and reliably (old `.entry-arrow`, ticker, etc.). To de-risk, I rendered all three as `▶` text glyphs sized via `font-size`/`color` rather than CSS borders. The `transparent` *color* keyword on `border-color` is standard CSS (the banned-`transparent` quirk is specifically about gradient stops), but the triangle TECHNIQUE itself wasn't verified to lay out — text glyph is the safe, proven path. If border-triangles ever get verified working in-engine, they'd be marginally crisper, but the glyph is indistinguishable at these sizes. Source: launcher-hub rebuild 2026-06-04.

- **2026-06-04:** **`backdrop-filter` (frosted glass) and `conic-gradient` (the Public Square studio mark) are both absent from s&box — substitute a solid dark rgba() and a `linear-gradient(135deg, ...)` square respectively.** Concept C's sidebar called for `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` over the bg; the project has never had a working backdrop-filter, so the "frosted" look is faked with a solid `linear-gradient(to bottom, rgba(20,13,44,0.92), rgba(13,8,30,0.96))` panel fill + a faint right border + drop shadow — reads as a distinct frosted surface against the busy menu bg without any real blur. The conic studio mark became a 22px `linear-gradient(135deg, #ffce3a, #ee5421)` rounded square. Both are documented in CLAUDE.md's still-NOT-supported list; the substitutions are the canonical fallbacks. Source: `.lh-sidebar` / `.lh-studio-mark` 2026-06-04.

- **2026-06-04:** **Featured-card marketing copy (kicker/title/desc) belongs in hand-set `@code` string fields, NOT the live event/announcement data feed.** When rebuilding the menu as a launcher hub with a big "UPDATE 1.2 · WEAVERTON / Five new beasts await." featured card, I added `featuredKicker` / `featuredTitle` / `featuredTitleAccent` / `featuredTitleTail` / `featuredDesc` as plain string fields (added to `BuildHash()`), commented as "update per release." The LIVE event pill stays wired to the existing server-driven `currentEventText/Icon/Timer`, but the per-update headline is editorial marketing — it changes once per release, not per active event, so client-side strings are correct. Future option noted in-code: wire the featured copy to the latest patch-notes entry or a dedicated "featured" field on the announcements payload so it doesn't need a client patch. The hero `<img>` reuses the existing `currentFeatureArt` (rotating starter-art placeholder) so no new asset was needed; real key-art swaps in later. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` featured-card fields 2026-06-04.

- **2026-06-04:** **Re-mapping an existing `entranceStage` cascade onto a new layout is cheaper than rewriting the timing — pick the stage gates that read as the right reveal order and leave `PlayEntranceSequence()` untouched.** The old menu used 7 `entranceStage` steps (logo→splash/ticker→PLAY→Roadmap→Options→utility→bottom HUD). For the launcher hub I reused the SAME ramp: sidebar shell at `>=1`, sidebar nav at `>=3`, main area at `>=4`, featured card at `>=5`, bottom cards at `>=6`. No code-behind timing change — just different `@(entranceStage >= N ? "entered" : "")` gates on the new elements, each with its own default-hidden/`.entered` transition pair in SCSS. The cascade now reads sidebar→nav→main→featured→cards. `entranceStage` was already in `BuildHash()`, so the replay-on-return-to-menu still works. Lesson: a staggered-reveal counter is layout-agnostic; only the per-element gate values and transitions are layout-specific. Source: `MainMenu.razor` + `.scss` launcher-hub entrance gates 2026-06-04.

- **2026-04-24:** **s&box `TextRenderer` has NO native outline / stroke / shadow property — use a child GameObject with a second `TextRenderer` as the shadow pass.** Inventoried the API via `Libraries/ozmium.oz_mcp/Editor/RenderingToolHandlers.cs` and in-project usage: `Text`, `FontSize`, `Scale`, `Color`, `HorizontalAlignment` (`TextRenderer.HAlignment`), `VerticalAlignment` (`TextRenderer.VAlignment`) are the only surfaced properties. No `Outline`, `OutlineColor`, `OutlineWidth`, `Stroke`, `Shadow`, `DropShadow`, `FontFamily`, or `FontWeight`. Canonical pattern for world-space text readability on busy backgrounds: spawn a child `GameObject` parented to the main text GO, add a second `TextRenderer` with the same text/fontSize but a dark color (`Color(0.05, 0.02, 0.08, 0.7)`) at `LocalPosition = new Vector3(shadowOffsetX, shadowOffsetY, shadowOffsetZ)` — the Z offset pushes it behind in render order. s&box's TextRenderer is billboarded so the shadow tracks orientation automatically. Floating damage numbers in `Code/Battle3D/FloatingDamageNumber.cs` use this pattern. Also confirmed `WorldRotation = Rotation.FromAxis(Vector3.Forward, degrees)` works on billboarded TextRenderer GOs to add a slight z-roll (±3°) for non-robotic feel — useful any time you want to de-grid world-space text. Source: 2026-04-24 floating damage number AAA juice pass.

- **2026-04-24:** **`animation-delay` on per-child positioning selectors is the clean way to stagger a cluster entrance in s&box.** `transition-delay` is silently dropped by the s&box parser (existing quirk), and `@keyframes` only play once on mount — but that's exactly what a state-driven panel like the battle action bar needs, because the element enters the DOM fresh each time `barState == "actions"` resolves (it's inside an `@if` block). Pattern: single keyframe (`@keyframes diamond-stagger-in { 0% { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(18px) scale(0.85); } 100% { opacity: 1; transform: none; } }`), then each child's positioning rule (`.action3d-buttons .action3d-btn:nth-child(1) { ...; animation: diamond-stagger-in 0.35s both; animation-delay: 0ms; }` through `:nth-child(4) { animation-delay: 180ms; }`). The `both` fill-mode keeps children invisible during their pre-delay so they don't flash in before their turn. 60ms steps read as a snappy cascade; wider 100ms+ steps feel draggy. Cubic-bezier `(0.2, 0.9, 0.3, 1.2)` adds a gentle landing overshoot without parsing as bounce. Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss` `.action3d-buttons .action3d-btn:nth-child(N)` 2026-04-24.

- **2026-04-24:** **[PROMOTE] `position: fixed` is REJECTED by the s&box CSS parser.** Runtime log: "Generic fixed is not valid with position". Only `absolute`, `relative`, and default (`static`) work. Implication: full-viewport overlays like drag shields / modal backdrops / page-wide capture layers can't use the standard `position: fixed; inset: 0;` web pattern. Workaround: use `position: absolute` with oversized negative offsets — `top: -4000px; left: -4000px; width: 8000px; height: 8000px;` covers any realistic viewport from any anchor point and is immune to the element's containing-block position (useful when the anchor parent is `bottom: 96; right: 440` rather than `0,0`). Added to `css-quirks.md`. Source: 2026-04-24 ChatPanel drag shield rework after the initial `position: fixed` version logged the parser rejection.

- **2026-04-24:** **Switching world-space text to a screen-space Razor overlay unlocks all CSS text effects.** Damage numbers were originally `TextRenderer` GameObjects; replaced with a Razor overlay (`Code/UI/Components/DamageNumberOverlay.razor`) backed by a `DamageNumberManager` singleton holding a `List<DamageNumberData>` and a per-frame prune. Pattern: BattleSceneController calls `mgr.SpawnHit(defenderId, damage, crit, super)`, which captures the defender's CURRENT `GetMonsterScreenPosition()` once at spawn, adds a row, and lets the overlay render the variant CSS from there. Position stays fixed at spawn (number leaves the target and drifts independently) so KO animations / sprite movement don't tear the number off mid-flight. Variants (normal / crit / super / miss) live as separate `@if` branches with class names that drive the keyframe variant. **Caveat:** s&box's Razor diff inside a foreach reuses element slots by index — so subsequent spawns may NOT replay their entry keyframes if the previous spawn is still alive. Best-effort mitigation is encoding the spawn id in a unique class name so the diff has a chance to treat them as distinct elements; if not, the second-spawn animation may skip until the first one expires. Acceptable for turn-based combat where hits land sequentially. The CLAUDE.md "render-key" gap is the real fix and remains open. Source: 2026-04-24 Manga Impact damage-number port; orphaned `DamageNumberSystem.cs` (a previous attempt) was removed in the same pass.

- **2026-04-24:** **Draggable-moving-popup pattern: root static, transform on wrapper, shield as sibling outside the transform.** Building drag-to-reposition on ChatPanel. First attempt put `transform: translate(Xpx, Ypx)` on `<root>` and `onmousemove` on root — the popup VISUALLY moved but `MousePosition` inside the handler is panel-local. As the root moves by dx, `MousePosition.x` decreases by dx in the root's own coord system → the delta calculation oscillates and the popup "fights" the cursor / shakes. Compensating with `MousePosition.x + Box.Rect.Left` didn't fix it (Box.Rect may not update with transforms in s&box). The robust pattern that DID work: **(1) Root stays STATIC** — no transform, no size change. **(2) Wrapper div inside root gets the `transform: translate(...)`** via inline style; wrapper has NO CSS rule of its own (default flow; any `position: absolute; top/left/right/bottom: 0` on the wrapper collapsed it to 0×0 since root had no explicit size, which broke hit-tests on descendants like the close button). **(3) Drag shield is a sibling of the wrapper inside root**, rendered only while `_isDragging`. Shield is `position: absolute` at `top: -4000px; left: -4000px; width: 8000px; height: 8000px` (since `position: fixed` isn't supported). Shield carries the `onmousemove` + `onmouseup` handlers. Because the shield doesn't inherit the wrapper's transform, `MousePosition` relative to the shield is a stable viewport-space coord — no feedback loop. **(4) Drag handle** (e.g. chat-header) has `onmousedown` that sets `_isDragging = true; _needsDragAnchor = true; StateHasChanged();` — the anchor flag is captured on the FIRST `OnDragMove` (on the shield) so we skip the header's noisy panel-local MousePosition and anchor in shield-space from the first frame. Source: `Code/UI/Components/ChatPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-24 drag refactor after a first attempt "worked but shook." Reusable for Radio/Effects/Notifications popups — same pattern applies.

- **2026-04-25:** **Rebuilding the OnlineHubPanel Tamers detail sidebar to mirror MonsterRoster sidebar.** Three failure modes the old sidebar exhibited that this rewrite fixed: (1) DEV badge inside a flex `.td-hero-info` column rendered as "D / E / V" stacked vertically — fix: pull the chip OUT of the flex flow and into a `position: absolute` overlay on the avatar wrapper. (2) Level chip as a flex-row sibling of name+title overlapped the text because the row had 3 children and `flex: 1` distribution failed (hits the existing 3+ siblings quirk). Fix: same as DEV — chip becomes an absolutely-positioned overlay pinned to the bottom of the avatar (`bottom: -6px; left: 50
- **2026-04-25:** **Rebuilding the OnlineHubPanel Tamers detail sidebar to mirror MonsterRoster sidebar.** Three failure modes the old sidebar exhibited that this rewrite fixed: (1) DEV badge inside a flex `.td-hero-info` column rendered as "D / E / V" stacked vertically — fix: pull the chip OUT of the flex flow and into a `position: absolute` overlay on the avatar wrapper. (2) Level chip as a flex-row sibling of name+title overlapped the text because the row had 3 children and `flex: 1` distribution failed (existing 3+ siblings quirk). Fix: same as DEV — chip becomes an absolutely-positioned overlay pinned to the bottom of the avatar (`bottom: -6px; left: 50%; margin-left: -28px`), removing it from row flex entirely. (3) Bare text mixed with `@-expr` in the same span (`Lv @level`) tripped Razor disambiguation; switched to `@($"LV {GetPlayerLevel()}")` interpolation form. Section pattern lifted from roster: `.tamer-detail-section-header` mirrors roster's `.detail-section-header` (3px purple left bar + 1px bottom underline + uppercase text). Sections: PORTRAIT → ACTIONS → STATS → FAVORITE BEAST → INFO. Element-color border on portrait set via inline `style="border-color: @titleColor"` overriding the SCSS baseline `border: 3px solid rgba(167,139,250,0.55)`. XP bar pattern lifted from `.portrait-xp-bar`: 5px rail with absolute fill. Online/collected branches reuse same components but skip XP (no data) — replaced with status pills (green ONLINE NOW dot, purple SNAPSHOT pill). General rule: chips that need to sit ALONGSIDE a flex column should be absolute-positioned overlays on a `position: relative` ancestor, not flex-row siblings of the column. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/OnlineHubPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-25.

- **2026-04-25:** **Persona 5 "stamped tag" tile pattern — pure CSS, scroll-grid safe.** OnlineHubPanel tamer tiles redesigned from "clean horizontal row" into P5-style identity stamps in the Beastborne palette. The recipe, all of which works in s&box: (1) FIVE absolute-positioned sibling layers BEFORE the avatar/info content — `.tile-bg-block` (purple gradient on left ~110px wide), `.tile-halftone` (twin-slash purple flourish via 135deg multi-stop gradient), `.tile-corner-cut` (gold diagonal accent line in top-right via 225deg hard-stop gradient), `.tile-rail-bar` (gold L→R fade across bottom 3px), and an optional index/selected stamp. (2) Foreground content (avatar, info, lv-pill) explicitly z-index: 2-3 to lift above stamp layers. (3) Stamp layers use `background-image: linear-gradient(...)` — gradient image overlays DO stack above parent `background-color` in s&box, unlike full-fill `<img>` overlays which paint behind (the existing absolute-bg-layer quirk). (4) NO transform/box-shadow on hover or selected — only border-color, background-color, and gradient-stop swaps on the rail/cut layers. (5) LV chip restyled as flat black-on-gold solid rectangle with 2px dark border + 1px hard offset shadow (no soft blur — stamps don't blur). DEV badge same recipe. (6) Italic 900-weight uppercase typography with 1px hard-offset text-shadow gives "stamped on" feel.
