(7) "01" italic gold index numeral on the YOU tile sits at top-right inside the gold corner-cut triangle, replaced by a "YOU" gold rectangle stamp when selected (CSS hides the index via `&.selected .tile-index-stamp { opacity: 0 }`). Anti-patterns avoided: data URI SVG backgrounds (unverified in s&box), `repeating-linear-gradient` (banned), `transform: rotate` for diagonal accents (would leak past scroll-grid clip), colored box-shadow halos for selection (same leak risk). The asymmetric color block + diagonal cut + bottom rail + corner stamp, taken together, transforms a tidy info row into something with unmistakable visual identity. Reusable for any "identity card" tile in a scrolling grid where the P5 stamp aesthetic suits. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/OnlineHubPanel.razor(.scss)` `.tamer-tile` family redesign 2026-04-25.

- **2026-04-25:** **Light P5 polish pass on global navigation: vocabulary not intensity.** Bottom-bar tabs in `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor(.scss)` got a subset of the OnlineHubPanel/LeaderboardPanel "stamp" treatment — but deliberately quieter, because navigation is glanced at 100x/session and shouldn't compete with content. What CARRIED OVER from the tile vocabulary: (a) gold rail under active tab using `background-image: linear-gradient(to right, #fbbf24 0%, #fbbf24 65%, rgba(251,191,36,0) 100%)` matching `.tile-rail-bar` exactly, 3px tall vs the prior 2px violet bar; (b) italic / 900 / `letter-spacing: 1.5px` on the active label only — directly lifting `.tile-name` typography but ONLY for the engaged tab so inactive labels stay visually quieter (font-weight differential becomes the active-state cue alongside the gold rail); (c) `.cb-tab-key` keyboard hint pills restyled as flat black-on-gold stamps with `1px 1px 0` hard-offset shadow, mirroring `.lb-section-tag`/`.tile-level-pill`. What was DELIBERATELY OMITTED: halftone slash flourishes (too busy for nav), full purple gradient bg blocks (would loud-up every tab when only one should hero), italic "01/02/03" index numerals (would confuse with the keyboard hints which are now THE stamp). Net move: bar still reads as muted nav at rest, but the active tab now looks "stamped on" instead of "tinted purple". Lesson: when porting a heavy content-tier visual treatment to global UI, don't import the whole vocabulary — pick the one or two layers with the highest signal-to-noise (rail bar + stamp typography on active state, P5 stamp on the small chip) and drop everything else. Restraint is what makes the borrowed elements feel intentional rather than copy-pasted. Source: `Code/UI/GameHUD.razor.scss` `.cb-tab` family 2026-04-25.

- **2026-04-25:** **Secondary P5-stamp slab — the lighter sibling scale for sort/filter chrome (vs the heavyweight header-tab variant).** Once the header-pill stamp pattern landed (see entry below), sort/filter buttons across the project still had the OLD generic outlined treatment (`padding: 8px 14px; border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.18); border-radius: 8px; faint purple active fill`) — they read as a different UI generation from the new header tabs. The fix: a deliberate "lighter sibling" scale that keeps the stamp DNA but steps everything down so it doesn't fight headers for hierarchy attention. The recipe: `padding: 8px 14px` (header is 11/20), `font-size: 13px / 800 / uppercase / letter-spacing: 1.2` (header is 14px / 800 / 1.5), `border-radius: 4px`, `border: 2px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55)`, `box-shadow: 2px 2px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4)` (header is 3px), bg `rgba(20, 14, 32, 0.92)`. Active state: same purple fill `rgba(88, 48, 150, 0.85)` with `box-shadow: 3px 3px 0 rgba(124, 58, 237, 0.4)` (header active is 4px). Pure color identity (element/rarity/destructive/fusion) goes on the active border-color + offset-shadow color + a tinted slab body, NEVER on the default-state slab body — slab body always stays in the dark-purple family so all variants read as siblings of one component. Same anti-rainbow pattern AchievementPanel `.ach-tab` already uses for its 10 category tabs. Applied to: `FilterBar.dropdown-btn` + `.sort-direction-btn` (shared component, propagates to Roster/Beastiary/Breeding/Arena/Expedition/Trading/TeamPicker), `InventoryPanel.rarity-pill` + `.sort-btn`, `MonsterRosterPanel.sort-btn` (with per-action color id: fusion=purple, multi-select=red, release-confirm=solid-red), `ShopPanelContent.dropdown-btn` + `.sort-btn`. Deliberately SKIPPED: `NotificationPanel.nf-pill` (underline-tab idiom, distinct intentional pattern in a tighter popup; slab would feel cramped), `FeedbackPanel.sort-toggle/.sort-opt` (rounded segmented control — different idiom, considered design), `FeedbackPanel.tag-chip` (tiny rounded tag chip with inline color, would inflate awkwardly in a packed toolbar). **Lesson:** a shared visual family doesn't mean every clickable target gets the same chrome. Reserve the slab pattern for "this is a meaningful filter/sort control" — keep segmented controls and tiny tag chips in their own idioms when they're already serving the panel's information density well. The hierarchy is: heavyweight stamp (header tabs, 11/20) > secondary stamp (sort/filter, 8/14) > rounded segmented controls (FeedbackPanel sort-toggle) > tiny tag chips (FeedbackPanel tag-chip, NotificationPanel nf-pill). Source: `Code/UI/Components/FilterBar.razor.scss`, `Code/UI/Panels/{Inventory,MonsterRoster,ShopPanelContent}.razor.scss` 2026-04-25 sort/filter unification pass.

- **2026-04-25:** **Canonical "P5 stamp tab pill" header pattern lives in `OnlineHubPanel.razor.scss` `.hub-pill` and is now ported to Inventory, Achievement, Daily, Feedback.** The recipe (transferable to any tabbed top-level panel): (1) Header bar at `padding: 14px 20px`, `rgba(8, 6, 18, 0.5-0.95)` bg, faint purple bottom rule, soft drop shadow, `headerSlideIn 0.3s ease-out` entrance. (2) Title group: 24×24 icon + 24px/700 white title, uppercase, `letter-spacing: 1.5px`, `line-height: 24` (s&box quirk #1). (3) Pill cluster wrapper: `tab-bg.webp` background image, 10px radius, faint purple border (`rgba(139, 92, 246, 0.15)`), `padding: 4px 8px`. (4) Each pill: `padding: 11px 20px` (or `11px 16px` if 5+ tabs), `border-radius: 4px`, `background-color: rgba(20, 14, 32, 0.92)`, `border: 2px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55)`, `box-shadow: 3px 3px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4)` — the hard offset shadow is THE signature; no blur, fixed offset. Label is 13-14px / 800 / uppercase / `letter-spacing: 1.4-1.5px`. (5) Active state: `rgba(88, 48, 150, 0.85)` purple fill, purple border, `4px 4px 0 rgba(124, 58, 237, 0.4)` matching purple offset shadow. For per-category accent (Achievement, Feedback), keep the dark-purple hue as a base and ONLY swap the border-color + offset-shadow color to the category accent — keeps every tab reading as a sibling of the same component. (6) Optional badge inside pill: `font-size: 10px / 800`, dark bg with white border, `border-radius: 5px`, `padding: 2px 7px`. **Per-panel SCSS DRY decision:** s&box doesn't reliably support SCSS `@import` partials, so each panel gets its own copy of the rules with renamed selectors (`.inv-pill`, `.fb-tab`, `.ach-tab`, `.sort-btn` for Daily). Class-name collisions across panels would also work because each `.razor.scss` is panel-scoped, but renaming makes the encapsulation explicit and avoids surprises if scoping changes. **Title-only panels** (Breeding, Credits, SkillTree) get just the chrome — 24px title, slide-in, faint purple bottom rule — without inventing fake tabs. Skipped panels: Quest/Help (vertical sidebar, different layout), MonsterRoster/Beastiary (already roster-canonical with FilterBar), WorldMap/Arena (intentionally headerless), GuildPanel/LeaderboardPanel (embedded inside OnlineHubPanel — parent's header serves them). Source: `Code/UI/Panels/{Inventory,Achievement,Daily,Feedback,Breeding,Credits,SkillTree}Panel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-25 cross-panel polish pass.

- **2026-04-24:** **GuildPanel plaza redesigned to push P5 vocabulary harder.** Previously the plaza had P5 ingredients (hard 2px black borders, 4px offset shadows on cards, P5 stage wash) but applied politely — soft circular emblem, centered name, plain text-button danger bar, perks rendered as plain rows. After redesign: angular emblem SLAB on banner with corner-cut accent; LEFT-aligned 30px italic uppercase name; three rotated stamp pills (TAG / LV / RANK) under name; 18px chunky XP slab with overlaid % text; monuments get index stamps ("01" gold, "BOSS" red), corner-cut accents and halftone hatches; ROSTER and PERKS yellow stamp headers on bottom-row boxes with halftone bg lattice; member portrait cards become P5 mini-stamps with 2px border + offset shadow + 13px italic name + role-color underline stripe; perk rows get hard offset shadows + 4px yellow LEFT-edge accent strip on unlocked + LOCKED corner stamp on locked + yellow icon disc + rotated yellow LV stamp pill; danger bar becomes "DANGER ZONE" labeled cluster of slab buttons (LEAVE vermillion `#dc2626`, DISBAND deeper crimson `#7f1d1d`, claim gold, transfer dark) all with hard borders + offset shadows + uppercase italic letterspaced labels; plaza-wide subtle halftone overlay. Total: ~12 new SCSS class families. **Constraints honored throughout:** no `transform: rotate` on any clickable element (only on `pointer-events: none` decorative chips/stamps), per-side border with style word avoided via absolute child div (`.perk-row-edge`), all radial-gradients use bare-stops, `background-color:` always explicit, halftones via `background-size` tiling on a single bare-stop radial. **Patterns reused from OnlineHubPanel:** `.tile-corner-cut` 225deg gradient → `.banner-emblem-cut` + `.monument-cut`; `.tile-halftone` 135deg multi-stop → `.monument-halftone`; `.tile-level-pill` flat black-on-gold stamp → `.banner-stamp.lv-stamp` + `.perk-row-level-stamp`; `.tile-selected-stamp` → `.plaza-box-stamp` (ROSTER/PERKS); italic 900 uppercase typography → `.banner-name` + `.member-portrait-name` + slab buttons. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/GuildPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-24 plaza P5 polish pass.

- **2026-04-25:** **Header-family port pass 3 — finishing the long-tail panels (Roster/Beastiary/Quest/Help/Guild).** After two earlier passes touched the obvious tabbed panels (Inventory/Achievement/Daily/Feedback) and gave a chrome-only treatment to single-screen popups (Breeding/Credits/SkillTree), the user reported "I see no changes" — meaning the day-to-day surfaces (Roster/Beastiary in particular) still wore the old chrome. Root cause was scope conservatism, not a global stylesheet — each panel SCSS is self-contained. The resolution: pick the closest treatment per panel and ship it. **Final per-panel decisions:** Roster + Beastiary already had the canonical FilterBar `tab-bg.webp` wrapper (matches `.hub-pills` exactly) — only needed the 22→24px title bump + 24px icon + line-height 24 to align with the family. Quest got a real chrome upgrade: flattened the purple 42×42 `.header-icon-wrap` square (read as a "category badge" misaligning with the lighter family) into a flat 24px iconify, swapped `rgb(22, 18, 35)` solid bg → `rgba(8, 6, 18, 0.5)` + faint purple border + soft drop shadow, dropped letter-spacing on the title (was 1px, family is 0), added `headerSlideIn` keyframe. Help got the same token swap plus a NEW icon (`lucide:book-open`) and uppercased title — was text-only before. GuildPanel's nested `.guild-header-bar` (used by browse + create-guild views) bumped 22→24, dropped `letter-spacing: 2px`, swapped `padding: 0 4px` → `padding: 14px 20px` with the family bg/border/shadow tokens. **Skip list with reasons (record so future passes don't re-litigate):** WorldMapPanel — explicit "no chrome by design, map is full-bleed" comment in the .razor; ProfilePanel — modal whose hero-banner IS the title zone, replacing it would erase the panel's identity; ExpeditionPanel — old `.panel-header` is gated behind `if (false &&` (dead code, header is unreachable since the world-map flow took over); TradingPanel — `.trade-header` is a themed trade-window bar (you-avatar / TRADE brand / partner-avatar), not navigation chrome, restyling would erase the design intent; ArenaPanel — comment "Header removed — Online tab bar handles navigation" makes it deliberately headerless. **Tip for future similar passes:** `headerSlideIn` is referenced across many panels but each `.razor.scss` is scoped, so EVERY panel that uses it must define the keyframe locally. Roster referenced it without defining it (silently skipped the entrance animation) — added the `0% { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(-6px); } 100% { opacity: 1; transform: translateY(0); }` keyframe to match the family. Same fix needed on Quest + Help. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/{MonsterRoster,Beastiary,Quest,Help,Guild}Panel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-25 third header-port pass.


- **2026-04-24:** **GuildPanel Browse and Raid surfaces redesigned to plaza P5 vocabulary.** Two surfaces remained text-only/old after the plaza polish: (1) BROWSE GUILDS list — was a text-only `.browse-row` with tiny emblem + concatenated stats string; redesigned as `.browse-card-v2` 76px-tall slabs with per-guild emblem-colored LEFT-edge stripe (4px absolute child div), top-right corner-cut diagonal in emblem color (inline `style="background-image: linear-gradient(225deg, ...)"` so the per-guild color flows through), bottom-right `.browse-card-halftone` decorative slash, P5 tag pill (purple solid w/ italic uppercase white text, rotated -2°, pointer-events:none), italic uppercase 17px white name with text-shadow, meta chips (members + RP + locked indicator), and a gold "Lv N" stamp pill on the right rotated -2°. Also added `.browse-topbar` with a plaza-style `.browse-title-slab` (gold corner-cut + italic uppercase title) plus refresh + back as `.plaza-icon-btn`. (2) RAID DETAIL — boss hero card got `.p5-stamp` surface override (hard 2px black border + 4px offset shadow), element-color corner-cut, vermillion halftone slash, rotated "BOSS" stamp in the top-right (vermillion fill, italic 13px uppercase white, -6°), bigger boss name (24→30px italic 900 uppercase), and `.raid-stamp-pill` element/level/rarity tags as solid-fill rotated stamps. Attack button became `.raid-attack-slab` chunky vermillion (`#b91c1c` → `#dc2626` hover) with hard border + 3px offset shadow + sword icon + italic uppercase letterspaced label. Pip area gained an `.raid-attempts-stamp` "X / Y USED" mini-stamp badge alongside the pips (clearer than the plain "0 of 4 used" text). Combo card got `.combo-edge` 3px LEFT-edge accent stripes per row (in the combo's color via inline `style`) — same absolute-child pattern as `.perk-row-edge`. Topbar got a `.raid-title-slab` "RAID — [boss name]" with vermillion corner-cut. Personal card + leaderboard got the `.p5-stamp` class for surface consistency. Also tightened the landing hero-card and action-cards to drop colored halo glow (which leaks past clipping) in favor of the hard 4-5px black offset shadow stamp surface. **Constraints honored:** no `transform: rotate` on clickable elements (only on `pointer-events: none` decorative pills/stamps); per-guild colors flow through via inline `style="background-image: linear-gradient(...)"` on the corner-cut div (gradient-in-style works fine in s&box, just not `url("...")` with quotes); browse cards live inside `.guild-content-card` (overflow-y: scroll) so they have NO box-shadow + NO transform on the parent (per the scroll-clip rule), while raid scene cards CAN have shadows because the parent's overflow is `scroll` but the cards are inline-flow and any leak between cards reads as intentional separation; `text-overflow: ellipsis` avoided on the name (which is in a flex child) — used `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0;` to let it clip naturally. **No functionality changes — pure restyle.** Source: `Code/UI/Panels/GuildPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-24 browse + raid P5 polish.


- **2026-04-28:** **ProfilePanel restamped to the canonical Persona-5 vocabulary, with rank-color driving the hard offset shadows.** The panel was a holdover from the old soft-card era — 12-16px rounded radii, 1px hairline `rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` borders, soft purple halo glow on the modal frame, circular avatar gradient frame, gem-style XP/discovery bars. Restyled top-to-bottom into the project's stamp family (matches PatchNotes / Roadmap / SkillTree / OnlineHub vocabulary). **Per-zone mapping (record for similar future restamps of "old" modal panels):** modal frame → `border-radius: 4px`, `border: 2px solid rgba(0,0,0,0.65)`, **inline-style `box-shadow: 6px 6px 0 GetRankShadow()`** so the offset color is keyed to the player's arena rank (Mythic = pink-deep, Legendary = gold-deep, etc — full mapping in `GetRankShadow()` in the .razor). Skewed corner-tag at `top: -14px; left: -8px` says "TAMER · LV N" in rank fill color. Hero banner kept its bg-image + dropped the soft 12-radius for sharp 4-radius, added a `radial-gradient(rgba(167,139,250,0.18) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 70%)` corner halftone (percent stops only, no shape keyword). Avatar slab: 4px corner, 2px rank-color border, `4px 4px 0 GetRankShadow()` hard offset, sealed by a tiny `.avatar-rank-stamp` (sharp 3px slab, slight `-6deg` rotate, rank-color fill) pinned INSIDE the avatar bounds at `bottom: 4px / right: 4px` (NOT -8px — the parent `.profile-hero` has `overflow: hidden` for hero-bg clipping, so overhanging children get clipped). Level "1" → italic 48px / 900 / `letter-spacing: -0.02em` / `text-shadow: 2px 2px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.75)` with line-height 24 (s&box font-clip quirk preserved). XP/discovery bars: dark inner with 1.5px black border + 1px hard offset → reads as a "stamped slot". Each section (CAREER STATS / FAVORITES / PROGRESSION) got a colored stamp head pill (`.profile-stamp-head` with `tone-purple/pink/blue/gold` modifiers), same DNA as `patchnotes-section-head`. Stat cards + prog pills became P5 stamp slabs with category-tinted hard offset shadows (pink/red for stats, blue/teal/gold/purple/pink for prog). Diamond chip bullets (8-10px square rotated 45°, 1.5px black border, 1px hard offset) sit on each prog pill at `top: -4px; left: -4px` and on each arena-stat pill — same vocabulary as `patchnotes-bullet`. Action buttons: full-width purple stamp slabs (`background: rgba(88,48,150,0.78); 3px 3px 0 rgba(124,58,237,0.65)`), with the count chip as a tiny rotated yellow stamp pill embedded in the button face. Footer/playtime/created-date got 10px / 800 / uppercase / 0.14em letter-spacing + 1px text-shadow stamp typography. **Critical razor changes alongside the SCSS:** added `GetRankShadow()` C# helper (mirrors `GetRankColorDark` but returns rgba strings tuned for hard-offset use — slightly more saturated than the rank fill). Updated `GetTitle()` and `GetEarnedLevelTitles()` to the launch-cap-of-50 ladder: Lv 1 Tamer / 10 Apprentice / 20 Skilled / 30 Expert / 40 Master / 50 Legendary (was 60 Master / 80 Legendary which were unreachable since the cap is 50). Resource pills got `gold-tone/gems-tone/ink-tone/tokens-tone` modifier classes on the wrapper div (NOT just on the inner value span) so the whole slab carries the currency identity. **Lessons:** (1) Inline-style hard offset shadows are the right call when the shadow color is data-driven (rank, guild emblem, element type) — keeps SCSS rules generic, makes per-instance theming readable in the razor. (2) When restamping an old soft modal, drop `overflow: hidden` from the modal container so corner tags + hard offset shadows can extend past panel bounds — but KEEP `overflow: hidden` on the inner hero banner that's holding a bg-image, and tuck overhanging child stamps INSIDE the hero bounds (no negative `top`/`left`/`bottom`/`right` on children of overflow:hidden ancestors). (3) When updating level-tier title lists, search BOTH the lookup function (`GetTitle()`) AND the earned-list (`GetEarnedLevelTitles()`) — they're separate code paths and easily drift out of sync. (4) `flex-wrap: wrap` removal from `.progression-pills` in favor of a single 5-pill row required dropping `min-width: 90px` to `min-width: 0` + `flex: 1 1 0` to keep narrow-pill layouts working, since 5 pills × 90px would push past 600px modal width. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-28 P5-stamp restyle pass.

- **2026-04-28:** **Inline-style box-shadow is the cleanest way to drive a panel's hard offset shadow off a runtime data value (rank tier, guild color, element type).** Pattern: `<div class="my-slab" style="box-shadow: 3px 3px 0 @(GetRankShadow())">`. Generic SCSS rule sets `border-radius`, `border`, base layout. Inline style overrides only the box-shadow color. Works without conflict because s&box honors inline `style` over external selectors. Compare to the alternative — N rank-class variants (`.rank-mythic`, `.rank-legendary`, ...) each with a `box-shadow` override — which works but bloats the SCSS, makes adding new variants painful (new C# enum + new SCSS rule + remember to also wire the modifier class), and decouples the shadow color from the data (you have to hop to the SCSS file to see what each rank gets). The inline pattern keeps the data → visual mapping in one place (a single C# helper that returns the rgba string), and SCSS stays generic. Caveat: this only works when the shadow has a single dynamic value; if you also need different OFFSETS per rank (4px vs 6px), the SCSS modifier-class approach scales better. For Beastborne's rank theming (offsets stay constant, only color shifts), inline-style is the right call. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor` `.profile-modal` + `.avatar-frame` + `.arena-showcase` + `.rank-pill` 2026-04-28.

- **2026-04-28:** **Layout cadence variance is its own design directive — "everything horizontal" is a smell even when individual rows look fine.** Followup pass on ProfilePanel got the explicit user note: *"not everything needs to be a horizontal thing."* The previous restamp had stamped vocabulary right but kept a monotonous "every zone is a flex row of 3-5 pills" cadence (resources row, two stat-card rows, prog-pill row, footer row). The fix wasn't a vocabulary change — it was breaking the cadence by mixing layouts. **Recipes that worked for breaking horizontal monotony in a 600px-wide modal:** (1) **Detail + sidebar pair** — arena slab (flex:1 on the left) beside a vertical resources column (fixed 130px on the right). Replaces "stacked horizontal rows" with the canonical Roster/Beastiary detail-sidebar split. (2) **Asymmetric featured stat + grid** — one big italic-skew "battles won" stamp slab (flex: 0 0 168px) beside a 3-col stats mini grid (the other 6 numbers as small slabs). Mirrors the Roadmap's "headline column + supporting bullets" recipe. ExpeditionPanel uses the same shape. (3) **Side-by-side stamp slabs with asymmetric weights** — favorite-beast slab (flex: 1.4) beside collection slab (flex: 1). Two related concepts each in their own contained box, instead of a single wide row. (4) **Progress meter rows replacing pill grids** — for any "X of Y" data (achievements, skill ranks), upgrade from a tiny value-only pill to a labeled gradient meter strip (label left, value right, gradient bar below). Lifted directly from Beastiary's `.bb-stat-bar-fill` pattern; re-uses the project's bar gradient vocabulary (`linear-gradient(to right, ...)`) so it visually rhymes with the XP bar in the same panel. **The principle:** in a multi-zone modal, AT LEAST 2-3 of the zones should be doing something OTHER than `display: flex; flex-direction: row`. Having one detail-pane zone, one asymmetric featured zone, and one vertical-meter zone reads as designer-cadenced; six identical horizontal pill rows reads as template-filled. **Anti-pattern to avoid:** `flex-wrap: wrap` for "responsive grid" — s&box miscalculates wrapped flex height. Use explicit row-children instead (e.g. two `.stats-mini-row` divs each with 3 stat slabs). Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-28 layout-variation pass.

- **2026-04-28:** **Replacing emoji currency icons with iconify lucide icons for parity with the rest of the UI.** ProfilePanel was carrying inline emoji (🪙 💎 🖋 🏆) inside `.resource-icon-text` spans — works but reads as a fallback / "I forgot to do this properly" once everything else uses lucide. Pattern that swaps in cleanly: replace the emoji span with `<span class="resource-icon-wrap"><iconify icon="lucide:coins" Size=@(14) Color="#fde68a" /></span>`. The wrapper span just needs `display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; flex-shrink: 0; width/height: 18px`. The iconify component carries its own `Size` attribute so SCSS doesn't need a `font-size` for the icon. Drop the old `.resource-icon-text { font-size: 16px }` rule. **Currency → lucide name mapping used in this project (record for future panels):** gold/coins → `lucide:coins`, gems → `lucide:gem`, contract ink → `lucide:feather` (or `lucide:pen-tool`), boss tokens → `lucide:trophy`. Pass `Color` matching the value text color so icon + value read as a unit. ~~Don't replace decorative Unicode glyphs that aren't emoji — chess crowns (♛ ♔ ♚) used as rank icons via GetRankIcon() are intentional and render fine; only swap when the source is actually an emoji codepoint masquerading as an icon.~~ **[OUTDATED — superseded 2026-04-28]:** user explicitly asked for the rank Unicode glyphs (★ ◆ ♦ ◇ ● ○ ▪ ▫) to be swapped to lucide as well — they were rendering thin / font-dependent (especially the unranked "○" reading as an awkward gray oval). New mapping added below. **The general principle going forward:** if a project decision is "iconify icons everywhere," that includes Unicode glyph icons used as rank/state indicators, not just emoji. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor` resource pills 2026-04-28; rank-glyph swap 2026-04-28 followup.

- **2026-04-28:** **Rank glyphs → lucide iconify mapping (canonical for Beastborne competitive UI).** Replaces the Unicode `★ ◆ ♦ ◇ ● ○ ▪ ▫` glyphs that `CompetitiveManager.GetRankIcon()` returns. The Unicode glyphs render thin and font-dependent — the unranked `○` in particular looks like a small empty oval inside its stamp slab, far from the "you have no rank yet" beat it should signal. **Mapping:** Unranked → `lucide:user-round`, Bronze → `lucide:shield-half`, Silver → `lucide:shield`, Gold → `lucide:award`, Platinum → `lucide:medal`, Diamond → `lucide:gem` (NOT `lucide:diamond` — that one's just a rotated square in lucide; `gem` is the unambiguous gem-cut shape), Master → `lucide:trophy`, Legendary → `lucide:crown`, Mythic → `lucide:sparkles`. The hierarchy escalates in visual weight (half-shield → shield → award → medal → gem → trophy → crown → sparkles). **Implementation pattern:** add a `GetRankLucideIcon()` static method alongside the existing `GetRankIcon()` (don't remove the legacy one — `TamerCardShowcasePopup`, `ArenaPanel`, etc still consume it; can be migrated later). Razor sites swap `<span class="rank-icon-text">@GetRankIcon()</span>` for `<iconify icon="@GetRankLucideIcon()" Size=@(N) Color="#1a0f30" />` — color `#1a0f30` reads cleanly against the rank-color stamp slab fills. Sizes: 14 for the avatar-sealed stamp, 12 for the rank pill, 28 for the arena-showcase rank-icon-square. **For pill markup,** wrap the iconify + label in their own spans (`<span class="rank-pill"><iconify .../><span class="rank-pill-text">@rank</span></span>`) since the `<span>` no longer contains both the glyph and text in one node — needs explicit `display: flex; gap: 5px; align-items: center` on the parent pill. Source: `Code/Core/CompetitiveManager.cs:1506-1525` (new `GetRankLucideIcon`); `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor` rank-icon sites 2026-04-28.

- **2026-05-01:** **ExpeditionPanel has large dead-code zones (gated `@if (false && currentView == "list")`) — only the team-view markup actually renders.** Since WorldMapPanel became the expedition entry point and ExpeditionResultPopup took over the result screen, the legacy "list" and "result" branches in `ExpeditionPanel.razor` (lines 26-489 and 621-711) are unreachable. Same for the monsters-popup, drops-popup, contract-target-search popup, and running-expedition-bar — all of those are nested inside the `@if (false ...)` block or triggered by buttons that only exist there. **Implication for future style passes:** when bringing this panel up to a new dialect (or doing any visual polish), the live styles to actually touch are: `.panel-title` / `.roster-title` / `.team-counter` / `.team-label` / `.btn-primary` (BEGIN EXPEDITION) / `.team-slot` / `.monster-option` / `.no-monsters` / `.available-monsters` / `.team-section` / `.action-bar` and team-view-specific selectors. Skip everything around `.expedition-card`, `.dropdown-btn`, `.auto-battle-toggle`, `.monsters-info-btn`, `.popup-*`, `.section-header` (popup variant), `.contract-search-*`, `.running-expedition-bar`, `.btn-danger`, `.result-view` — they're all dead. The dead CSS still works as a record but polishing it is wasted effort. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ExpeditionPanel.razor` P5-stamp dialect pass 2026-05-01 — punch list shrunk from 15+ candidates to 6 actual live-rendering elements after gating audit.

- **2026-05-01:** **The "Persona-stamp" recipe ported from OnlineHubPanel `.hub-pill` (line ~111) is now the canonical primary-action button shape across the codebase.** Specifically: `padding: 11px 20px; border-radius: 4px; background-color: rgba(20, 14, 32, 0.92); border: 2px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55); box-shadow: 3px 3px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4);` for the surface, and `font-size: 14px; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: 1.5; text-transform: uppercase; color: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.85); line-height: 16;` for the label. Active state bumps shadow to `4px 4px 0 rgba(124, 58, 237, 0.4)` (purple-tinted). When porting to a new panel, the dialect rule of thumb is: **header pill / tab pill / tag** = 3px3px shadow at 0.4 alpha; **hero CTA** = 4px4px-5px5px shadow at 0.45-0.55 alpha (with rank/element-color tint on hover); **internal section title slab** = 2px2px shadow at 0.45 alpha (one notch quieter than the surrounding interactive surfaces). Numerically larger offsets (5+px) are reserved for hover/setpiece states — base state stays at 3-4. Italic + uppercase + letterspacing always travel together for label typography; never apply just italic without uppercase or vice versa. Source: ExpeditionPanel team-view dialect harmonization 2026-05-01 (49-line additive pass); cross-referenced with OnlineHubPanel:111, GuildPanel:751/4097/4124/4291, WorldMapPanel:152.

- **2026-05-03:** **Per-entity compounding rule for stamped nameplates: scope the FULL stamp treatment to ONE instance per axis; revert duplicates to the soft drop-shadow recipe.** When porting the Persona-stamp dialect onto BattleView's 3D nameplates, applying `border: 2px solid black + box-shadow: 4px 4px 0` to ALL enemy cards would compound — multi-enemy waves stack 3+ nameplates vertically along the right edge, and 3+ hard-offset shadows in a column form a visual rail that reads as scene-framing chrome (the thing the battle UI charter explicitly forbids — see `feedback_battle_ui_layout.md` in MEMORY: "per-entity accents COMPOUND across multiple instances and become framing"). **Resolution:** the active enemy + the player nameplate (always 1 instance each) carry the full stamp. Benched enemy cards (`.secondary` selector — already in use for de-prioritized panels) revert to the prior soft yellow border + soft drop shadow + breathe animation. **Halftone is the exception** — at 0.06-0.08 opacity, repeating texture across 3+ benched cards does NOT compound visually; texture is texture, edge accents form rails. Halftone stays applied across all panels. **The general principle:** when adding a hard-offset shadow or saturated edge accent to a UI element type that can have N>1 instances on screen, ask "would this read as a column/row of accents at N=3?" If yes, gate the treatment to a primary instance and use a softer recipe for the rest. Same reasoning applies anywhere we have repeating panels — talent rows in skill tree, enemy rows in expedition combat preview, etc. **Reference site:** `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss` `.panel3d-card` (active stamp) vs `.secondary .panel3d-card` (soft revert), 2026-05-03 dual-typing + dialect upgrade pass.

- **2026-05-03:** **Dual-typed monster diamond pattern — replace the single chip with a vertical pair, half-size each.** When a beast has both `Element` and `SecondaryElement` (the data field already exists on `MonsterSpecies` since the design-pivot dual-typing work), the Razor renders a `.panel3d-diamond-stack` (flex-column, 4px gap) of two `.panel3d-diamond.dual-primary` and `.dual-secondary` chips at 46×46 each (vs 72×72 single). Total stack height is 96px — slightly taller than the single 72px diamond. Two layout details that matter: (1) `.panel3d-diamond-wrapper.dual-typed { align-items: flex-start; }` keeps the CARD top-aligned with single-typed peers — without it, flex `align-items: center` shifts dual-typed cards down ~10px relative to single-typed neighbors. (2) For benched (`.secondary`) enemies, shrink dual chips to 36×36 to fit the smaller 60px slot AND revert their box-shadow to the soft `0 3px 8px` drop (compounding rule applies inside dual chips too — three benched dual-typed enemies = six hard-stamp chips in a column otherwise). **Selector specificity gotcha:** `.secondary .panel3d-diamond` ALSO matches dual-primary/dual-secondary chips inside benched enemies (they're still `.panel3d-diamond`). To override correctly without losing the dual-stack sizing, the targeted rule must add specificity: `.secondary .panel3d-diamond-stack .panel3d-diamond.dual-primary` (4 classes) wins over both `.panel3d-diamond.dual-primary` (2) and `.secondary .panel3d-diamond` (2). Reference: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss:2918-2980` 2026-05-03.

- **2026-05-03:** **Per-element saturated stamp tints — the canonical palette ported from `.action3d-btn.swap/bag/contract` to `.panel3d-diamond.el-bg-*`.** When taking the action-cluster's flat-saturated stamp recipe to a new diamond-shaped element-tagged surface (like the 3D nameplate diamonds), reuse this palette so the whole battle HUD shares ONE color family: fire `rgba(96, 30, 24, 0.96)`, water `rgba(28, 50, 92, 0.96)`, nature `rgba(22, 60, 36, 0.96)`, electric `rgba(80, 70, 18, 0.96)`, ice `rgba(22, 60, 78, 0.96)`, earth `rgba(60, 42, 22, 0.96)`, wind `rgba(50, 56, 60, 0.96)`, metal `rgba(40, 42, 50, 0.96)`, shadow `rgba(58, 30, 92, 0.96)`, spirit `rgba(80, 28, 60, 0.96)`, neutral `rgba(15, 10, 25, 0.96)`. **Why these specific values:** picked to sit at the same saturation level as the action-cluster swap-blue/bag-green/contract-purple (rgba * * * 0.96), but pulled toward "deep / nighttime" so a white-tinted SVG icon stays legible at 0.85 opacity. **Implementation pattern:** declare ONE generic rule for the diamond shape (`.panel3d-diamond { border, box-shadow, neutral fallback bg }`), then layer per-element overrides as flat `.panel3d-diamond.el-bg-fire { background-color: ... }` rules — NOT a single rule with nested `&.el-bg-*` (the latter compiles fine but reads worse and the per-element overrides need their own selector for the `.secondary .panel3d-diamond.el-bg-*` mute-revert cascade to work cleanly). Same rules duplicated for `.panel3d-diamond-secondary` for the dual-typing companion. Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss:2922-2932, 2985-2995` 2026-05-03 nameplate dialect pass.

- **2026-05-03:** **`.diamond-halftone` is a reusable child class — extend the selector list when porting it to a new diamond surface, don't duplicate the rule.** The action-cluster shipped `.action3d-diamond .diamond-halftone, .end-diamond .diamond-halftone { ... }` with `position: absolute; opacity: 0.18; transform: rotate(-45deg); inner img scaled to 142%`. When a new diamond-shaped surface (the 3D nameplate primary + secondary diamonds) needs the same halftone, the right move is to ADD selectors to the existing rule list (`.panel3d-diamond .diamond-halftone, .panel3d-diamond-secondary .diamond-halftone`), not write a fresh rule per surface. Razor edit pattern: `<div class="panel3d-diamond ...">` gets `<div class="diamond-halftone"><img src="ui/bg-patterns/bg-halftone.svg" /></div>` as its first child, then the icon `<img>`. Halftone needs `z-index: 0` (already in the rule); the icon needs an explicit `z-index: 1` override scoped to the new surface (`.panel3d-diamond .panel3d-diamond-icon { position: relative; z-index: 1 }`) — without it the halftone paints OVER the icon. **Compounding rule still applies inside diamonds:** at N>1 instances on screen, drop the halftone opacity from 0.18 → 0.08 on the secondary/benched variants so the dot pattern doesn't form a visible texture rail. Source: `Code/UI/Components/BattleView.razor.scss:3700-3729` (halftone selector list) + 2922-2932 (per-element bg) + 3145-3164 (`.secondary` mute-revert) 2026-05-03.

- **2026-06-03:** `[PROMOTE candidate]` **`transition-delay` WORKS as of s&box 26.06.03 — the 2026-04-09 "never use transition-delay" learning is now OUTDATED.** The 26.06.03 CSS-parser rewrite added real `transition-delay` / `-duration` / `-property` / `-timing-function`, plus `min()/max()/clamp()`, `:has()`, `currentColor`, `oklch()/lab()/hwb()`, `dvh/svh`, `overflow:auto`, `filter:none`, `inset` shorthand, and the parser now RECOVERS from a bad rule instead of bailing the whole stylesheet. **This unlocks the proper staggered-reveal idiom: per-element `style="transition-delay: 0.Ns"` (inline so each row carries its own offset) + a default hidden state + a `.visible` parent class that flips the settled state.** All delays count from the instant `.visible` is added, so flipping one C# bool gives a clean cascade — no more per-row `await Task.Delay` flag-flipping. Used on BreedingPanel's fusion-result gene table (6 rows + total + quality cascade 40→500ms). **Still hard-broken in 26.06.03 (do NOT use):** unitless `line-height` is now a MULTIPLIER (always px), `box-sizing: border-box`, `position: fixed`, `display: block`, `inline-flex`, `transparent` keyword inside gradients, `radial-gradient` shape keywords / px stops, `object-position` % pairs, `box-shadow: inset`, `:focus-within`, `repeating-linear-gradient`. And `@keyframes` STILL only fire on first DOM mount — for re-openable panels keep using transitions (default state on element, override under `.visible`/`.active` parent) so they replay every open. Source: BreedingPanel T4 fusion-cinematic pass 2026-06-03; engine-version note from the task brief.

- **2026-06-03:** **T4 reveal arc recipe (anticipation → slam → cascade), built without `@keyframes`, replays every open.** Raising BreedingPanel's fuse from a gentle 3-stage fade to evolution-grade used a 4-stage C#-driven sequence where each stage flips one `fusionStage` int and CSS transitions carry the motion: (0) mounted hidden; (1) wash darkens to 0.94 + a charge ring (`.result-charge`, clamp-sized disc, `scale(0.4)→1 rotate 0→150deg`) spins up over a ~620ms HELD anticipation beat with a soft "gathering" sound; (2) a flash (`.result-flash`, gated on `fusionStage == 2` so it auto-fades when stage advances) blooms as the portrait SLAMS in (`scale(0.55)→1` on `cubic-bezier(0.16,0.9,0.28,1.32)` overshoot) on a vertical light pillar (`.result-pillar`, `height: 0→176px` gradient column behind the sprite via `z-index: -1`) + kicker kern-opens (`letter-spacing 2→4`) + catch-impact sound; (3) gene rows cascade via inline `transition-delay` + success chime. **The held anticipation beat is the load-bearing part** — without the ~600ms dark pause where the charge ring builds, the slam has nothing to land against and reads as Tier 2, not Tier 4. **Anti-gacha check passed:** every effect celebrates the deterministic result arriving, not an RNG roll being concealed — it would read identically with only one possible offspring (the Hades-not-HSR test). The panel frame stays `opacity:0` until stage 2 so the wash + charge own the empty dark stage during anticipation. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/BreedingPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-04-28:** **"A little too Persona 5" is a real and recurring failure mode of the stamp vocabulary — the fix is to keep the iconic SHELL but soften INTERNAL slabs.** The ProfilePanel went through four polishing passes and at each pass the user said it still felt overstamped. The trap: the stamp vocabulary (sharp 4px corners, 2px black borders, hard offset rank-color shadows, halftone, diamond bullets, hard text-shadows) is REALLY GOOD on the OUTERMOST layer of a panel, and gets progressively worse as you apply it to nested children inside that already-stamped shell. By the fifth pass we had: outer modal stamped (good), corner tag stamped (good), avatar stamped (good — it's the visual hero), arena slab stamped, favorite-beast slab stamped, discovery slab stamped, achievements meter stamped, win/loss/streak pills stamped — eight stamps fighting each other. **The recipe that landed:** keep the OUTER iconic shell at full P5 vocabulary (modal `border: 2px solid black`, `6px 6px 0 GetRankShadow()`, `border-radius: 4px`, corner tag at `-14px / -8px`, halftone behind hero, avatar slab with hard offset). Pull the INTERNAL slabs back to: thin subtle border (`1px solid rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.07)`), soft drop shadow (`0 2px 6px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3)`), gentle 8px corners, lighter bg fill. KEEP small accent elements as stamps (the corner tag, the rank pill, the title-edit button, the close button, the arena-stamp-tag — these are the "one P5 flourish per zone" that survives) but don't make EVERY container a stamp. **Strip text-shadows from internal numbers** (winrate, wins/losses, discovery count, achievements count) — keep the hard text-shadow ONLY on the giant `.level-number` (the panel's identity hero) and on text that sits over the busy hero banner art (`.tamer-name`, `.title-text`) where it's readability not flair. **Drop diamond chip bullets except in ONE place per card** — diamonds are flair, repeating them on every row turns flair into chrome. Achievements row keeps its diamond; arena-stat pills lost theirs (became plain colored dots). **For category-tinted slabs** (the achievements meter was the gold-flooded offender), replace the heavy treatment (tinted bg + thick colored border + hard offset colored shadow) with a slim 3px colored left-accent strip (`border-left: 3px solid rgba(251, 191, 36, 0.55)`) plus the colored gradient fill in the bar itself. The category color now KEYS the slab without DROWNING it. **For headline blocks** (the level block's bright yellow LEVEL pill was redundant with the giant level number directly below it), strip the pill chrome and let the eyebrow be a bare uppercase letterspaced label in `rgba(255,255,255,0.5)`. **The principle:** stamp vocabulary obeys diminishing returns. One iconic stamp (the outer modal + corner tag + avatar) reads as a strong design statement. Eight stacked stamps read as a screaming Persona 5 menu. When the user says "too Persona 5," the answer isn't to abandon Persona 5 — it's to PROTECT the iconic Persona 5 moments by calming everything around them. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-04-28 "less Persona" pass after four prior polish passes.

- **2026-06-03:** **DailyPanel Day-7 raised to T4 setpiece; routine claim stays T2 (restraint preserves the gap).** `ClaimDailyReward()` now branches on `GetTodayReward().IsDay7` → `PlayDay7Setpiece()` (a fullscreen sibling-of-modal overlay, z-index 2000) instead of the routine 12-particle burst. 4-stage C#-driven arc (~3.4s): (1) anticipation — scrim dims to 0.92 + an iris ring contracts toward center over a ~700ms HELD beat + click cue; (2) reveal — Master Ink slams UP a light pillar (`day7PrizeSlam` overshoot on cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)) + spotlight wash blooms + layered success/gold sound; (3) celebration — name slam banner (P5 italic-display at 52px), 12 god-rays fade in, 18 gold/purple confetti pieces arc out, manager `ClaimDailyReward()` committed mid-celebration so the GameHUD roll-up lands while rays blaze; (4) settle — everything eases out + swoosh. Anti-gacha safe: the prize (Master Ink) is shown plainly the WHOLE time, no silhouette-then-reveal tease — celebrates an EARNED 7-day streak, passes the Hades-not-HSR test. **Critical s&box pitfall hit + solved: stage-gated @keyframes via an ANCESTOR class (`.stage-3 .day7-prize`) do NOT replay when the ancestor class flips** (same root cause as the documented "@keyframes only fire on first DOM mount" — an ancestor class toggle is not a mount of the descendant). **Two fixes used together:** (a) mount each phase element via Razor `@if (day7Stage >= N)` so its intro @keyframe fires fresh on mount (iris, rays, banner, confetti); (b) for elements that must PERSIST across sub-phases (spotlight, pillar, prize-wrap), put a `phase-@day7Stage` class on the ELEMENT ITSELF (not an ancestor) — s&box DOES replay the animation when the element's own class string changes. Selector form: `.day7-prize-wrap.phase-2 { animation: slam }` / `.phase-3 { animation: idle }` / `.phase-4 { animation: out }`. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/DailyPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03 Phase-2 Day-7 setpiece pass.

- **2026-06-03:** **AchievementPanel raised T1→T3: staggered list entrance + claim celebration. Two non-obvious `transition-delay` gotchas worth remembering.** The list cascade uses inline per-row `style="transition-delay: {Math.Min(index,12)*0.035}s"` (capped at 12 rows so a long list finishes in <0.5s), gated behind the `AchievementPanel.visible` element-name selector so it replays every open; category switches remount rows so they re-fire for free (a `listVersion % 8` class on `.modal-scroll` is also bumped, kept modulo so an unbounded session counter never falls off the styled set). **Gotcha 1 — inline `transition-delay` lags EVERYTHING in the row's `transition` shorthand, including hover bg/border** (felt broken: hovers took up to 0.42s to respond). Fix: `&:hover { transition-delay: 0s; }` so only the one-shot entrance carries the delay. **Gotcha 2 — entrance `opacity` fights state-based dimming**: claimed rows rest at `opacity: 0.65`, but the generic `.visible .ach-row { opacity: 1 }` resting rule is more specific and was forcing claimed rows fully opaque. Fix: re-assert `.visible .ach-row.state-claimed { opacity: 0.65 }` with the extra class for higher specificity. The Tier-3 claim celebration (gold flash + 2 shockwave rings + 12-mote `cbP0..cbP11` radial burst at 30° apart + center reward card slam) is a `pointer-events:none` sibling of the modal inside `<root>`, mounted via `@if (claimCelebrationActive)` so its `@keyframes` fire fresh per claim (remount = the legit use of first-mount keyframes); the `AchievementManager.ClaimReward()` call is deferred ~0.42s (DailyPanel pattern) so the row flip + GameHUD currency roll-up land as the burst peaks, and display strings (name + first reward via `GetRewardText`) are captured BEFORE the deferred call flips the row to claimed. Claim All shares the overlay with `is-all` variant + a snapshot count. Anti-gacha clean: rewards are deterministic + already shown on the row; card says "CLAIMED," not "you got lucky." Source: `Code/UI/Panels/AchievementPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **DailyPanel open stagger converted from C#-stage-machine to real `transition-delay` (26.06.03).** The streak tab's 3-section reveal (hero → week strip → milestone) previously ticked `streakRevealStage` 1→2→3 with a `GameTask.DelaySeconds` between each flip, each gating its own `.entered`. Now `PlayStreakRevealSequence()` flips `streakRevealStage = 3` ONCE (arming all three `.entered` conditions simultaneously) and the visual cascade lives in CSS: `.today-hero.entered { transition-delay: 0s }`, `.week-strip.entered { 0.12s }`, `.milestone-line.entered { 0.24s }`. Smoother than the render-cadence-gated stage machine and not subject to the old ">=100ms apart or the flip gets missed" caveat. The per-section swoosh sound cues are KEPT in C# (timed to the CSS delays) because sound is UI — the audio staircase shouldn't collapse just because the visual one moved to CSS. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/DailyPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03 (adversarial review):** **A panel that is `@if`-MOUNTED FRESH every time it appears must use @keyframes for entrances, NOT `.visible`-parent transitions — the transition has no prior painted state to interpolate from and silently never fires.** ContractNegotiationPanel is wrapped in BattleView `@if (showNegotiationOverlay ...)` with `IsVisible=@true` HARDCODED, so the panel + its children mount directly into `.visible` in one synchronous `Initialize()` pass (sets IsVisible=true + StateHasChanged together, no deferred flip). An animation pass had converted the 4 approach-diamond fan-in and the bottom-chrome slide-up to `transition-delay` on `.contract-radial.visible`, with a comment claiming it "REPLAYS on every re-open." It does NOT: the element is destroyed + recreated per contract, never re-opened, and CSS transitions only fire when an ALREADY-PAINTED element changes state. Reverted to per-slot `@keyframes` (`fanInTop/Mid/Bottom` + `bottomSectionUp`) staggered via `animation-delay` (0.02/0.09/0.16/0.23s; chrome 0.28s) — keyframes fire on every fresh mount, which is exactly this case (and matches why the SAME panel correctly uses keyframes on its fresh-mounted result subtree). **The discriminator:** DailyPanel/AchievementPanel `transition-delay` works because their rows are persistent/remounted under a toggled `.entered`/`.visible` and get a painted rest frame first (deferred stage flip or category-switch remount). A fresh-`@if`-mount with the visible class already on = keyframes only. Decision rule: element toggled while staying painted -> transition; element `@if`-mounted fresh into its visible state -> @keyframes. Also fixed: the per-slot settled `transform` is authored statically on each slot (matching each keyframe%s 100%) so hover/selected/dimmed compose with the two centered slots translateY(-50%) without snapping. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ContractNegotiationPanel.razor.scss` + `BattleView.razor:1128-1134` adversarial review 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **CardCollectionPanel raised T1→T2: staggered collection entrance + NEW-card reveal.** Entrance: `.collected-card-wrapper` rests at `opacity: 0`; a `hasEntered` field flipped ~30ms after open (via `OnOpened()` edge-detected in `Tick()` on `IsVisible && !wasVisible`, reset on close) toggles `.entered` on `<root>`, and `.entered .card-grid .collected-card-wrapper { opacity: 1; transition: opacity 0.3s ease-out }` fades them in. Per-card `transition-delay` is supplied INLINE (`GetStaggerDelay(index)`, step 0.045s capped at 0.5s) formatted with `InvariantCulture` — float interpolation in inline styles otherwise emits a comma decimal on some locales and s&box rejects it. NEW-card reveal: `GetCollectedCards()` is sorted by `LastUpdated` desc so a freshly-collected card sits at index 0; `OnOpened()` flags `newCardSteamId` only if that top card's `CollectedAt` (NOT `LastUpdated` — an UPDATED existing card also bubbles to top but keeps its old CollectedAt, so this correctly fires only on genuinely new cards) is within 12s of now. The flagged card renders a `@if isNew` `.new-card-fx` subtree (ring + diagonal sheen sweep + rotated P5 "NEW" ribbon) whose `@keyframes` fire fresh because the element only mounts when new — the legit first-mount-keyframes case. **Scroll-clip safety:** the grid is inside `overflow-y: scroll`, so the reveal uses border-only ring (no colored box-shadow halo, which leaks past the scroll viewport per the known quirk) and keeps the ribbon INSIDE the card bounds (`top/right: 6px`, not overhanging negative offsets). The ring rests at `opacity: 0.5` (its keyframe ends there too) so the new card stays glance-findable after the one-shot burst — a @keyframes element snaps back to its CSS default when done, so the resting default must equal the desired persistent state. `SoundManager.PlaySuccess()` fires on a new card (T2 audio). Added `hasEntered` + `newCardSteamId` to BuildHash. TamerCardComponent usage preserved unchanged (wrapped, not modified). Source: `Code/UI/Panels/CardCollectionPanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **ProfilePanel raised to T3: open-entrance converted to replayable transitions + a rank-up celebration. Rank-up detection rides the existing `BuildHash`/`Tick` poll — no manager hook needed.** Entrance: the four-pass-polished panel previously used first-mount `@keyframes` (`heroFadeIn`/`zoneFadeIn`) that DON'T replay on re-open and animated transforms (snap-prone in 26.06.03). Replaced with the canonical replayable idiom: every staggered element rests at `opacity: 0` + `transition: opacity 0.32s`; a `hasEntered` field flipped ~30ms after open (edge-detected in `Tick()` on `IsVisible && !_wasVisible`, reset on close) adds `.entered` to `<root>`; `&.visible.entered { .avatar-frame {opacity:1; transition-delay:0.08s} ... .profile-footer {…0.54s} }` cascades hero→content top-to-bottom. Opacity-only (no transform) per the reliability rule. The modal-scale pop (`modalEnter` + `.profile-modal` scale transition) was LEFT as-is — harmless if it snaps, the modal still appears. **T3 rank-up:** no `CompetitiveManager` event exists, but the panel already polls `GetArenaPoints()` every `BuildHash`, and rank is `GetRankFromPoints(points)`. So a local `RankOrder[]` (Unranked→Mythic, mirroring the point thresholds) + `RankIndexOf()` lets `Tick()` compare the live rank index against `_lastRankIndex`; an INCREASE fires `TriggerRankUp()`. `_lastRankIndex` is SEEDED on first open (`if (_lastRankIndex < 0) … = RankIndexOf(current)`) so opening at an existing rank never false-fires — only a change observed WHILE the panel is open celebrates. A point LOSS that drops a tier is silently absorbed (index updated, no celebration). The celebration is a `pointer-events:none` full-bleed sibling of the modal inside `<root>`, gated `@if (rankUpActive)` + render-keyed `@key=rankUpVersion` so its mount-time `@keyframes` fire fresh each time (Day-7/Achievement burst lineage): rank-color flash → 2 expanding rings (`border-radius: 50%`, widely supported) → 12-mote radial burst at 30° (per-index `@keyframes` with precomputed `translate(dx,dy)` since CSS can't compute trig — multi-function transforms are FINE in mount-time keyframes, only class-toggle TRANSITIONS snap) → P5 stamp card slam with the reached rank's icon+name, themed via inline `GetRankColor()/GetRankShadow()/GetRankLucideIcon()`. Auto-dismiss after 2200ms via `DismissRankUp()`; whole overlay eases out with a `rankupCardSettle` tail keyframe. **Anti-gacha:** deterministic — you crossed a point threshold by winning, shown plainly, passes the Hades-not-HSR test. Added `hasEntered`, `rankUpActive`, `rankUpVersion` to BuildHash. **Out-of-scope flags:** (1) no `ui.profile.rank_up` localization key exists (Assets/ out of scope) — used a literal `RANK UP` string since a missing key renders the raw key; flag to product to add the key. (2) No dedicated rank-up sound cue — layered `PlayClick + PlaySuccess + PlayGoldReward` as the T3 shape; flag for product. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ProfilePanel.razor(.scss)` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-03:** **ArenaPanel raised to T3 rank-up + T4 win-streak — and the rank-up overlay it shipped with was DEAD CODE.** The `.scss` already had a full `.rank-up-overlay`/`.rank-up-content`/`.rank-up-text` block, `showRankUp`/`previousRank`/`DismissRankUp` state, and `HandleKeyboardInput` even called `DismissRankUp()` — but there was NO markup anywhere in the `.razor` rendering the overlay. It was styled + wired + never shown. Lesson: when a panel "already has" a celebration in SCSS, grep the RAZOR for the class before assuming it works — orphaned celebration CSS is a real pattern in this codebase. (1) **Missing `StateHasChanged()`:** `OnSetCompleteEvent` set `currentView = "result"` but — unlike every sibling handler (`OnBetweenGamesStart`, `OnNextGameStartEvent`, etc.) — did NOT call `StateHasChanged()`. The result view rendered anyway via later ticks, but a celebration overlay that must MOUNT on the result frame needs the explicit re-render; added it. (2) **Both celebrations live INSIDE the `else if (currentView == "result")` block** as siblings of the result content, anchored to `.result-screen` (`position: relative`, NOT a scroll container) — so colored glow + scale transforms are safe here (unlike the grid panels). Conditionally-rendered + render-keyed (`@key=@($"streak-{streakSetpieceVersion}")` — ONE interpolated expression per the adjacent-expression rule) so mount-time `@keyframes` replay each fire. (3) **T4 win-streak** fires on `won && streak ∈ StreakMilestones{3,5,10,15,20,25,30,40,50}`, read from `GetWinStreak()` (already incremented by `CompetitiveManager` before `OnSetComplete` fires). If a streak milestone fires, the rank-up overlay is SUPPRESSED that set (no double-stack) — rank still updates on the result card. Keyboard dismiss order streak→rankup→continue. (4) **NO Sass `@for`:** wanted 14 god-ray rays at 25.7deg increments; grepped the whole `Code/UI` tree and found ZERO Sass control flow (`@for`/`@each`/`@mixin`/`@if`) in any `.scss` — only Razor `@if`. Toolchain support is unverified, so wrote 14 explicit `.streak-ray-N { transform: rotate(Ndeg) }` rules instead. **Default for this codebase: enumerate explicitly, don't trust Sass loops.** (5) **Skew + keyframe transform conflict:** `.rank-up-text` has a static `transform: skewX(-8deg)` (Persona dialect) and an infinite `rankUpTextPulse` scale keyframe; a `scale()`-only keyframe OVERRIDES the static skew during play (text un-skews while pulsing). Fix: bake the skew into the keyframe (`skewX(-8deg) scale(1.05)`). Same trap anywhere a P5-skewed element gets an infinite transform loop. **Anti-gacha:** both are deterministic win outcomes; no sound cue exists (reuses PlayVictory/PlayClick) — flag for product. Source: `Code/UI/Panels/ArenaPanel.razor(.scss)`, `CompetitiveManager.cs:804,793` 2026-06-03.

- **2026-06-08:** **[PROMOTE] VERIFIED FROM SHIPPED BINARIES — s&box has NO standalone `perspective` property and NO `transform-style: preserve-3d`. Per-element `perspective()` is the ONLY 3D path; the "shared camera on a parent" technique is IMPOSSIBLE in s&box.** Inspected `Sandbox.Engine.dll` (CSS-property parser) and `Sandbox.System.dll` (transform-function parser) with `System.Reflection.Metadata` (metadata-only read — `Assembly.GetTypes()` FAILS here because the DLLs target .NET 10 / `System.Runtime 10.0.0.0` and a net8 host can't resolve deps; reflection-via-execution is NOT viable for these binaries — use raw metadata + UTF-16 string scan instead). Findings: (1) `Sandbox.UI.BaseStyles` is the FULL supported-CSS property surface (every CSS prop maps to a property here) — it has `PerspectiveOriginX/Y`, `Transform`, `TransformOriginX/Y` but NO `Perspective` member. (2) Engine's property-name token set is exactly `transform`, `transform-origin(-x/-y)`, `perspective-origin(-x/-y)` — there is NO `perspective` standalone token, so the declaration `perspective: 2200px` on a parent is unparseable. (3) `transform-style` and `preserve-3d`/`flat` appear in NEITHER DLL — no `TransformStyle` enum/property exists anywhere; conclusively unsupported. (4) `perspective`/`rotateY`/`rotate3d`/`matrix3d`/`translate3d`/`scale3d`/`skewX/Y` are transform-FUNCTION tokens in `Sandbox.System.dll`, valid only inside a `transform:` value. **Consequence for the main-menu angled UI:** the size-dependent dip mismatch between the wide featured card and the narrow bottom cards (wider element at the same `rotateY` recedes its right edge further — see 2026-06-08 inverse-width learning) is INHERENT to per-element `perspective()` and cannot be cured by a shared camera. The ONLY way to get a truly uniform dip across mixed-width stacked panels is a 2D `skewY` fallback (parallel edges, identical slant, zero depth) — you trade away the real recede for perfect consistency. `perspective-origin` exists as a property but, with no standalone `perspective` to re-origin, it only nudges the `perspective()` function's vanishing point — not a path to a shared plane. Method note: clean up the temp probe project after; do NOT leave a .csproj in /tmp referenced by later runs. Source: binary inspection of sbox 26.06.03 managed DLLs; updated `.claude/ui-knowledge/css-quirks.md` "3D transforms ARE supported" section 2026-06-08.

## Doctrine port #1 — OptionsPanel stroke purge + SNAP (2026-06-09)

- **2026-06-09:** **Stroke-purge migration recipe for sectioned panels: colored hairline borders → (a) per-section LEFT ACCENT BAR on the card + (b) SOLID OPAQUE gem fill on the section icon.** OptionsPanel's six category identities were carried by tone-colored 1.5px borders on the diamond icon frames. Under the vector rule those are outlines → deleted. The identity ports losslessly to two budget-legal signals: `border-left: 3px solid <tone>` on `.settings-section` (the permitted card accent bar) + a solid opaque tone fill on the gem (`#4D3C1C` gold, `#2D4152` sky, `#4D2834` rose, `#30483E` green, `#382F51` purple, `#4D353C` danger ≈ 25% tone mixed into `#131019`). **Opacity matters:** the gem overhangs the card edge onto the modal bg — an rgba() translucent fill shows the surface seam through the gem; pre-mixed solid hexes don't. Same trick anywhere a slab straddles two surface tones. Source: `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor.scss` doctrine rebuild 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **[DOCTRINE FEEDBACK] The line budget (ring / left accent bar / rarity strip) is component-scoped and says nothing about PANEL-LEVEL chrome — modal outer borders and top/bottom signature gradient lines fall in a gap.** OptionsPanel had a 1.5px purple modal border + 2px gradient top AND bottom lines (MenuPopup language). Resolution chosen: delete the modal border (overlay dim + dark elevation shadow separate it fine), delete the bottom line, keep ONE top strip but convert it from 5-stop gradient shimmer to a solid 2px `#7B4DDB` bar (crisp slab language). Proposal for guiding-star: add an explicit fourth budget item — "panel signature strip: one solid 2px accent bar on a modal's top edge, optional" — otherwise every modal port re-litigates this. Also: "Done"-tier modal confirm buttons should be gold-fill/dark-text per the Components Primary tier — OptionsPanel's was purple; switched to `#FFCE3A`/`#2A1605`. Source: OptionsPanel doctrine rebuild 2026-06-09, flag for user review.

- **2026-06-09:** **Borderless row separation inside a rounded card = transparent rows-container with `padding: 8px; gap: 4px` + each row a solid inset slab (`#131019`, radius 8) on the raised card (`#1C1830`).** Replaces hairline `border-bottom` row dividers: the card tone showing through the 4px gaps IS the separator. Bonus 1: solves the geometry problem where full-bleed solid rows would poke square corners past the card's 12px radius (card has `overflow: visible` for the gem overhang, so it can't clip them). Bonus 2: rows become discrete hover/focus targets — kb-cursor = solid violet fill `#2A2150` + 3px `#9b6cff` left bar with `padding-left` reduced 14→11 to compensate content-box. For a SELECTED card state (active language), reserve the ring slot at idle: `border: 2px solid rgba(0,0,0,0)` always present, animate border-color only — geometry never shifts when selection moves. Source: `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor.scss` `.section-rows`/`.setting-row`/`.lang-card` 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **MainMenu launcher hub is now fully stroke-purged — its complete line inventory is: the violet cursor/rings (#9b6cff), the Discord card's NEW `border-left: 4px solid rgba(88,101,242,0.85)` accent bar, the two `.lh-card-edge` accent bars, and the `.lh-feat-topedge` 2px specular strip.** Everything else lost its hairline: sidebar right edge (shadow + fill contrast carries it; width 419→420 since the 1px border left the 500px target), `.lh-item` reserved transparent border + `.has-new` gold edge (pip carries "new"), LIVE event pill gold ring (bg solidified to rgba(13,8,30,0.92)), featured card violet 1px, ghost CTA glass outline (→ solid slab rgba(38,30,72,0.92), idle text dimmed), `.lh-card` base border + all hover border-recolors, card divider border-tops (whitespace separates), IN DEV chips (→ recessed rgba(10,7,28,0.72) slabs darker than the card). **Two deliberate judgment calls to know about:** (1) `.lh-feat-topedge` is a LINE but was kept — it's lighting (specular "lit from above"), not an outline; the distinction is "does it trace the component's silhouette?" (2) The community card's `.lh-card-icn` went SOLID `#5865F2` (matching the sidebar's Discord disc) instead of outlined-tint — solid brand-color discs are the color-block answer for icon chips. Source: MainMenu.razor.scss stroke purge 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **Dead-SCSS purge record for MainMenu.razor.scss (5127 → 3968 lines, −1159): the entire pre-Concept-C menu zone was deleted** (menu-feature-portrait/aura-*/feature-*, menu-hero/logo-*/game-logo/splash-*, news-ticker/ticker-*, menu-button-column/menu-utility-stack/menu-entry*/entry-*, dev-credit*, menu-bottom-*/update-*/discord-link/version-text, event-toast/toast-*, .starter-panel, .panel-header, .lh-spacer/.lh-shot, .lh-card-avatars, entry-new-dot, beta-disclaimer-status-* and -btn-secondary*, @keyframes lh-featured-glow) — every selector verified 0-referenced in MainMenu.razor first. **Deliberately KEPT despite zero razor references — do NOT "rediscover" these as dead:** (1) ALL `.hiring-*` + `.now-hiring-pill` styles (user-protected; the popup markup is currently absent from the razor but the family is expected back), (2) the per-item `.lh-*-ring` elements (markup still renders them; CSS sets `display:none` as the documented fallback if the gliding `.lh-cursor` is ever reverted), (3) `@keyframes panelPopIn` (its original `.starter-panel` owner died but `.roadmap-panel-v2` still uses it — always grep keyframe usage before deleting with its owner). Source: MainMenu dead purge 2026-06-09.

## Menu features pass — detonation / deal-in / element tint / splash (2026-06-09)

- **2026-06-09:** **An element that NEEDS a CSS `transition: transform` for its normal states (Persona pop) gets a temporary state class that re-declares the transition WITHOUT transform whenever code drives its transform imperatively per-frame.** The PLAY detonation squash (scale 1→0.93→1.05→1 over 320ms via `Style.Transform`) was unbuildable under `.lh-play`'s `transition: transform 0.14s` — s&box applies CSS transitions to imperative Style changes too, lowpassing per-frame values to mush (same reason `.lh-cursor` carries no transform transition at all). But deleting the transition outright would kill the pop. Fix: `.lh-play.squash { transition: background-color 0.14s ease-out; }` toggled for exactly the imperative window (~320ms), then removed so the pop transition resumes; the imperative chain re-includes the full static pose (`perspective(1300px) translateX(9px) rotateY(13deg) scale(1.04·s)`) and ends with `Style.Transform = null` so the stylesheet value takes back over seamlessly. General rule: imperative-transform window ⇒ matching transition-suppression class on the same window. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` UpdatePlayDetonation + `.lh-play.squash`/`.lh-feat-cta.squash` 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **Refinement of the AchievementPanel `transition-delay` gotcha (2026-06-03): when the staggered rows are INTERACTIVE controls, pair the per-row delays with a stage-driven `settled` class that zeroes ALL delays once the entrance completes — `:hover`/`.selected` re-asserts alone leave the delay live on the DESELECT direction forever.** MainMenu nav cascade: `.lh-nav.entered > *:nth-child(N) { transition-delay: 0/0.06/0.12/0.18s }` staggers the rows, but the delay rides every property in each row's transition list (bg/color/transform), so un-hovering a row would lag 0.18s for the lifetime of the menu. The markup adds `settled` at entranceStage ≥ 4 (when the deal-in planes finish) and `.lh-nav.entered.settled > *:nth-child(N) { transition-delay: 0s }` (nth-child kept for specificity over the delay rules) kills every delay for good; `.lh-nav.entered > .selected { transition-delay: 0s }` (AFTER the nth-child block — equal specificity, source order) covers selection during the ~700ms entrance window itself. Pattern: stagger delays are ENTRANCE-scoped state, so tie their removal to the entrance-complete stage flag, not to per-state re-asserts. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-nav` + `MainMenu.razor` entranceStage 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **Entrance "deal-in along the 3D plane" recipe for transform-holding panels: drive `perspective → translateX → rotateY` imperatively per-frame from a displaced pose (x 70, deg 15) toward the CSS steady-state (x 0, deg 10) with exponential lerp 0.16/frame (~450ms), keep the FADE as the normal class-toggle opacity transition in parallel, then null the inline transform on settle.** Used for `.lh-featured` + `.lh-cards` (cards start 120ms later). Why not class-toggle transform transitions: they snap (26.06.03); why not @keyframes: the elements persist across menu re-entries so keyframes fire once. Critical companions: (1) the steady-state CSS rule must have the SAME chain shape so convergence is exact; (2) `transform-origin` stays authored in CSS (inline transform respects it — the cards row keeps its `left -374px` shared vanishing point); (3) anything reading static angles (CursorRot) must stay gated until the planes settle — the cursor goes live at the stage flip that UpdateDealIn fires on settle, never on a timer. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` UpdateDealIn/ApplyDealTransform 2026-06-09.

- **2026-06-09:** **Never bake `perspective(...)` (or any per-item 3D chain) into `@keyframes` — the PLAY detonation flash did and NEVER rendered in-engine (user: "you just see a little yellow line").** The one-shot `.lh-play-flash` keyframes carried `perspective(1300px) translateX(9px) rotateY(13deg) scale(...)` so the bloom would expand "on the tilted plane"; the user saw no flash at all, consistent with the keyframe transform failing to parse and the animation being dropped. Rebuilt FLAT (`opacity 0.95→0, scale 1→1.18`) — a flat bloom over a tilted button is indistinguishable at 260ms. **Companion rule for imperative transform windows:** when a per-frame `Style.Transform` envelope ends, assign the EXPLICIT stylesheet-matching rest pose and only null the inline transform at final cleanup (well after the transition-suppressing class has cleared) — `Style.Transform = null` in the same window as a class swap was the other suspect for invisible squash. Also: amplitude floors — 0.93→1.05 squash-overshoot on a ~460px button doesn't read; 0.90→1.07 is the minimum visible pop. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` UpdatePlayDetonation/DetonationRestPose + `.razor.scss` `.lh-play-flash`, detonation rebuild 2026-06-09. ⚠ Flat-flash fix is the prime-suspect diagnosis — verify in-engine; if the flash STILL doesn't show, suspect the conditional-mount @key path next.

- **2026-06-09:** **Slide-in side panels: animate the `right` PROPERTY (class-toggle transition), never `transform: translateX` — and mount content one frame CLOSED before flipping `.open`.** The menu's Roadmap/Options/PatchNotes slide-in shell (`.lh-detail`, 720px right-edge panel) slides via `right: -790px → 0` with asymmetric timing (in 0.26s decelerating, out 0.18s ease-in). Transform was rejected because a transform on the shell poisons flex-grow width resolution in EVERY descendant (css-quirks: TeamPickerPopup 6-hour bug) — fatal here since the shell hosts the docked OptionsPanel whose rows are `flex: 1 1 0` chains. Position-property transitions are proven smooth in s&box (the living cursor glides via left/top/width/height). **Mount-then-open:** freshly-mounted content rendered WITH `.open` already present snaps (no state change = no transition); `OpenDetail` sets `detailView`, StateHasChanged, `await Task.Delay(30)`, THEN `detailOpen = true` — same trick as PlayEntranceSequence's stage 0 frame. Keep content mounted through close so the panel never empties mid-slide-out; stagger child reveal with transition-delay rules scoped under `.open` only (base state delay-free → close fades everything together). Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` OpenDetail/CloseDetail + `.razor.scss` `.lh-detail`, slide-in conversion 2026-06-09. ⚠ `right`-property transition smoothness on a 720px panel not yet verified in-engine — fallback if it steps: drive it imperatively per-frame like the cursor.

- **2026-06-09:** **Dual-context component pattern (centered modal ↔ docked sheet): a `Docked` bool property + one `.docked` SCSS override block + host-side `AnyVisible` polling — no fork, no events.** OptionsPanel now serves GameHUD as a centered modal AND the main-menu slide-in shell as a fill-parent sheet from ONE component: markup gates only the overlay (`@if (!Docked)`), `.options-container.docked` strips chrome (fills parent, `transform: none` — 26.06.03 override semantics — keeps transforms out of the shell's flex ancestry) and adds docked-only roominess + `.section-sub` detail lines + staggered section reveal. The host learns the panel closed itself (Done/X/Q) by polling `OptionsPanel.AnyVisible` in OnUpdate and retracting the shell silently (Hide() already played the popdown — don't double-sound). Show()'s existing PickActiveInstance ancestry-visibility routing makes the docked instance win on the menu and GameHUD's win in-game with zero changes. Reusable for any panel that needs both a popup life and an embedded life. Source: `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor`(+.scss) Docked mode + `MainMenu.razor` options poll, 2026-06-09.

## Scene swap rebuild — deal-out/deal-in shell (2026-06-09)

- **2026-06-09:** **"Clicked control dies last" can't be a simple delayed child fade — parent opacity multiplies.** If the departing container (sidebar slab) fades to 0 in 160ms, a child holding opacity 1 still vanishes with it (child renders at parent×child). Shipped pattern: the SLAB fades slightly long (0.16s), its non-held children fade FAST (0.10s), and only the held control gets the 0.06s delay + 0.10s fade — the relative contrast reads as "dies last" inside the parent's envelope without restructuring the tree. In-plane controls (featured CTAs, card pills) can't get the hold at all — they ride their plane's single opacity; their acknowledgment is the pre-flight SNAP (pop/edge-flare) instead. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-sidebar.departing` block, scene-swap task 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **A component that manages its own `.visible` opacity pops fully-formed when a `display:none` ancestor flips to flex — gate it FROM THE HOST at higher specificity.** The docked OptionsPanel is Show()n at click time (sound semantics), so by the scene's swap frame it's already `.visible{opacity:1}` inside the hidden wrapper; the display flip would skip the mounted-closed frame entirely. Fix: host rule `.lh-scene .options-container.docked { opacity: 0 }` (0-3-0 beats the component's `.visible` 0-2-0) with the lift scoped to `.lh-scene.scene-options.arrived ...` — the host owns the arrival, the component keeps owning its internal section stagger. Generalizable to the GameHUD scene-swap rollout for any self-visibility-managing child. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-scene` options block, scene-swap task 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **Lazy-initialized tween objects + pass-by-field-argument is a silent no-op trap.** `StartTween(_twFeatured, ...)` evaluates `_twFeatured` BEFORE the callee's `EnsureTweens()` runs — if the field is still null the callee's null-guard eats the call and the plane never moves, with zero errors. Call `EnsureTweens()` at the top of every public entry point (RunSwap, PlayEntranceSequence, UpdatePlaneTweens) before any `_tw*` field is read. Source: `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` `RunSwap()`, scene-swap task 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **Imperative `Style.Transform` writes land SAME-FRAME; class-driven `display:none` lands NEXT rendered frame — never null an outgoing plane's inline transform at a swap frame.** v1 of the scene swap called `ResetPlanesForSwap` at the swap frame, nulling the departing menu planes' transforms; the stylesheet's steady pose resumed instantly while the wrapper's `.scene-off` (display:none) needed one more render — the menu visibly FLASHED back to its rest pose before disappearing. Rule: outgoing planes keep their flown-out inline pose (harmless while hidden) and get re-seeded/nulled lazily at the next arrival (`StartTween` seeds when inactive) or fresh entrance. Only the INCOMING plane gets pre-posed at the swap frame. Source: departure-flash fix, `Code/UI/MainMenu.razor` `ResetPlanesForSwap`, 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **Cross-scene cursor retargets must POLL the destination rect, not fire on a timer.** Setting `selectedIndex = 20` at swap+1 frame read the close button's Box.Rect before layout (wrapper flipped to flex that same frame) — the living ring stranded over empty space. Shipped pattern: arm a `_pendingSceneFocus` flag, poll each OnUpdate until the target's rect reads real (`Width >= 1 && Top >= 1`) for 2 consecutive frames, THEN retarget. Companion rule: null the scene-content `@ref` fields whenever the mounted `@if` branch swaps — a deleted panel's frozen Box.Rect satisfies the validity check and re-strands the ring. Source: stranded-ring fix, `MainMenu.razor` `UpdatePendingSceneFocus`, 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09: [PROMOTE]** **s&box drops the whitespace between a bare text node and an adjacent nested element — `Foo <span>Bar</span>` renders "FooBar".** Seen on both scene headers ("What We'reBuilding", "WhisperingHollow"). Fix: make multi-color titles GAP-FLEX rows of sibling word-spans (`.lhd-title { display: flex; gap: 9px }` + `.lhd-title-white`/`.lhd-title-gold`) — deterministic word spacing, no reliance on text-node whitespace. Source: roadmap/patch-notes header fix, `MainMenu.razor` + `.scss` `.lhd-title`, 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **`lucide:scale` renders an EMPTY iconify gem in-engine — treat it as unavailable.** The roadmap "Balance & Economy" diamond rendered blank while five sibling icons rendered fine; the patch-notes Balance section head used the same name. Replaced with `lucide:coins` / `lucide:sliders-horizontal`. NOTE: `Code/UI/Panels/HelpPanel.razor:1750,3128` still uses `lucide:scale` — likely blank there too; flag on next Help pass. Source: user screenshot, roadmap icon fix 2026-06-09.
- **2026-06-09:** **Wider angled backdrop slabs need a SHALLOWER rest angle — corner coverage shrinks with width.** At the full-canvas footprint (1700px frame / 2070px slab) the 10° menu-dialect rest pose foreshortened the slab's far edge so much its projected top/bottom pulled inside the frame's top-right corner. Scene slabs now rest at 7° (arrive 12→7, depart 7→14); menu planes keep 10°. Geometry: far-edge vertical coverage ≈ slabHalfHeight × persp/(persp + width·sin θ) — check it covers the frame half-height before picking θ. Source: full-width scene redesign, `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-scene-bg`, 2026-06-09.
