- **2026-06-09:** **Sharing one section markup source between two structural layouts = RenderFragment properties, composed differently per mode.** OptionsPanel's six sections moved into `private RenderFragment SectionFoo => __builder => {...}` fragments; docked mode composes them into two `.opt-col` scroll columns, the in-game modal keeps the single column — zero duplication, and the `_toggleRowCounter` keyboard indices stay aligned because the toggle sections render first in both modes (all in the docked LEFT column, so W/S walks one coherent column). Scroll quirk honored: each `.opt-col` is its own `overflow-y: scroll` with sections as DIRECT children. Source: docked Options two-column pass, `Code/UI/Components/OptionsPanel.razor`(+.scss), 2026-06-09.

## Scene swap hitch root-cause + LighthouseScene split — 2026-06-10

- **2026-06-10: [PROMOTE]** **Flipping `display: none↔flex` on a large always-mounted subtree costs a FULL first-layout + style match in ONE frame — on the menu's scene swap that was a 90–230ms single-frame stall that READ as two separate bugs.** swapdbg per-frame logs proved zero ticks for ~230ms after the swap frame (wall-clock gap between consecutive log lines; grows with panel count). During the stall the screen freezes on the last presented frame (user saw "the menu ghost flashes"), time-based tweens silently consume their opening segment (first rendered frame of the `.lh-scene-bg` deal-in was already at x=68 of 100→0 — user saw "the backdrop won't load in until the menu disappears"), and short `Task.Delay` choreography (the mounted-closed frame) expires entirely inside the stall so transition staggers fire off a never-rendered base state. **Fix pattern: park offscreen instead (`left: -10000px` ↔ `0`, keep `display: flex`)** — layout stays warm from boot, base opacity-0 states are committed long before any swap, the class flip costs one cheap frame. Applied to BOTH `.lh-scene` and `.menu-content` (its hide is now opacity 0 + left -10000 only — inline, imperative, and per-frame battery all agree; display is never touched). Companion: any Box.Rect validity poll must add `Left > -1000` — a parked panel's rect is "real" but offscreen. Source: sbox-dev.log swapdbg census 2026-06-10 sessions (SWAP FRAME → next tick gaps: 86ms v=1 … 232ms v=9) + `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-scene`, `MainMenu.razor` swap-frame hide.
- **2026-06-10: [PROMOTE]** **`PanelComponent` NEVER fires `OnAfterTreeRender` — the hook only works on `Panel`-derived razor components. A mitigation placed there is a silent no-op that compiles fine.** MainMenu's "ghost sweep" (`OnAfterTreeRender` override that `Delete(true)`d duplicate `.menu-content` children) logged ZERO times across every session in sbox-dev.log despite SWAP_DEBUG being on. Every working override in the codebase (OptionsPanel, ChatPanel, GiftInboxPopup, ~10 more) is on `@inherits Panel` components. Worse, had it fired it would have been harmful: the markup renders `.menu-content` unconditionally, so the sweep would have deleted the LIVE menu panel on every re-render while a scene was up. Deleted. Rule: on a PanelComponent, post-render work goes in `OnUpdate`/`Tick` guarded by frame checks — and always verify a new hook actually logs before trusting it as a mitigation. Source: log grep `SWEPT` = 0 hits vs in-file SWAP_DEBUG logging, `MainMenu.razor` sweep removal 2026-06-10.
- **2026-06-10:** **The render differ duplicating root children (rootChildren 6→14 with TWO `.menu-content` copies, ~0.5s cleanup) happens at HOTLOAD tree rebuilds, NOT at normal state-change re-renders — don't architect runtime defenses against it.** The census burst that drove the ghost-sweep mitigation sits exactly at an assembly hotload boundary (census line FORMAT changes mid-burst = new code logging; 2s frame gap; both copies invisible op=0). Per-swap census across 10 consecutive real swaps showed menuCount=1 every time. The real shipping defense is structural: big sibling regions that swap visibility live in their OWN child component (`Components/LighthouseScene.razor` — own BuildHash/differ; host drives via value params + METHOD-GROUP callbacks like `CloseRequested=@CloseScene`, which are hotload-safe and reassigned every parent render; SCSS needs no move when the component mounts inside the host's root — selectors cascade). This also cut what a scene class flip re-renders from ~3,300 lines to ~250. Source: sbox-dev.log 11:28:07 burst analysis vs 11:40 per-swap census; LighthouseScene extraction 2026-06-10.

## Scene Identity v3 implementation — 2026-06-10

- **2026-06-10:** **After the component split, `@ref` fields INSIDE an `@if` branch of the child component went stale (ring stranded on the dead sidebar control) while a `@ref` OUTSIDE the branch (`SceneBg`) provably kept working — swapdbg `tween p2` lines ran on every swap.** That asymmetry localizes the failure to the @if-branch @ref lifecycle, not to `_sceneComp` binding or the rect-validity guard. Shipped defense: host code NEVER reads the raw ref fields — it calls `LighthouseScene.FindFocusable(idx)` / `ResolveScrollBody()`, which trust the @ref only when it reads live on-screen geometry (`Left > -1000 && Width >= 1` while shown) and otherwise WALK THE TREE by class (`FindByClass` over `Panel.Children`, recursive) and re-cache into the field. Immune to differ recreate, remount, and hotload. Generalize: any cross-component focus/scroll handle should ship with a class-based self-heal path; @refs are the fast path, never the only path. Source: stranded-ring post-split fix, `LighthouseScene.razor` FindFocusable + `MainMenu.razor` FocusEl/UpdatePendingSceneFocus, 2026-06-10. ⚠ Root MECHANISM of the @ref staleness still unconfirmed — the SWAP_DEBUG focus-poll logging now prints which condition fails if it ever re-strands.
- **2026-06-10:** **The post-split "every iconify renders EMPTY in LighthouseScene" bug resisted static diagnosis — facts gathered: zero `[Iconify]` warnings in sbox-dev.log (so `LoadIcon` either never ran or silently cache-hit), the nested docked OptionsPanel's own `OnAfterTreeRender` DOES fire under the scene (its instance registration works), and EmptyState/HelpHero render icons fine with the IDENTICAL minimal directive block (`@namespace` + Sandbox/Sandbox.UI usings + `@inherits Panel`) — so "missing usings" is NOT proven as the mechanism.** Shipped: (1) `@using Iconify;` added anyway (BattleView precedent, zero cost); (2) a one-shot `ICON_DEBUG` census in `LighthouseScene.Tick()` that logs every descendant with ElementName "iconify": `type=Panel` ⇒ tag never resolved to `Iconify.IconifyPanel` (fix = qualified component tags); `type=IconifyPanel kids=0` ⇒ load path never fired (fix in the library's OnAfterTreeRender trigger); `kids=1` ⇒ icon present but invisible (styling). Read the census from the next session's log before theorizing further. The library lives at `Libraries/mint.iconify/Code/IconifyPanel.cs` ([Alias("iconify")], loads via OnAfterTreeRender → IconCache HTTP/disk). Source: gem-bug diagnosis session 2026-06-10.
- **2026-06-10:** **Per-scene angled-backdrop leans (Suto wayfinding: +7° roadmap / −7° patch notes / +5° options) need THREE things in lockstep or the null-on-settle handback flashes: (1) a per-scene steady CSS pose (`.lh-scene.scene-* .lh-scene-bg { transform: ... rotateY(Ndeg) }` — the scene class is present from the swap frame), (2) `SceneRestDeg(SceneId)` in code feeding StartTween/ResetPlanesForSwap, (3) SIGN-CONSISTENT over-rotation offsets (`rest + 5°·sign(rest)` arrival start, `rest + 7°·sign(rest)` departure target) so a mirrored scene exits along its own entrance vector instead of crossing 0°.** With `transform-origin: left center`, a negative rotateY swings the slab's right edge TOWARD the camera (projection grows) — coverage is safe; only positive leans foreshorten the far edge (the 2026-06-09 corner-coverage check applies to positive angles). Source: Scene Identity v3 lean implementation, `MainMenu.razor` SceneRestDeg + `.razor.scss` `.lh-scene-bg` scene overrides, 2026-06-10.

## Scene Identity v3.1 deltas — 2026-06-11

- **2026-06-11:** **When a class-toggle ENTRANCE transform and a HOVER transform share an element, the entrance's settled state (`&.arrived ... { transform: translateX(0px) }`, specificity (0,3,0)) silently stomps the per-class hover transform (`.lhp:hover { transform: scale(...) }`, (0,2,0)) — hover bg/child effects keep working, only the transform dies, which reads as "the scale randomly stopped".** Shipped pattern: declare all hover/focus transforms at BOOSTED specificity gated on `.settled` (`&.settled .lhd-body > .lhp:hover { transform: translateX(0px) scale(1.02) }`, (0,4-5,0)), and have `.settled` also swap the element's transform transition from the slow arrival curve (0.30s decel) to the 0.12s SNAP duration. Works because the host's Arrived/Settled/Departing flags are mutually exclusive in code. Cost: hover transforms are inert during the ~300ms arrived-but-not-settled window (child-property feedback still fires). Source: v3.1 delta 6, `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lh-scene` block, 2026-06-11.
- **2026-06-11:** **The "scroll containers don't clip descendant transforms" quirk BLOCKS slide-in entrances for scroll-column children entirely — it's not just a hover rule.** A `translateX` on `.lhd-scroll > *` children would render below-fold sections OUTSIDE the scroll viewport (they escape the clip while transformed), and `translateX(0px)` is still "a transform" so even the settled state stays escape-prone. Fallback hierarchy used in v3.1: (1) scroll children = opacity-only, (2) move the slide onto a transform-safe SIBLING (the fixed-width `.lhd-rail`) so the scene still visibly travels with the backdrop, (3) make that sibling transform-safe by purging flex-grow descendants (`.lhd-toc-name` `flex: 1 1 0` → `0 0 auto` + `margin-left: auto` on the count — same recipe as `.lhd-header-right`). Source: v3.1 delta 6 fallback, `MainMenu.razor.scss` `.lhd-rail`/`.lhd-toc-*`, 2026-06-11.
- **2026-06-11:** User taste (v3.1): scene chrome should carry its own key vocabulary — when the footer key-hint rail was cut, the accepted replacement was putting the A/D caps ON the prev/next arrow buttons themselves (like Q on close), NOT recreating any hint bar. Also: identity accents read better as under-title rules that flare on hover than as left edge bars (poster `.lhp-rule` 72→128px replaces the 4→10px edge flare). Source: v3.1 user deltas 3/4/5, 2026-06-11.

## Phone launcher prototype + NavManager wiring — 2026-07-01

- **2026-07-01:** **`Input.Pressed` same-frame double-fire crosses the MODAL boundary, not just handler order inside one component — any key that both OPENS and CLOSES/ROUTES through a modal needs a shared latch.** The Slot8 press that opens PhoneLauncher (GameHUD handler) is still edge-true when the phone's own `Tick()` runs later that same frame — without a guard the phone instantly closes itself (and symmetrically, a direct-route key the phone consumes gets re-fired by GameHUD once the modal gate releases mid-frame). The house popups dodged this by never sharing keys (Slot7 opens QuestPanel, Q closes it); the phone's true-toggle Slot8 + direct-route keys made it unavoidable. Shipped fix: `NavManager.RoutedThisFrame` (`Time.Now - _lastRouteAt < 0.05s`, stamped by every successful GoTo/OpenOverlay) checked at the top of BOTH GameHUD.HandleKeyboardInput and PhoneLauncher.HandleKeyboard. This is the PanelHandledBackKey lesson generalized through the router. Source: PhoneLauncher build 2026-07-01, `Code/UI/NavManager.cs`.
- **2026-07-01:** **Static-registry hotload hardening pattern for router/host delegates (NavManager):** (1) method groups only at RegisterHost call sites, (2) the host RE-REGISTERS every frame from its OnUpdate (two-field write, free) so a hotload-replaced instance re-owns the router within a frame, (3) dispatch wrapped in a try/catch that CLEARS the host on throw (a stale delegate self-heals next frame instead of spamming). Same family as the PlaneTween int-ID law but for cross-component delegates. Also: a UI singleton razor Panel can re-assert `Instance = this` in `Tick()` instead of relying on OnAfterTreeRender — hotload-proof by construction. Source: NavManager hotload-safety pass 2026-07-01.
- **2026-07-01:** **The liquid-expansion recipe (the locked launcher signature) as shipped:** solid 100×100 `border-radius: 50%` div, per-frame `PanelTransform.AddScale` + `Style.Opacity` (both re-asserted EVERY frame — rebuild-proof), timings Expand 240ms ease-out-cubic → route at u=0.5 (~87% eased coverage — the destination's mount-stall lands while the screen is a solid accent flood) → Fade 180ms at full cover; 420ms total. Cover scale = farthest-corner distance from the tile center / 50, ×1.08. Bloom origin: tile panels found by fresh `HasClass("pl-app")` traversal (loop `@ref`s don't exist; traversal is hotload-safe). Circle must sit ABOVE the destinations it reveals (phone root z-index 9500 — QuestPanel is 1000, InventoryPanel 9000). Juice-tier call: liquid for full-screen destinations only; widget apps (chat/radio/effects/notifs) just close-and-toggle. Source: `Code/UI/Components/PhoneLauncher.razor` 2026-07-01.

## Phone launcher v2 — app icons + accent swipe (2026-07-01)

- **2026-07-01: [PROMOTE]** **All-edge absolute stretching (`top/left/right/bottom: 0`) resolves to ZERO SIZE on an EMPTY element — full-viewport backdrops/shields must use `top: 0; left: 0; width: 100%; height: 100%` instead.** The phone launcher's `.pl-backdrop` dim never rendered at all in-engine with the four-offset form (user: "backdrop dim is dead"); the same element with width/height 100% works. Same family as the "`width: auto` doesn't shrink-wrap absolutes" quirk — s&box's absolute-position solver doesn't derive size from opposing offsets on childless elements. Elements WITH content may get away with it (content props the box open); empty overlay divs never do. Source: PhoneLauncher v2 fix from live user feedback, `PhoneLauncher.razor.scss` `.pl-backdrop`, 2026-07-01.
- **2026-07-01:** **The liquid-expansion circle is RETIRED as the launcher signature — replaced by THE ACCENT SWIPE after live feedback ("doesn't expand all the way" + "jarring — maybe a side swipe?").** Two lessons: (1) the under-cover was a screen-px/style-px mismatch — cover geometry derived from `Box.Rect` alone assumes scale 1; convert with `ScaleFromScreen` (verified present in Sandbox.Engine.dll 26.06.03 as `get_ScaleFromScreen`) AND oversize generously (+30%) since transition slabs are solid fills where overshoot is free. (2) A radial bloom is perceptually louder than a directional wipe at equal duration — for ROUTINE navigation (Tier 2), the angled-slab sweep (the menu→game Persona-wipe vocabulary at phone speed: skewX(-12°) slab, right→left, 220ms ease-out in / 30ms covered hold / 200ms ease-in out = 450ms) reads calmer and still hides the destination's mount stall under full cover. Keep the circle recipe only as a reference for future Tier 3/4 radial moments. Source: PhoneLauncher v2, `Code/UI/Components/PhoneLauncher.razor` StartSwipe/StepFx, 2026-07-01.
- **2026-07-01:** User taste (launcher grid): "isn't it supposed to look like apps?" — the phone metaphor must be LITERAL: solid accent-filled rounded squares (color IS the icon), label OUTSIDE below, badge overlapping the icon corner. Dark-slab tiles with a small gem read as "menu rows", not apps. Corollaries: the launcher grid is the ONE sanctioned full-rainbow surface (P4's one-accent-per-view yields to launcher identity there); BbDiamond stays out of nav grids (diamond = action shape only); accent-on-accent badges need a dark punch-out border to separate from the icon; and "brighten on focus" over a solid accent fill = C# inline color swap toward white, NOT a white overlay child (absolute full-cover overlays paint behind the parent fill — existing quirk). Source: PhoneLauncher v2 user feedback pass, 2026-07-01.

## Phone launcher v3 — PawPad: living ring + alignment doctrine (2026-07-01)

- **2026-07-01: [PENDING IN-ENGINE VERIFY]** **Auto-width flex children placed on the CROSS axis (`align-items: center`) or right-anchored by `justify-content: space-between` can land as if their width were ZERO — content then paints rightward from the wrong anchor.** Screenshot evidence from the PawPad v2 live test (grid under a `perspective+rotateY(6deg)` ancestor): label+keycap rows started at the icon's CENTER and ran right past it; the auto-width PROTOTYPE stamp / tamer-LV span at the right end of space-between rows ran off the body edge; the whole grid read right-shifted. Fixed-width children (88px iconwrap, 168px tiles) placed fine — only auto-width boxes drifted. Doctrine applied in `PhoneLauncher.razor.scss`: every centered/right-anchored box gets an EXPLICIT width; centering happens via `justify-content` INSIDE a fixed box or a hard margin (`margin-left: 40px` for the icon), never via cross-axis auto placement. Likely same family as the transformed-ancestor flex-grow poisoning (CLAUDE.md TeamPickerPopup quirk). If the next live test confirms the fix, upgrade this to [PROMOTE]. Source: PawPad v3 alignment pass from screenshot-verified user feedback, 2026-07-01.
- **2026-07-01:** **Living-cursor rings inside a 3D-leaned subtree need NO perspective compensation — put the ring in the same transformed container as its targets.** MainMenu's root-level cursor must manually project controls inside tilted panels (rotateY math, pivot sandwiches — ~90 lines). The PawPad's ring is a child of `.pl-grid` (inside the leaned `.pl-device`), so both the ring and the icons share the transform and flat `Box.Rect` deltas (`(icon.Rect − grid.Rect) × ScaleFromScreen`) position it exactly. Recipe: bordered div (4px #9b6cff, no bg), left/top/width/height via markup style + CSS transitions (0.27s `cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)`, `.snap` class during 2-frame warmup), ink-lean wave via per-frame imperative `Style.Transform` (menu's multi-sine × 0.6 amplitude — full-size degrees read as flailing on an ~112px ring), host container `position: relative` + NO background (absolute children paint behind a parent's own bg fill). Focused elements found by fresh `HasClass` traversal from a container `@ref` (loop `@ref`s don't exist). Source: `Code/UI/Components/PhoneLauncher.razor` UpdateRing/ApplyRingWave, 2026-07-01.
- **2026-07-01:** Player-facing branding canonized: the phone is the **PawPad**, running **TamerLink OS** (header kicker "TAMERLINK OS", title "PawPad"). Code identity stays `PhoneLauncher` — do not rename files/classes. Source: coordinator directive relaying user canonization, 2026-07-01.
